  \item \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}rho\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CLW\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{int\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}(P;\allowbreak{}B)} --- \texttt{clw\_\allowbreak{}integer\_\allowbreak{}budget\_\allowbreak{}arm\_\allowbreak{}normalized\_\allowbreak{}design\_\allowbreak{}value\_\allowbreak{}ratio}; \([1,\infty]\); \texttt{diagnostic}
  \par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Definition.} \texttt{The best-\allowbreak{}tie arm-\allowbreak{}normalized profiled bridge-\allowbreak{}value ratio of the exact integer-\allowbreak{}budget CLW selector.}
  \item \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}rho\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CLW\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{IKM,\allowbreak{}int\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}(P;\allowbreak{}B)} --- \texttt{clw\_\allowbreak{}integer\_\allowbreak{}budget\_\allowbreak{}ikmw\_\allowbreak{}design\_\allowbreak{}value\_\allowbreak{}ratio}; \([1,\infty]\); \texttt{diagnostic}
  \par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Definition.} \texttt{The best-\allowbreak{}tie IKM-\allowbreak{}normalized profiled bridge-\allowbreak{}value ratio of the exact integer-\allowbreak{}budget CLW selector.}
  \item \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}epsilon\_\allowbreak{}m} --- \texttt{triangular\_\allowbreak{}weak\_\allowbreak{}proxy\_\allowbreak{}signal}; \((0,10^{-3})\); \texttt{perturbation\_\allowbreak{}sequence}
  \par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Definition.} A deterministic early-proxy signal sequence satisfying \(\epsilon_m\downarrow0\) along the empirical CLW witness path.
  \item \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat r\_\allowbreak{}\{d,\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{lin\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}} --- \texttt{pilot\_\allowbreak{}affine\_\allowbreak{}linear\_\allowbreak{}residual\_\allowbreak{}variance}; \([0,\infty)\); \texttt{estimator}
  \par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Definition.} The minimized observational-pilot affine OLS residual quadratic for the nonredundant regressor \(\xi_d\), totalized at zero when the observational pilot is empty.
  \item \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}Psi\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CLW\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}d,\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}(B)} --- \texttt{pilot\_\allowbreak{}clw\_\allowbreak{}continuous\_\allowbreak{}criterion}; \([0,\infty)\); \texttt{benchmark\_\allowbreak{}objective}
  \par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Definition.} The empirical continuous criterion \(C_d\widehat r_{d,m}^{\mathrm{lin}}/B\).
  \item \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}Psi\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CLW\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}d,\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{int\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}(B)} --- \texttt{pilot\_\allowbreak{}clw\_\allowbreak{}integer\_\allowbreak{}criterion}; \([0,\infty]\); \texttt{benchmark\_\allowbreak{}objective}
  \par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Definition.} The empirical exact-integer criterion \(\widehat r_{d,m}^{\mathrm{lin}}/\lfloor B/C_d\rfloor\) for positive count and \(+\infty\) otherwise.
  \item \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat d\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CLW\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}(B)} --- \texttt{pilot\_\allowbreak{}clw\_\allowbreak{}continuous\_\allowbreak{}selector}; \(\mathcal D\); \texttt{benchmark\_\allowbreak{}decision}
  \par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Definition.} \texttt{The deterministically tie-\allowbreak{}broken minimizer of the empirical continuous CLW criterion.}
  \item \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat d\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CLW\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{int\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}(B)} --- \texttt{pilot\_\allowbreak{}clw\_\allowbreak{}integer\_\allowbreak{}selector}; \(\mathcal D\); \texttt{benchmark\_\allowbreak{}decision}
  \par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Definition.} \texttt{The deterministically tie-\allowbreak{}broken minimizer of the empirical exact-\allowbreak{}integer CLW criterion.}
  \item \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}rho\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CLW\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{int\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}(P;\allowbreak{}B)} --- \texttt{empirical\_\allowbreak{}clw\_\allowbreak{}integer\_\allowbreak{}achieved\_\allowbreak{}arm\_\allowbreak{}normalized\_\allowbreak{}ratio}; \([1,\infty]\); \texttt{diagnostic}
  \par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Definition.} \texttt{The arm-\allowbreak{}normalized profiled bridge-\allowbreak{}value ratio achieved by the empirical exact-\allowbreak{}integer CLW selector.}
  \item \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}rho\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CLW\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{IKM,\allowbreak{}int\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}(P;\allowbreak{}B)} --- \texttt{empirical\_\allowbreak{}clw\_\allowbreak{}integer\_\allowbreak{}achieved\_\allowbreak{}ikmw\_\allowbreak{}ratio}; \([1,\infty]\); \texttt{diagnostic}
  \par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Definition.} \texttt{The IKM-\allowbreak{}normalized profiled bridge-\allowbreak{}value ratio achieved by the empirical exact-\allowbreak{}integer CLW selector.}
\end{itemize}

\section{Assumptions}

\begin{assumption}[ass:longterm-consistency]\label{ass:longterm-consistency}
\(Y=Y(A)\) almost surely.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Long-term-outcome consistency, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:shortterm-consistency]\label{ass:shortterm-consistency}
\(Z=Z(A)\) almost surely.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Short-term-outcome consistency, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:observational-latent-exchangeability]\label{ass:observational-latent-exchangeability}
\((Y(a),Z(a))\perp A\mid U,X,G=O\) for each \(a\in\{0,1\}\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Observational latent exchangeability, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:observational-overlap]\label{ass:observational-overlap}
\(0<P(A=1\mid U,X,G=O)<1\) almost surely.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Latent observational overlap, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:experimental-randomization]\label{ass:experimental-randomization}
\((Y(a),Z(a),U,X)\perp A\mid G=E\) for each \(a\in\{0,1\}\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Complete experimental randomization, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:experimental-overlap]\label{ass:experimental-overlap}
\(0<P(A=1\mid G=E)<1\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Experimental treatment overlap, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:external-validity]\label{ass:external-validity}
For every \(d\in\mathcal D\) and \(a\in\{0,1\}\),
\[
  (S_{1d}(a),S_{2d}(a),S_{3d}(a),U,X)\perp G.
\]
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Full short-term external validity across samples, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:source-support]\label{ass:source-support}
\(p_P(U,X\mid A=a,G=E)/p_P(U,X\mid A=a,G=O)<\infty\) almost surely for each \(a\in\{0,1\}\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Cross-sample support overlap, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:sequential-outcomes]\label{ass:sequential-outcomes}
\((Y(a),S_{3d}(a))\perp S_{1d}(a)\mid S_{2d}(a),U,X,G=O\) for every \(d\in\mathcal D\) and \(a\in\{0,1\}\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Designwise sequential-outcome restriction, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:late-proxy-completeness]\label{ass:late-proxy-completeness}
For every \(d\in\mathcal D\), \(a\in\{0,1\}\), and square-integrable \(v\), \(\mathbb E_P[v(U)\mid S_{3d},S_{2d},A=a,X,G=O]=0\) almost surely implies \(v(U)=0\) almost surely.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Late-proxy completeness, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:early-proxy-completeness]\label{ass:early-proxy-completeness}
For every \(d\in\mathcal D\), \(a\in\{0,1\}\), and square-integrable \(v\), \(\mathbb E_P[v(U)\mid S_{1d},S_{2d},A=a,X,G=O]=0\) almost surely implies \(v(U)=0\) almost surely.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Early-proxy completeness, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:selection-bridge-existence]\label{ass:selection-bridge-existence}
For every \(d\in\mathcal D\), \(q_d\) is a square-integrable solution of \(p_P(S_{3d},S_{2d},X\mid A,G=E)/p_P(S_{3d},S_{2d},X\mid A,G=O)=(T_d^\star q_d)(S_{3d},S_{2d},A,X)\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Observed selection-bridge existence, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:operator-bijectivity]\label{ass:operator-bijectivity}
\(T_d\) is bijective for every \(d\in\mathcal D\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Designwise bridge-operator bijectivity, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:nondegenerate-components]\label{ass:nondegenerate-components}
\(0<\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\min\{V_{E,d},V_{O,d}\}\le\max_{d\in\mathcal D}\max\{V_{E,d},V_{O,d}\}<\infty\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Finite nondegenerate influence-component variances, \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:independent-pilot]\label{ass:independent-pilot}
\(\mathcal P_m\) is independent of the main-study observations used to construct \(\widehat\tau_B\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Pilot and analysis sample splitting, \cite{ChernozhukovEtAl2018}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:uniform-outcome-weak-rate]\label{ass:uniform-outcome-weak-rate}
For every deterministic sequence \(d_B\in\mathcal D\), \(\alpha_B\in(0,1)\) with \(|\alpha_B-\alpha_{d_B}^*(P)|\to0\), and fixed \(L\),
\[
\max_{1\le\ell\le L}\|T_{d_B}(\widehat h_{d_B,B}^{(-\ell)}-h_{d_B})\|_{2,O,q,d_B}=O_P(\delta_{h,B}).
\]
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:uniform-outcome-strong-rate]\label{ass:uniform-outcome-strong-rate}
For every deterministic sequence \(d_B\in\mathcal D\), \(\alpha_B\in(0,1)\) with \(|\alpha_B-\alpha_{d_B}^*(P)|\to0\), and fixed \(L\),
\[
\max_{1\le\ell\le L}\|\widehat h_{d_B,B}^{(-\ell)}-h_{d_B}\|_{2,B}^{\circ}=O_P(\rho_{h,B}).
\]
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:uniform-selection-weak-rate]\label{ass:uniform-selection-weak-rate}
For every deterministic sequence \(d_B\in\mathcal D\), \(\alpha_B\in(0,1)\) with \(|\alpha_B-\alpha_{d_B}^*(P)|\to0\), and fixed \(L\),
\[
\max_{1\le\ell\le L}\|T_{d_B}^{\star}(\widehat q_{d_B,B}^{(-\ell)}-q_{d_B})\|_{2,B}^{\circ}=O_P(\delta_{q,B}).
\]
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:uniform-selection-strong-rate]\label{ass:uniform-selection-strong-rate}
For every deterministic sequence \(d_B\in\mathcal D\), \(\alpha_B\in(0,1)\) with \(|\alpha_B-\alpha_{d_B}^*(P)|\to0\), and fixed \(L\),
\[
\max_{1\le\ell\le L}\|\widehat q_{d_B,B}^{(-\ell)}-q_{d_B}\|_{2,O,q,d_B}=O_P(\rho_{q,B}).
\]
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:outcome-weak-rate-vanishes]\label{ass:outcome-weak-rate-vanishes}
\(\delta_{h,B}=o(1)\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Maintained estimator-level rate condition, \cite{ChernozhukovEtAl2018}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:outcome-strong-rate-vanishes]\label{ass:outcome-strong-rate-vanishes}
\(\rho_{h,B}=o(1)\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Maintained estimator-level rate condition, \cite{ChernozhukovEtAl2018}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:selection-weak-rate-vanishes]\label{ass:selection-weak-rate-vanishes}
\(\delta_{q,B}=o(1)\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Maintained estimator-level rate condition, \cite{ChernozhukovEtAl2018}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:selection-strong-rate-vanishes]\label{ass:selection-strong-rate-vanishes}
\(\rho_{q,B}=o(1)\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Maintained estimator-level rate condition, \cite{ChernozhukovEtAl2018}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:uniform-bridge-product-rate]\label{ass:uniform-bridge-product-rate}
\(\min\{\delta_{h,B}\rho_{q,B},\rho_{h,B}\delta_{q,B}\}=o(B^{-1/2})\).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:pilot-gradient-variance-rate]\label{ass:pilot-gradient-variance-rate}
\(\max_{d\in\mathcal D}\max\{|\widehat V_{E,d}-V_{E,d}|,|\widehat V_{O,d}-V_{O,d}|\}=O_P(r_{V,m})\), where probability is under the full-catalogue pilot law and the maximum is over the entire finite admissible family and both sources.
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:pilot-variance-rate-vanishes]\label{ass:pilot-variance-rate-vanishes}
\(r_{V,m}=o(1)\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Maintained full-catalogue pilot estimator condition, \cite{ChernozhukovEtAl2018}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:main-gradient-variance-rate]\label{ass:main-gradient-variance-rate}
Under the joint pilot and main-study law,
\[
 \max_{g\in\{E,O\}}|\widetilde V_{g,\widehat d_m,B}-V_{g,\widehat d_m}|=O_P(r_{\widetilde V,B}).
\]
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:main-variance-rate-vanishes]\label{ass:main-variance-rate-vanishes}
\(r_{\widetilde V,B}=o(1)\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (Maintained estimator-level rate condition, \cite{ChernozhukovEtAl2018}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:source-arm-positivity]\label{ass:source-arm-positivity}
For every \(g\in\{E,O\}\) and \(a\in\{0,1\}\), \(0<p_{g,a}<1\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Novel.} standard: source-arm positivity
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:observed-outcome-bridge-existence]\label{ass:observed-outcome-bridge-existence}
For every \(d\in\mathcal D\), \(h_d\) is a square-integrable solution of
\[
\mathbb E_P[Y\mid S_{2d},S_{1d},A,X,G=O]
=\mathbb E_P[h_d(S_{3d},S_{2d},A,X)\mid S_{2d},S_{1d},A,X,G=O].
\]
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Standard} (observed proximal outcome-bridge existence (ImbensKallusMaoWang2025), \cite{ImbensKallusMaoWang2025}).
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:common-identification]\label{ass:common-identification}
For every \(d\in\mathcal D\), \(\theta_d(P)=\tau\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Novel.} novel: explicit common-identification subclass for design selection
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[ass:postselection-multiplier-condition]\label{ass:postselection-multiplier-condition}
For every deterministic \(d_B\in\mathcal D\) and \(\alpha_B\in(0,1)\) with \(|\alpha_B-\alpha_{d_B}^*(P)|\to0\), writing \(\Delta h_{B,\ell}=\widehat h_{d_B,B}^{(-\ell)}-h_{d_B}\) and \(\Delta q_{B,\ell}=\widehat q_{d_B,B}^{(-\ell)}-q_{d_B}\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\max_{1\le\ell\le L}\bigl(&
\|\mathbf1\{G=O\}\Delta q_{B,\ell}(Y-h_{d_B})\|_{2,B}^{\circ}
+\|\mathbf1\{G=O\}q_{d_B}\Delta h_{B,\ell}\|_{2,B}^{\circ}\\
&+\|\mathbf1\{G=O\}\Delta q_{B,\ell}\Delta h_{B,\ell}\|_{2,B}^{\circ}
\bigr)=o_P(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
\end{assumption}

\section{Definitions}

\begin{definition}[def:regular-law-class]\label{def:regular-law-class}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Regular causal full-\allowbreak{}catalogue law class}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} \(\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}\) is the subclass of \(\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\) satisfying long- and short-term consistency, observational latent exchangeability and overlap, experimental randomization and overlap, full short-term external validity, source support, the sequential-outcome restriction, and both proxy-completeness conditions for every \(d\in\mathcal D\) and \(a\in\{0,1\}\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Member properties.} \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}longterm-\allowbreak{}consistency}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}shortterm-\allowbreak{}consistency}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}observational-\allowbreak{}latent-\allowbreak{}exchangeability}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}observational-\allowbreak{}overlap}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}experimental-\allowbreak{}randomization}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}experimental-\allowbreak{}overlap}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}external-\allowbreak{}validity}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}source-\allowbreak{}support}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}sequential-\allowbreak{}outcomes}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}late-\allowbreak{}proxy-\allowbreak{}completeness}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}early-\allowbreak{}proxy-\allowbreak{}completeness}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}source-\allowbreak{}arm-\allowbreak{}positivity}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}observed-\allowbreak{}outcome-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}existence}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}selection-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}existence}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}operator-\allowbreak{}bijectivity}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}nondegenerate-\allowbreak{}components}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:observable-bridge-law-class]\label{def:observable-bridge-law-class}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Observable bridge law class}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} \[
\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}
=\left\{P:\begin{array}{l}
\text{source-arm positivity, a square-integrable observed outcome bridge, a square-integrable}\cr
\text{observed selection bridge, designwise bijectivity of \(T_d\), and finite strictly positive}\cr
\text{source-score variances hold for every \(d\in\mathcal D\)}
\end{array}\right\}.
\]
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Member properties.} \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}source-\allowbreak{}arm-\allowbreak{}positivity}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}observed-\allowbreak{}outcome-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}existence}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}selection-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}existence}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}operator-\allowbreak{}bijectivity}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}nondegenerate-\allowbreak{}components}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:design-value-profile]\label{def:design-value-profile}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Observable bridge-\allowbreak{}functional budget-\allowbreak{}normalized design-\allowbreak{}value profile}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} For \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\), \(d\in\mathcal D\), and \(0<\alpha<1\), set
\[
\mathcal V_d(\alpha;P)=\frac{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}{\alpha}+\frac{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}}{1-\alpha}.
\]
Set the value to \(+\infty\) outside the observable regular set.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{lem:\allowbreak{}designwise-\allowbreak{}gradient}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}observable-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}law-\allowbreak{}class}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:profiled-design-value]\label{def:profiled-design-value}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Profiled observable measurement-\allowbreak{}design value and oracle separation}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Set \(\mathcal V_d^*(P)=\inf_{0<\alpha<1}\mathcal V_d(\alpha;P)\) and retain all population ties in \(D^*(P)=\arg\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_d^*(P)\). Define \(\gamma(P)=\min_{d\notin D^*(P)}\{\mathcal V_d^*(P)-\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)\}\), with \(\gamma(P)=+\infty\) if \(D^*(P)=\mathcal D\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}design-\allowbreak{}value-\allowbreak{}profile}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}observable-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}law-\allowbreak{}class}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:design-value-gap]\label{def:design-value-gap}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Pairwise observable design-\allowbreak{}value gap}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} \(\Delta_P(d,d')=\mathcal V_d^*(P)-\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}profiled-\allowbreak{}design-\allowbreak{}value}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:pilot-selector]\label{def:pilot-selector}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Independent full-\allowbreak{}catalogue pilot selector}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} For every \(d\in\mathcal D\), fit the two observed bridge equations by cross-fitting on \(\mathcal P_m\), estimate \(V_{E,d}\) and \(V_{O,d}\) by foldwise score second moments, form \(\widehat{\mathcal V}_{d,m}^*=(\sqrt{c_{E,d}\widehat V_{E,d}}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}\widehat V_{O,d}})^2\), select a deterministic-tie-broken \(\widehat d_m\in\arg\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\widehat{\mathcal V}_{d,m}^*\), compute \(\widehat\alpha_{d,m}=\sqrt{c_{E,d}(\widehat V_{E,d}\vee\underline v_m)}/[\sqrt{c_{E,d}(\widehat V_{E,d}\vee\underline v_m)}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}(\widehat V_{O,d}\vee\underline v_m)}]\), set \(\widehat\alpha_m=\widehat\alpha_{\widehat d_m,m}\), and purchase \(N_{E,B}=\lfloor\widehat\alpha_mB/c_{E,\widehat d_m}\rfloor\) experimental units and \(N_{O,B}=\lfloor(1-\widehat\alpha_m)B/c_{O,\widehat d_m}\rfloor\) follow-up units.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal P\_\allowbreak{}m}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}profiled-\allowbreak{}design-\allowbreak{}value}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}observable-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}law-\allowbreak{}class}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:selected-wald-variance]\label{def:selected-wald-variance}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Selected-\allowbreak{}design-\allowbreak{}only Wald estimator and total variance}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} After the independently funded pilot chooses \(\widehat d_m\) and \(\widehat\alpha_m\), the main study measures only \(M_{\widehat d_m}\). Let \(\mathcal I_{E,B}\) and \(\mathcal I_{O,B}\) have sizes \(N_{E,B}\) and \(N_{O,B}\), and partition \(\mathcal I_{O,B}=\bigsqcup_{\ell=1}^L\mathcal I_{O,B,\ell}\) into deterministic balanced folds. Train only \(\widehat h_{\widehat d_m,B}^{(-\ell)}\) and \(\widehat q_{\widehat d_m,B}^{(-\ell)}\), excluding observational fold \(\ell\) from both fits and using the experimental sample only for the selection-bridge fit. For \(a\in\{0,1\}\), define the selected-design arm counts, empirical arm probabilities, bridge means, residual corrections, and scores exactly by
\[
e_{B,a}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{E,B}}\mathbf1\{A_i=a\},
\qquad
o_{B,\ell,a}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{O,B,\ell}}\mathbf1\{A_i=a\},
\]
\[
\widehat\mu_{\widehat d_m,a}^{(-\ell)}
=\frac1{e_{B,a}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{E,B}}\mathbf1\{A_i=a\}
\widehat h_{\widehat d_m,B}^{(-\ell)}(S_{3\widehat d_m,i},S_{2\widehat d_m,i},a,X_i),
\]
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widehat\mu_{B,\widehat d_m}(a)
={}&\frac1L\sum_{\ell=1}^L\widehat\mu_{\widehat d_m,a}^{(-\ell)}\\
&+\frac1L\sum_{\ell=1}^L\frac1{o_{B,\ell,a}}
\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{O,B,\ell}}\mathbf1\{A_i=a\}\widehat q_{\widehat d_m,B}^{(-\ell)}
\{Y_i-\widehat h_{\widehat d_m,B}^{(-\ell)}\},
\end{aligned}
\]
with every function evaluated at its selected-design arguments, and set \(\widehat\tau_B(\widehat d_m,\widehat\alpha_m)=\widehat\mu_{B,\widehat d_m}(1)-\widehat\mu_{B,\widehat d_m}(0)\). For \(i\in\mathcal I_{E,B}\), define
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widehat\phi_{E,\widehat d_m,B,i}
=\frac1L\sum_{\ell=1}^L\sum_{a=0}^1(-1)^{1-a}
\frac{\mathbf1\{A_i=a\}}{e_{B,a}/N_{E,B}}
\bigl\{&\widehat h_{\widehat d_m,B}^{(-\ell)}
(S_{3\widehat d_m,i},S_{2\widehat d_m,i},a,X_i)
-\widehat\mu_{\widehat d_m,a}^{(-\ell)}\bigr\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
For \(i\in\mathcal I_{O,B,\ell}\), define
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widehat\phi_{O,\widehat d_m,B,i}
={}&\sum_{a=0}^1(-1)^{1-a}
\frac{\mathbf1\{A_i=a\}}{o_{B,\ell,a}/|\mathcal I_{O,B,\ell}|}
\widehat q_{\widehat d_m,B}^{(-\ell)}(S_{2\widehat d_m,i},S_{1\widehat d_m,i},a,X_i)\\
&\quad\times\bigl\{Y_i-\widehat h_{\widehat d_m,B}^{(-\ell)}
(S_{3\widehat d_m,i},S_{2\widehat d_m,i},a,X_i)\bigr\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Set
\[
\widetilde V_{E,\widehat d_m,B}
=\frac1{N_{E,B}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{E,B}}
\widehat\phi_{E,\widehat d_m,B,i}^2,
\qquad
\widetilde V_{O,\widehat d_m,B}
=\frac1{N_{O,B}}\sum_{\ell=1}^L\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{O,B,\ell}}
\widehat\phi_{O,\widehat d_m,B,i}^2.
\]
Let \(\mathsf D_B\) require positive selected source, arm, and fold-arm counts. On \(\mathsf D_B\), define
\[
\widehat s_B^2=B\left\{\frac{\widetilde V_{E,\widehat d_m,B}}{N_{E,B}}+\frac{\widetilde V_{O,\widehat d_m,B}}{N_{O,B}}\right\},
\quad
s_{0,B}^2=B\left\{\frac{V_{E,\widehat d_m}}{N_{E,B}}+\frac{V_{O,\widehat d_m}}{N_{O,B}}\right\},
\]
and \(\mathsf G_{\mathrm{tot}}=\mathsf D_B\cap\{\widehat s_B^2>0\}\). On \(\mathsf G_{\mathrm{tot}}\), use the selected estimator, statistic centered at \(\theta_{\widehat d_m}(P)\), and Wald interval; on its complement set the estimator, standard error, and statistic to zero and the interval to \([0,0]\). Every zero denominator is totalized at zero. No unselected main-study fit or score variance is part of this measurable map.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}pilot-\allowbreak{}selector}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}observable-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}law-\allowbreak{}class}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:common-identification-class]\label{def:common-identification-class}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Common causal-\allowbreak{}identification subclass}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} \[
\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{id}}
=\{P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}:\theta_d(P)=\tau\text{ for every }d\in\mathcal D\}.
\]
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Member properties.} \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}longterm-\allowbreak{}consistency}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}shortterm-\allowbreak{}consistency}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}observational-\allowbreak{}latent-\allowbreak{}exchangeability}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}observational-\allowbreak{}overlap}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}experimental-\allowbreak{}randomization}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}experimental-\allowbreak{}overlap}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}external-\allowbreak{}validity}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}source-\allowbreak{}support}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}sequential-\allowbreak{}outcomes}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}late-\allowbreak{}proxy-\allowbreak{}completeness}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}early-\allowbreak{}proxy-\allowbreak{}completeness}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}source-\allowbreak{}arm-\allowbreak{}positivity}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}observed-\allowbreak{}outcome-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}existence}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}selection-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}existence}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}operator-\allowbreak{}bijectivity}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}nondegenerate-\allowbreak{}components}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}common-\allowbreak{}identification}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:bridge-differentiable-submodel]\label{def:bridge-differentiable-submodel}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Bridge-\allowbreak{}differentiable regular two-\allowbreak{}source submodel}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\) and \(d\in\mathcal D\), and write
\[
 H_d=(S_{3d},S_{2d},A,X),\qquad L_d=(S_{2d},S_{1d},A,X).
\]
A regular two-source parametric submodel \(\{P_t:|t|<t_0\}\) through \(P=P_0\), with source-conditional scores \(\ell_E\) and \(\ell_O\), is called \emph{bridge-differentiable for design \(d\) at \(P\)} when the following conditions hold.  First, the source-conditional laws are dominated on a common support and the path is differentiable in quadratic mean in each source.  Second, for all sufficiently small \(|t|\), \(P_t\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\) and its design-\(d\) outcome bridge \(h_{d,t}\) is unique.  Third, after identifying the common \(L_2\) coordinate spaces along the dominated path, there is \(\dot h_d\in L_2(H_d\mid G=O)\) such that
\[
 \left\|\frac{h_{d,t}-h_{d,0}}{t}-\dot h_d\right\|_{L_2(P(\cdot\mid G=O))}\longrightarrow0,
\]
and the conditional bridge equation
\[
 \mathbb E_{P_t}\{Y-h_{d,t}(H_d)\mid L_d,G=O\}=0
\]
may be differentiated at \(t=0\) in \(L_2(L_d\mid G=O)\) using the conditional-score rule.  This is a restriction on submodels, not a consequence of pointwise bijectivity of \(T_d\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}observable-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}law-\allowbreak{}class}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:clw-linear-cost-selector]\label{def:clw-linear-cost-selector}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Source-\allowbreak{}faithful CLW affine-\allowbreak{}linear continuous and exact-\allowbreak{}integer cost selectors}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Fix the source-faithful transported comparison from Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}clw\text{-}population\text{-}design\text{-}rule}\): treatment is completely randomized with equal arm allocation, and the two arms have the same affine-regression residual variance.  These are restrictions on the transported comparison, not on the persistent-confounding bridge law.  Map the published support \(\mathcal I(\gamma)\) only to \(M_d\); the role assignment \(r_d\) remains preattached by this note.

Choose a finite-dimensional nonredundant affine encoding \(X_{\mathrm{nr}}\), with constant and reference columns removed, and define
\[
 \xi_d=(1,X_{\mathrm{nr}}^\top,Z_{M_d}^\top)^\top,
 \qquad
 \Gamma_d(P)=\mathbb E_P(\xi_d\xi_d^\top\mid G=O),
 \qquad
 g_d(P)=\mathbb E_P(\xi_dY\mid G=O).                       \tag{1}
\]
The domain \(\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{CLW}}\) consists of laws \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\) such that, for every \(d\in\mathcal D\),
\[
 \mathbb E_P(Y^2+\|\xi_d\|^2\mid G=O)<\infty,
 \qquad \det\Gamma_d(P)\ne0,                              \tag{2}
\]
and the transported balanced comparison has the arm-homoskedastic residual required by Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}clw\text{-}population\text{-}design\text{-}rule}\).  The determinant condition concerns the full Gram matrix on the nonredundant basis; no pseudoinverse is used.  Put
\[
 \beta_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)=\Gamma_d(P)^{-1}g_d(P),
 \qquad
 r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)=
 \mathbb E_P\!\left[
 \{Y-\xi_d^\top\beta_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)\}^2\mid G=O
 \right].                                                   \tag{3}
\]
Under the linear paired-unit cost
\[
 c(M_d,n)=nC_d,
 \qquad C_d=c_{E,d}+c_{O,d},                                \tag{4}
\]
define the continuous and exact integer criteria by
\[
 n_{\mathrm{CLW},B}(d)=\frac{B}{C_d},
 \qquad
 \Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d}(P;B)=\frac{C_dr_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)}{B}, \tag{5}
\]
\[
 \Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)=
 \begin{cases}
 r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)/\lfloor B/C_d\rfloor,&\lfloor B/C_d\rfloor>0,\\
 +\infty,&\lfloor B/C_d\rfloor=0.
 \end{cases}                                                \tag{6}
\]
Retain all ties in
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)
 =\arg\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d}(P;B),
 \qquad
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)
 =\arg\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B). \tag{7}
\]
The continuous and exact-integer arm-normalized best-tie ratios are
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)=
 \frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)}\mathcal V_d^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)},
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)=
 \frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)}\mathcal V_d^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)}.             \tag{8}
\]
The IKM-normalized continuous and integer ratios are defined in Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}ikmw\text{-}normalized\text{-}design\text{-}value}\).  Equations (3)--(8) transport only the population affine-linear criterion.  The exact finite-catalogue empirical affine least-squares counterpart of equation~(3.6) is defined separately in Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}clw\text{-}empirical\text{-}linear\text{-}cost\text{-}selector}\).  Nothing here attributes persistent-confounding analysis, sequential bridge-role choice, or bridge-value results to Carneiro--Lee--Wilhelm, and no claim concerns their approximate OGA procedure or their original randomized-trial theorem.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{lem:\allowbreak{}clw-\allowbreak{}population-\allowbreak{}design-\allowbreak{}rule}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}profiled-\allowbreak{}design-\allowbreak{}value}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}observable-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}law-\allowbreak{}class}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:predictive-benchmark]\label{def:predictive-benchmark}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Distinct generic unrestricted-\allowbreak{}risk selectors and named CLW affine-\allowbreak{}linear cost selector}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} For each \(d\in\mathcal D\), define the two generic unrestricted observational prediction risks
\[
 R_d(P)=\inf_{f\in L_2(X,Z_{M_d})}
 \mathbb E_P[(Y-f(X,Z_{M_d}))^2\mid G=O],
\]
\[
 R_d^A(P)=\inf_{f\in L_2(A,X,Z_{M_d})}
 \mathbb E_P[(Y-f(A,X,Z_{M_d}))^2\mid G=O].                \tag{1}
\]
Their tie-preserving population selectors and arm-normalized source-score ratios are
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)=\arg\min_{d\in\mathcal D}R_d(P),
 \qquad
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)=\arg\min_{d\in\mathcal D}R_d^A(P),
\]
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)=
 \frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)}\mathcal V_d^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)},
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)=
 \frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)}\mathcal V_d^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)}.           \tag{2}
\]
Distinctly, on \(\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{CLW}}\), the transported Carneiro--Lee--Wilhelm population rule uses the affine-linear residual variance \(r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)\), not either unrestricted risk.  Under \(c(M_d,n)=nC_d\), its continuous and integer criteria, selectors, and arm-normalized ratio are those in Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}clw\text{-}linear\text{-}cost\text{-}selector}\):
\[
 \Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d}(P;B)=\frac{C_dr_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)}{B},
 \qquad
 \Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)
 =\frac{r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)}{\lfloor B/C_d\rfloor}.      \tag{3}
\]
The support is mapped only to \(M_d\), and this note's role assignment remains preattached.  No equality between \(r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)\) and \(R_d(P)\) or \(R_d^A(P)\) is imposed away from the binary centers where it is proved.  The two unrestricted-risk rules in (1)--(2) are secondary comparisons and are not attributed to a named published proxy-selection workflow.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}profiled-\allowbreak{}design-\allowbreak{}value}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}observable-\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}law-\allowbreak{}class}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}clw-\allowbreak{}linear-\allowbreak{}cost-\allowbreak{}selector}; \texttt{lem:\allowbreak{}clw-\allowbreak{}population-\allowbreak{}design-\allowbreak{}rule}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:binary-witness]\label{def:binary-witness}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Binary two-\allowbreak{}design arbitrary-\allowbreak{}cost-\allowbreak{}disadvantage witness family}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Fix an arbitrary prescribed \(\chi>1\). Set \(K=4\), \(c_1=c_2=(5\chi-3)/4\), \(c_3=c_4=1/2\), \(b_E=1\), \(b_O=2\), and \(\kappa=(5\chi-3)/2\), and put
\[
 y_{\chi}=\sqrt{1-\frac{3}{10\chi}}.
\]
Let \(X\) and every \(S_{2d}\) be constant, and take the nonredundant affine encoding \(X_{\mathrm{nr}}\) to be zero-dimensional. Set \(P_{\epsilon}(G=E)=P_{\epsilon}(G=O)=1/2\), and let a Rademacher \(U\) be independent of \(G\). In source \(E\), \(P_{\epsilon}(T=t\mid U=u,G=E)=1/2\); in source \(O\), \(P_{\epsilon}(T=t\mid U=u,G=O)=(1+tu/4)/2\). The proxies \(W_{\epsilon},W_{y_{\chi}},W_{1/2}^{(1)},W_{1/2}^{(2)}\) are mutually independent conditional on \(U\), are independent of \(G,A\) conditional on \(U\), and form
\[
 Z(a)=(W_{\epsilon},W_{y_{\chi}},W_{1/2}^{(1)},W_{1/2}^{(2)}),
\]
while \(Y(a)=U\) for both treatments. Take \(\mathcal D=\{d_{\mathrm p},d_{\mathrm e}\}\), where \(d_{\mathrm p}\) assigns \(W_{\epsilon}\) to role one and \(W_{y_{\chi}}\) to role three, whereas \(d_{\mathrm e}\) assigns \(W_{1/2}^{(1)}\) to role one and \(W_{1/2}^{(2)}\) to role three. Then
\[
 c_{E,d_{\mathrm p}}=\frac{5\chi-1}{2},\qquad
 c_{O,d_{\mathrm p}}=\frac{5\chi+1}{2},\qquad
 c_{E,d_{\mathrm e}}=2,\qquad c_{O,d_{\mathrm e}}=3,
\]
so the paired costs are \(C_{d_{\mathrm p}}=5\chi\) and \(C_{d_{\mathrm e}}=5\). For a design with early signal \(x\) and late signal \(y\), the candidate bridges are
\[
 h_d(l,a)=\frac{l}{y},
 \qquad
 q_d(e,a)=\frac{1-(2a-1)e/(4x)}{1-1/16},
\]
and define the exact center residual function
\[
 R(x,y)=\frac{(1-x^2)(1-y^2)}{1-x^2y^2}.                  \tag{1}
\]
Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\), rather than this construction, proves that the unrestricted unadjusted risk and the distinct CLW affine-linear residual both equal (1) at the symmetric centers. No such equality is imposed on perturbations in the constrained model. The named transported CLW selector compares \(C_dr_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)/B\); the two generic unrestricted-risk selectors remain separate secondary comparisons.

The \emph{binary constrained finite-cell model} \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{bin}}(\chi)\) is the family of laws \(P\) with the preceding prescribed catalogue, costs, cap, and role assignments, constant \(X\), zero-dimensional \(X_{\mathrm{nr}}\), constant \(S_{2d}\), latent variables \(U,T,W_1,W_2,W_3,W_4\in\{-1,1\}\), \(A=(T+1)/2\), and potential variables
\[
 Z(a)=(W_1,W_2,W_3,W_4),\qquad Y(a)=U,
 \qquad a\in\{0,1\}.
\]
For strictly positive probability vectors \((\pi_g:g\in\{E,O\})\), \((\nu_u:u\in\{-1,1\})\), \((\lambda_t:t\in\{-1,1\})\), observational treatment kernels \(e_u(t)\), and proxy kernels \(k_{j,u}(w)\), its latent-cell mass factorizes as
\[
 P(G=g,U=u,T=t,W_1=w_1,\ldots,W_4=w_4)
 =\pi_g\nu_u\bigl\{\mathbf 1\{g=E\}\lambda_t
 +\mathbf 1\{g=O\}e_u(t)\bigr\}
 \prod_{j=1}^4k_{j,u}(w_j).                               \tag{2}
\]
Thus \(U\) is independent of \(G\), treatment is randomized independently of \(U\) in source \(E\), and the four proxies are mutually independent conditional on \(U\) and invariant across source and treatment. The design \(d_{\mathrm p}\) assigns \((W_1,W_2)\) to roles one and three, and \(d_{\mathrm e}\) assigns \((W_3,W_4)\) to roles one and three; role two is constant. For each \(0<\epsilon<10^{-3}\), the preceding center \(P_{\epsilon}=P_{\chi,\epsilon}\) is the point having \(\pi_E=\pi_O=1/2\), \(\nu_{-1}=\nu_1=1/2\), \(\lambda_{-1}=\lambda_1=1/2\), \(e_u(t)=(1+tu/4)/2\), and
\[
 k_{j,u}(w)=\frac{1+s_juw}{2},
 \qquad
 (s_1,s_2,s_3,s_4)=(\epsilon,y_{\chi},1/2,1/2).
\]
The model is parameterized by the relative interiors of these finite probability simplices. Its relative topology is equivalently the Euclidean parameter topology or the maximum-norm topology \(\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{cell}}\) induced by the complete latent-cell vector in (2).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}regular-\allowbreak{}law-\allowbreak{}class}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}predictive-\allowbreak{}benchmark}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}clw-\allowbreak{}linear-\allowbreak{}cost-\allowbreak{}selector}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:binary-region]\label{def:binary-region}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Relatively open exact-\allowbreak{}integer binary CLW and joint-\allowbreak{}prediction region}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Fix \(\chi>1\).  For every \(0<\epsilon<10^{-3}\), decrease the radius \(r_\epsilon\), if necessary, as permitted by Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}exact\text{-}integer\text{-}binary\text{-}stability}\), and set \(r_\epsilon^{\mathrm{opt}}=r_\epsilon\).  Choose \(0<\delta_\epsilon<r_\epsilon^{\mathrm{opt}}\) and define
\[
 \mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}
 =\bigcup_{0<\epsilon<10^{-3}}
 \left\{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{bin}}(\chi):
 \|P-P_\epsilon\|_{\mathrm{cell}}<\delta_\epsilon\right\}. \tag{1}
\]
The radii are chosen simultaneously to retain the regularity, common-identification, CLW-admissibility, continuous named-rule, two generic prediction-rule, arm-normalized bridge-ordering, and exact-integer margin conclusions of Lemmas~\(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}witness\text{-}stability}\), \(\mathrm{lem{:}joint\text{-}predictive\text{-}binary\text{-}stability}\), and \(\mathrm{lem{:}exact\text{-}integer\text{-}binary\text{-}stability}\).  The set is relatively open in the constrained model \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{bin}}(\chi)\); no ambient-simplex openness is part of the definition.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}binary-\allowbreak{}witness}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}regular-\allowbreak{}law-\allowbreak{}class}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}clw-\allowbreak{}linear-\allowbreak{}cost-\allowbreak{}selector}; \texttt{lem:\allowbreak{}binary-\allowbreak{}witness-\allowbreak{}stability}; \texttt{lem:\allowbreak{}joint-\allowbreak{}predictive-\allowbreak{}binary-\allowbreak{}stability}; \texttt{lem:\allowbreak{}exact-\allowbreak{}integer-\allowbreak{}binary-\allowbreak{}stability}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:ikmw-normalized-design-value]\label{def:ikmw-normalized-design-value}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{IKM-\allowbreak{}normalized continuous and exact-\allowbreak{}integer profiled values}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} For each regular \(d\), define
\[
 \psi_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=(1-p_{E,1})\phi_{E,d},
 \qquad
 \psi_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=(1-p_{O,1})\phi_{O,d},
\]
\[
 V_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=(1-p_{E,1})^2V_{E,d},
 \qquad
 V_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=(1-p_{O,1})^2V_{O,d}.               \tag{1}
\]
Set
\[
 \mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)=
 \left\{
 \sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}}
 +\sqrt{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}}
 \right\}^2,
 \qquad
 D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)=
 \arg\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P). \tag{2}
\]
For the named continuous and exact-integer CLW selectors, define
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P;B)=
 \frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)}
 \mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d',\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)},
\]
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM,int}}(P;B)=
 \frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)}
 \mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d',\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)}. \tag{3}
\]
The two generic prediction-selector ratios are
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P)=
 \frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)}
 \mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d',\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)},
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{A,\mathrm{IKM}}(P)=
 \frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)}
 \mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d',\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)}. \tag{4}
\]
These are normalization-specific source-score profiles.  Their interpretation as causal efficiency bounds is designwise and requires the conditions of Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}ikmw\text{-}fixed\text{-}system\text{-}efficiency}\).  The named selectors in (3) transport only the CLW affine-linear criterion.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{lem:\allowbreak{}ikmw-\allowbreak{}fixed-\allowbreak{}system-\allowbreak{}efficiency}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}predictive-\allowbreak{}benchmark}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}clw-\allowbreak{}linear-\allowbreak{}cost-\allowbreak{}selector}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}common-\allowbreak{}identification-\allowbreak{}class}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:binary-ikmw-region]\label{def:binary-ikmw-region}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Relatively open exact-\allowbreak{}integer IKM-\allowbreak{}normalized binary region}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Fix \(\chi>1\).  For every \(0<\epsilon<10^{-3}\), jointly decrease \(r_\epsilon^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) as permitted by Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}exact\text{-}integer\text{-}binary\text{-}stability}\).  Let \(\delta_\epsilon\) be the radius in Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}binary\text{-}region}\), choose
\[
 0<\widetilde\delta_\epsilon
 <\min\{\delta_\epsilon,r_\epsilon^{\mathrm{IKM}}\},
\]
and define
\[
 \mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}
 =\bigcup_{0<\epsilon<10^{-3}}
 \left\{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{bin}}(\chi):
 \|P-P_\epsilon\|_{\mathrm{cell}}<\widetilde\delta_\epsilon\right\}. \tag{1}
\]
The radii retain the conclusions used to define \(\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}\), together with the strict IKM optimizer ordering and the exact-integer IKM-ratio stability from Lemmas~\(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}ikmw\text{-}normalized\text{-}stability}\) and \(\mathrm{lem{:}exact\text{-}integer\text{-}binary\text{-}stability}\).  This is a relatively open subset of \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{bin}}(\chi)\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}binary-\allowbreak{}witness}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}binary-\allowbreak{}region}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}clw-\allowbreak{}linear-\allowbreak{}cost-\allowbreak{}selector}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}ikmw-\allowbreak{}normalized-\allowbreak{}design-\allowbreak{}value}; \texttt{lem:\allowbreak{}binary-\allowbreak{}ikmw-\allowbreak{}normalized-\allowbreak{}stability}; \texttt{lem:\allowbreak{}joint-\allowbreak{}predictive-\allowbreak{}binary-\allowbreak{}stability}; \texttt{lem:\allowbreak{}exact-\allowbreak{}integer-\allowbreak{}binary-\allowbreak{}stability}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}common-\allowbreak{}identification-\allowbreak{}class}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:operator-comparison-handle]\label{def:operator-comparison-handle}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Operator comparison handle for raw population prediction risk}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Use \(\mathsf H_{\mathrm{comp}}\) to compare the source-score quadratic forms underlying \(\mathcal V_d^*(P)\) with the raw residual-risk quadratic form underlying \(R_d(P)\). These source-score forms have a gradient interpretation only along the bridge-differentiable paths declared for design \(d\). The exact target is a lawwise constant bounding the raw-risk selector ratio \(\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)\); the handle does not assert that a generalized eigenvalue is sharp and does not give an unconditional gradient or causal-efficiency interpretation.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}predictive-\allowbreak{}benchmark}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}profiled-\allowbreak{}design-\allowbreak{}value}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}bridge-\allowbreak{}differentiable-\allowbreak{}submodel}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:treatment-aware-pilot-selector]\label{def:treatment-aware-pilot-selector}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Split-\allowbreak{}sample saturated treatment-\allowbreak{}aware pilot selector}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} On an observational full-catalogue pilot from the binary witness family, order the records with \(G=O\) by their original pilot indices and split them deterministically into a training half \(\mathcal I_{m,O}^{\mathrm{tr}}\) and a validation half \(\mathcal I_{m,O}^{\mathrm{va}}\), whose cardinalities differ by at most one. For each \(d\in\mathcal D\) and each cell \(v=(a,x,z_{M_d})\), define the saturated training-half regression
\[
 \widehat f_{d,m}^A(v)=
 \frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{m,O}^{\mathrm{tr}}}
 \mathbf 1\{(A_i,X_i,Z_{M_d,i})=v\}Y_i}
 {\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{m,O}^{\mathrm{tr}}}
 \mathbf 1\{(A_i,X_i,Z_{M_d,i})=v\}},
\]
with value zero when the denominator vanishes. Define the held-out treatment-aware risk estimator
\[
 \widehat R_{d,m}^A=
 \frac{1}{|\mathcal I_{m,O}^{\mathrm{va}}|}
 \sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{m,O}^{\mathrm{va}}}
 \{Y_i-\widehat f_{d,m}^A(A_i,X_i,Z_{M_d,i})\}^2,
\]
with value zero if the validation half is empty, and let \(\widehat d_m^A\) be the deterministically tie-broken element of \(\arg\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\widehat R_{d,m}^A\). This is a measurable finite-cell rule using only the independently funded full-catalogue pilot; it does not fit a bridge or use main-study outcomes.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}predictive-\allowbreak{}benchmark}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}binary-\allowbreak{}witness}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:clw-empirical-linear-cost-selector]\label{def:clw-empirical-linear-cost-selector}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Exact finite-\allowbreak{}catalogue empirical CLW affine-\allowbreak{}linear selectors}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Order the full-catalogue pilot records by their original indices and let
\[
 \mathcal I_{m,O}=\{i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}:G_i=O\},
 \qquad m_O=|\mathcal I_{m,O}|.
\]
For every \(d\in\mathcal D\), define the exact observational-pilot affine residual quadratic
\[
 \widehat r_{d,m}^{\mathrm{lin}}=
 \begin{cases}
 \displaystyle
 \min_{b\in\mathbb R^{\dim(\xi_d)}}
 \frac1{m_O}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{m,O}}
 \{Y_i-\xi_{d,i}^\top b\}^2,&m_O>0,\\[6pt]
 0,&m_O=0.
 \end{cases}                                                \tag{1}
\]
This finite convex quadratic is evaluable even when its empirical Gram matrix is singular.  Define
\[
 \widehat\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d,m}(B)
 =\frac{C_d\widehat r_{d,m}^{\mathrm{lin}}}{B},
\]
\[
 \widehat\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d,m}^{\mathrm{int}}(B)=
 \begin{cases}
 \widehat r_{d,m}^{\mathrm{lin}}/\lfloor B/C_d\rfloor,
 &\lfloor B/C_d\rfloor>0,\\
 +\infty,&\lfloor B/C_d\rfloor=0.
 \end{cases}                                                \tag{2}
\]
Let \(\widehat d_{\mathrm{CLW},m}(B)\) and
\(\widehat d_{\mathrm{CLW},m}^{\mathrm{int}}(B)\) be the deterministically tie-broken minimizers of the two criteria in (2) over \(\mathcal D\).  Their exact-integer achieved ratios are
\[
 \widehat\rho_{\mathrm{CLW},m}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)=
 \frac{\mathcal V_{\widehat d_{\mathrm{CLW},m}^{\mathrm{int}}(B)}^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)},
\]
\[
 \widehat\rho_{\mathrm{CLW},m}^{\mathrm{IKM,int}}(P;B)=
 \frac{\mathcal V_{\widehat d_{\mathrm{CLW},m}^{\mathrm{int}}(B),\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d',\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)}. \tag{3}
\]
This is the exact finite-catalogue affine least-squares minimizer corresponding to Carneiro--Lee--Wilhelm equation~(3.6), transported with support mapped only to \(M_d\).  It does not implement or analyze approximate OGA.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}clw-\allowbreak{}linear-\allowbreak{}cost-\allowbreak{}selector}; \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal P\_\allowbreak{}m}.
\end{definition}

\section{Main results}

\begin{lemma}[lem:designwise-gradient]\label{lem:designwise-gradient}
For every \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\) and \(d\in\mathcal D\), the observed-data bridge contrast \(\theta_d(P):=\mu_{d,1}-\mu_{d,0}\) is identified. Along every bridge-differentiable regular two-source submodel for design \(d\) at \(P\) in Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}bridge\text{-}differentiable\text{-}submodel}\), its pathwise gradient has source components \(\phi_{E,d}\) and \(\phi_{O,d}\). Hence independent source sample sizes \(n_E,n_O\) have source-score variance
\[
 \frac{V_{E,d}}{n_E}+\frac{V_{O,d}}{n_O},
\]
and this is the gradient variance on the declared bridge-differentiable submodel class. Outside that class, \(V_{E,d}\) and \(V_{O,d}\) remain source-score variances, but pointwise bijectivity alone does not assert pathwise differentiability. No latent causal restriction, completeness condition, or equality with \(\tau\) is used or asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\) and \(d\in\mathcal D\), and abbreviate
\[
 H_d=(S_{3d},S_{2d},A,X),\qquad L_d=(S_{2d},S_{1d},A,X).
\]
Observed outcome-bridge existence gives
\[
 \mathbb E_P\{Y-h_d(H_d)\mid L_d,G=O\}=0. \tag{1}
\]
Equivalently, \(T_dh_d=\mathbb E_P(Y\mid L_d,G=O)\).  Bijectivity of \(T_d\) makes this solution unique.  Thus \(h_d\), the two experimental means \(\mu_{d,a}\), and the contrast \(\theta_d(P)=\mu_{d,1}-\mu_{d,0}\) are identified from the two observed source laws.

Now take a bridge-differentiable regular two-source submodel in Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}bridge\text{-}differentiable\text{-}submodel}\), with source-conditional scores \(\ell_E,\ell_O\) and bridge derivative \(\dot h_d\).  Differentiability in quadratic mean, the declared \(L_2\) bridge differentiability, and the usual product rule give
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \dot\mu_{d,a}
 &={\mathbb E}_P\!\left[
   \frac{\mathbf 1\{A=a\}}{p_{E,a}}
   \{h_d(S_{3d},S_{2d},a,X)-\mu_{d,a}\}\ell_E
   \mathrel{\Big|}G=E\right]\\
 &\quad+{\mathbb E}_P\{\dot h_d(S_{3d},S_{2d},a,X)\mid A=a,G=E\}. \tag{2}
\end{aligned}
\]
The selection-bridge equation states that \(T_d^\star q_d\) is the conditional density ratio of \((S_{3d},S_{2d},X)\) in source \(E\) relative to source \(O\), within each treatment arm.  Therefore, for every square-integrable \(f(H_d)\), iterated expectation gives
\[
 {\mathbb E}_P\{f(H_d)\mid A=a,G=E\}
 ={\mathbb E}_P\{q_d(L_d)f(H_d)\mid A=a,G=O\}. \tag{3}
\]

The defining submodel condition permits differentiation of (1) in conditional \(L_2\).  The conditional-score centering term is multiplied by the zero conditional residual mean in (1), and hence
\[
 T_d\dot h_d
 ={\mathbb E}_P[\{Y-h_d(H_d)\}\ell_O\mid L_d,G=O]. \tag{4}
\]
Apply (3) with \(f=\dot h_d\), then apply (4) and iterated expectation:
\[
\begin{aligned}
 {\mathbb E}_P(\dot h_d\mid A=a,G=E)
 &={\mathbb E}_P(q_d\dot h_d\mid A=a,G=O)\\
 &={\mathbb E}_P[q_d\{Y-h_d(H_d)\}\ell_O\mid A=a,G=O]. \tag{5}
\end{aligned}
\]
Subtracting (2) at \(a=0\) from (2) at \(a=1\), substituting (5), and using source-arm positivity to express arm-conditional expectations as source-conditional inner products yields
\[
 \dot\theta_d(P)
 ={\mathbb E}_P(\phi_{E,d}\ell_E\mid G=E)
  +{\mathbb E}_P(\phi_{O,d}\ell_O\mid G=O). \tag{6}
\]
The definitions of \(\mu_{d,a}\) imply \({\mathbb E}_P(\phi_{E,d}\mid G=E)=0\), while (1) implies \({\mathbb E}_P(\phi_{O,d}\mid G=O)=0\).  The nondegenerate-component condition makes both components square-integrable.  Thus they are the two source components of a pathwise gradient on the declared submodel class.  For independent source samples of sizes \(n_E,n_O\), the variances of their sample averages add, giving
\[
 \frac{V_{E,d}}{n_E}+\frac{V_{O,d}}{n_O}. \tag{7}
\]
This derivation uses the explicit bridge-differentiability restriction at the differentiation step (4); it does not infer local inverse stability from pointwise bijectivity.  The identification part and the definitions of the source-score variances remain meaningful without that path restriction.  No latent causal restriction, completeness condition, or equality with \(\tau\) was used.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The repaired lemma identifies the observed bridge contrast law-by-law and derives its two source gradient components only along the declared bridge-differentiable regular submodels.  The restriction is placed exactly at the load-bearing differentiation of the conditional bridge equation. \textit{Gap.} Pointwise \(L_2\) bridge existence and bijectivity do not supply local inverse continuity or differentiability of the bridge solution along every regular path. \textit{Consumer.} A downstream designer may always use \(V_{E,d}\) and \(V_{O,d}\) as source-score variance inputs, and may call their combination a gradient variance only after verifying bridge-differentiability for the relevant submodel class.

\begin{theorem}[thm:budget-profile]\label{thm:budget-profile}
For every \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\), \(d\in\mathcal D\), and fixed \(0<\alpha<1\), as \(B\to\infty\),
\[
B\left\{\frac{V_{E,d}}{n_{E,B}(d,\alpha)}+\frac{V_{O,d}}{n_{O,B}(d,\alpha)}\right\}
=\mathcal V_d(\alpha;P)+\mathfrak r_{d,B}(\alpha;P),
\]
where, once \(B\ge2\max\{c_{E,d}/\alpha,c_{O,d}/(1-\alpha)\}\),
\[
0\le\mathfrak r_{d,B}(\alpha;P)\le \frac2B\left\{
\frac{V_{E,d}c_{E,d}^2}{\alpha^2}+
\frac{V_{O,d}c_{O,d}^2}{(1-\alpha)^2}\right\}.
\]
Thus the floor-count source-score variance converges to \(\mathcal V_d(\alpha;P)\). The unique minimizer and minimum are
\[
\alpha_d^*(P)=\frac{\sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}}{\sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}}},
\qquad
\mathcal V_d^*(P)=\left(\sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}}\right)^2.
\]
On the bridge-differentiable submodel class of Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}bridge\text{-}differentiable\text{-}submodel}\), this profiles the designwise gradient variance. For \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{id}}\), it profiles the gradient variance of the common causal target only on that declared submodel class. It is a local causal semiparametric efficiency bound only when the designwise gradient is additionally canonical for \(\tau\), in particular under the fixed-system Imbens--Kallus--Mao--Wang conditions recorded separately.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\), \(d\in\mathcal D\), and \(0<\alpha<1\), and set
\[
 x_E=\frac{\alpha B}{c_{E,d}},\qquad
 x_O=\frac{(1-\alpha)B}{c_{O,d}}.
\]
The asserted lower bound on \(B\) makes \(x_E,x_O\ge2\). For every \(x\ge2\),
\[
 0\le \frac1{\lfloor x\rfloor}-\frac1x
 =\frac{x-\lfloor x\rfloor}{x\lfloor x\rfloor}
 \le\frac2{x^2}, \tag{1}
\]
because \(0\le x-\lfloor x\rfloor<1\) and \(\lfloor x\rfloor\ge x/2\). Applying (1) to the two source counts gives the exact decomposition
\[
 B\left\{\frac{V_{E,d}}{n_{E,B}(d,\alpha)}
 +\frac{V_{O,d}}{n_{O,B}(d,\alpha)}\right\}
 =\mathcal V_d(\alpha;P)+\mathfrak r_{d,B}(\alpha;P)
\]
and
\[
 0\le\mathfrak r_{d,B}(\alpha;P)
 \le\frac2B\left\{
 \frac{V_{E,d}c_{E,d}^2}{\alpha^2}
 +\frac{V_{O,d}c_{O,d}^2}{(1-\alpha)^2}\right\}. \tag{2}
\]
For fixed \(d\) and \(\alpha\), the right-hand side tends to zero. The left-hand side is the budget-scaled source-score variance; by Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}designwise\text{-}gradient}\), it is also the budget-scaled gradient variance along every bridge-differentiable submodel.

Put \(a_d=c_{E,d}V_{E,d}\) and \(b_d=c_{O,d}V_{O,d}\). Positive costs and Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}nondegenerate\text{-}components}\) imply \(a_d,b_d>0\). Direct algebra gives
\[
 \frac{a_d}{\alpha}+\frac{b_d}{1-\alpha}
 -(\sqrt{a_d}+\sqrt{b_d})^2
 =\frac{\{(1-\alpha)\sqrt{a_d}-\alpha\sqrt{b_d}\}^2}
 {\alpha(1-\alpha)}\ge0. \tag{3}
\]
Equality holds exactly when
\[
 \alpha=\frac{\sqrt{a_d}}{\sqrt{a_d}+\sqrt{b_d}}
 =\frac{\sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}}
 {\sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}}}
 =\alpha_d^*(P).
\]
This proves uniqueness and
\[
 \mathcal V_d^*(P)
 =(\sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}})^2.
\]
On \(\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{id}}\), the observed contrast equals the common causal target, but the gradient interpretation still requires the declared bridge-differentiable submodel restriction. If that gradient is additionally canonical for \(\tau\), its variance is the local semiparametric efficiency lower bound; the separately recorded fixed-system Imbens--Kallus--Mao--Wang result supplies that interpretation under its own normalization and premises. Thus the calculation itself is a cost profile of source-score variance and does not, by algebra alone, assert a new cross-design canonical-efficiency theorem.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Profiles the exact two-source source-score variance under monetary costs and quantifies integer-count rounding. \textit{Gap.} The anchor fixes a limiting source ratio; this algebraic profile optimizes it under design-specific costs, while the cross-design advance comes from comparing admissible measurement systems rather than from claiming a new fixed-system efficiency formula. \textit{Consumer.} A study designer receives an explicit budget share and profiled source-score value, with causal-efficiency interpretation only under separately stated canonical-gradient premises.

\begin{theorem}[thm:pilot-oracle-regret]\label{thm:pilot-oracle-regret}
Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\). For the independently funded full-catalogue pilot selector,
\[
0\le\mathcal V_{\widehat d_m}^*(P)-\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_d^*(P)\le2\varepsilon_m,
\qquad
\{\widehat d_m\notin D^*(P)\}\subseteq\{2\varepsilon_m\ge\gamma(P)\}.
\]
Conditional on the maintained full-catalogue pilot variance condition in Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}pilot\text{-}gradient\text{-}variance\text{-}rate}\), together with \(r_{V,m}=o(1)\) from Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}pilot\text{-}variance\text{-}rate\text{-}vanishes}\) and \(\underline v_m=o(1)\),
\[
\varepsilon_m=O_P(r_{V,m}),
\qquad
\max_{d\in\mathcal D}|\widehat\alpha_{d,m}-\alpha_d^*(P)|=O_P(r_{V,m}),
\]
and \(P\{\widehat d_m\notin D^*(P)\}\to0\), with the event empty when \(D^*(P)=\mathcal D\). With the stipulated zero value when either floor count vanishes,
\[
\max_{d\in\mathcal D}\left|B\left\{
\frac{V_{E,d}}{\lfloor\widehat\alpha_{d,m}B/c_{E,d}\rfloor}
+\frac{V_{O,d}}{\lfloor(1-\widehat\alpha_{d,m})B/c_{O,d}\rfloor}
\right\}-\mathcal V_d^*(P)\right|
=O_P(r_{V,m}^2+B^{-1})=o_P(1).
\]
The selector consistently chooses the complete optimizer set under these maintained pilot conditions; no causal interpretation is needed for this observable bridge-functional regret statement. The theorem does not derive the maintained variance condition from a primitive bridge learner.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\).  Write
\[
 v_d=\mathcal V_d^*(P),\qquad
 \widehat v_d=\widehat{\mathcal V}_{d,m}^*,
 \qquad v_*=\min_{d\in\mathcal D}v_d,
\]
and choose \(d^*\in D^*(P)\).  By the definition of \(\varepsilon_m\) and the minimizing property of \(\widehat d_m\),
\[
 v_{\widehat d_m}
 \le \widehat v_{\widehat d_m}+\varepsilon_m
 \le \widehat v_{d^*}+\varepsilon_m
 \le v_*+2\varepsilon_m. \tag{1}
\]
Since \(v_*\le v_{\widehat d_m}\),
\[
 0\le v_{\widehat d_m}-v_*\le2\varepsilon_m. \tag{2}
\]
If \(\widehat d_m\notin D^*(P)\), the definition of \(\gamma(P)\) gives
\[
 \gamma(P)\le v_{\widehat d_m}-v_*\le2\varepsilon_m. \tag{3}
\]
When every design is optimal, the left event is empty; otherwise finiteness of \(\mathcal D\) gives \(\gamma(P)>0\).

Set
\[
 Z_m=\max_{d\in\mathcal D}\max\bigl\{|\widehat V_{E,d}-V_{E,d}|,
 |\widehat V_{O,d}-V_{O,d}|\bigr\}.
\]
The maintained pilot variance condition and its vanishing-rate companion give \(Z_m=O_P(r_{V,m})=o_P(1)\).  By the pilot construction and Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}budget\text{-}profile}\),
\[
 \widehat v_d=G_d(\widehat V_{E,d},\widehat V_{O,d}),
 \qquad v_d=G_d(V_{E,d},V_{O,d}),
\]
where \(G_d(x,y)=(\sqrt{c_{E,d}x}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}y})^2\).  Positive population variances, positive costs, and finiteness of \(\mathcal D\) put all population pairs in a compact subset of \((0,\infty)^2\); with probability tending to one, the estimated pairs lie in a slightly larger such compact set.  The finitely many gradients \(\nabla G_d\) are uniformly bounded there.  Thus
\[
 \varepsilon_m=\max_{d\in\mathcal D}|\widehat v_d-v_d|
 =O_P(r_{V,m})=o_P(1). \tag{4}
\]
Equations (3)--(4) imply \(P\{\widehat d_m\notin D^*(P)\}\to0\).

The same compact-set argument applied to
\[
 A_d(x,y)=\frac{\sqrt{c_{E,d}x}}
 {\sqrt{c_{E,d}x}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}y}}
\]
shows that the variance truncation is inactive with probability tending to one and
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}|\widehat\alpha_{d,m}-\alpha_d^*(P)|
 =O_P(r_{V,m}). \tag{5}
\]
Finally, Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}second\text{-}order\text{-}allocation\text{-}loss}\), whose premises are exactly the same maintained pilot variance condition, its vanishing rate, nondegeneracy, and the pilot definition, gives
\[
\max_{d\in\mathcal D}\left|B\left\{
\frac{V_{E,d}}{\lfloor\widehat\alpha_{d,m}B/c_{E,d}\rfloor}
+\frac{V_{O,d}}{\lfloor(1-\widehat\alpha_{d,m})B/c_{O,d}\rfloor}
\right\}-\mathcal V_d^*(P)\right|
=O_P(r_{V,m}^2+B^{-1})=o_P(1).
\]
The deterministic tie rule was used only to choose an empirical minimizer in (1); the conclusion is therefore membership in the complete optimizer set, not convergence to an arbitrarily selected population minimizer.  Every stochastic conclusion in the proof is conditional on the maintained pilot variance condition; no primitive bridge learner or variance-rate derivation has been supplied.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The generic regret certificate is operational under the fixed-dimensional lemma, which supplies uniform pilot source-score variance error of order \(m^{-1/2}\). \textit{Gap.} Outside the fixed-dimensional regime the displayed pilot variance condition remains a hypothesis, not a consequence of the observable class. \textit{Consumer.} A pilot designer with fixed known bridge bases obtains a feasible plug-in selector with the theorem's oracle-regret bound.

\begin{theorem}[thm:postselection-wald]\label{thm:postselection-wald}
Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\). Conditional on the independent full-catalogue pilot, suppose the purchased experimental and observational samples are mutually independent and independent and identically distributed within source. Use the selected-design-only estimator, score moments, total event, variance estimator, statistic, and interval of Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}selected\text{-}wald\text{-}variance}\). Impose the maintained estimator-level conditions in Assumptions~\(\mathrm{ass{:}uniform\text{-}outcome\text{-}weak\text{-}rate}\), \(\mathrm{ass{:}uniform\text{-}outcome\text{-}strong\text{-}rate}\), \(\mathrm{ass{:}uniform\text{-}selection\text{-}weak\text{-}rate}\), \(\mathrm{ass{:}uniform\text{-}selection\text{-}strong\text{-}rate}\), \(\mathrm{ass{:}uniform\text{-}bridge\text{-}product\text{-}rate}\), \(\mathrm{ass{:}postselection\text{-}multiplier\text{-}condition}\), \(\mathrm{ass{:}pilot\text{-}gradient\text{-}variance\text{-}rate}\), and \(\mathrm{ass{:}main\text{-}gradient\text{-}variance\text{-}rate}\), together with the four declared bridge-rate vanishing conditions and the pilot and main-study variance-rate vanishing conditions. Let \(m\to\infty\), \(B\to\infty\), and \(\underline v_m=o(1)\). Then
\[
 P(\mathsf G_{\mathrm{tot}})\longrightarrow1,
\]
\[
 s_{0,B}^2-\mathcal V_{\widehat d_m}^*(P)=o_P(1),
 \qquad
 \widehat s_B^2-\mathcal V_{\widehat d_m}^*(P)=o_P(1),
\]
and the selected observable contrast obeys
\[
 \mathsf T_B=
 \frac{\sqrt B\{\widehat\tau_B-\theta_{\widehat d_m}(P)\}}{\widehat s_B}
 \rightsquigarrow\mathcal N(0,1),
 \qquad
 P\{\theta_{\widehat d_m}(P)\in I_B\}\longrightarrow1-\eta.
\]
All listed nuisance-rate, product-rate, multiplier, pilot-variance, and main-study-variance requirements are sufficient conditions of this conditional theorem. They are not conclusions of the observable law class or of the cited fixed-design theory. Primitive selected-path attainability without additional learner structure remains the separate open object \(\mathrm{oeq{:}primitive\text{-}selected\text{-}path\text{-}attainability}\).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(P\) as in the theorem.  We first establish a deterministic-design-and-allocation expansion and then transfer it through the independent pilot.

\emph{Interior counts.}  For each \(d\in\mathcal D\), positivity of the costs and Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}nondegenerate\text{-}components}\) imply \(0<\alpha_d^*(P)<1\).  Since \(\mathcal D\) is finite, some \(a_0\in(0,1/2)\) satisfies
\[
 2a_0\le \alpha_d^*(P)\le1-2a_0
 \qquad(d\in\mathcal D).                                    \tag{28}
\]
Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}pilot\text{-}oracle\text{-}regret}\) gives
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}|\widehat\alpha_{d,m}-\alpha_d^*(P)|=o_P(1). \tag{29}
\]
Thus, with probability tending to one, \(\widehat\alpha_m\in[a_0,1-a_0]\), and both purchased source counts are bounded below by a positive multiple of \(B\).  Conditional on the pilot, source-arm positivity and the assumed independent and identically distributed sampling imply convergence of every empirical source-arm proportion to its positive population value.  A finite union bound over the experimental arms and the fixed observational fold-arm cells gives
\[
 P(\mathsf D_B)\longrightarrow1.                              \tag{30}
\]

\emph{Deterministic-path expansion.}  Take any deterministic path \((d_B,\alpha_B)\) such that \(d_B\in\mathcal D\) and \(|\alpha_B-\alpha_{d_B}^*(P)|\to0\), and let \(e_B,o_B\) be its floor counts.  By (28) and finiteness of the cost catalogue,
\[
 e_B\asymp B,\qquad o_B\asymp B.                             \tag{31}
\]
Write \(h=h_{d_B}\), \(q=q_{d_B}\), \(\Delta h_\ell=\widehat h_{d_B,B}^{(-\ell)}-h\), and \(\Delta q_\ell=\widehat q_{d_B,B}^{(-\ell)}-q\).

We use the following elementary arm-ratio expansion.  If \(W_1,\ldots,W_N\) are independent and identically distributed, \(p_a=P(A=a)>0\), \(N_a=\sum_i\mathbf1\{A_i=a\}\), and \(f\in L_2\), then
\[
 \frac1{N_a}\sum_{i=1}^N\mathbf1\{A_i=a\}f(W_i)
 -E\{f(W)\mid A=a\}
 =\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N\frac{\mathbf1\{A_i=a\}}{p_a}
 \bigl[f(W_i)-E\{f(W)\mid A=a\}\bigr]+o_P(N^{-1/2}).       \tag{32}
\]
Indeed, the centered numerator divided by \(N\) is \(O_P(N^{-1/2})\), while \(Np_a/N_a-1=O_P(N^{-1/2})\); their product is \(O_P(N^{-1})\).  Conditional on the training observations, the same calculation and conditional Chebyshev inequality show that a fold-external random function with conditional \(L_2\)-norm \(o_P(1)\) contributes \(o_P(N^{-1/2})\).  Because the observational partition is deterministic and balanced and \(L\) is fixed, replacing equal fold weights by observation weights changes the average by \(o_P(B^{-1/2})\).

The observed outcome-bridge equation and the selection-bridge change-of-measure identity imply, for each arm and every square-integrable early-argument function \(u\) and late-argument function \(f\),
\[
 E_O\{u(S_{2d_B},S_{1d_B},a,X)(Y-h)\mid A=a\}=0,             \tag{33}
\]
\[
 E_E\{f(S_{3d_B},S_{2d_B},a,X)\mid A=a\}
 =E_O\{qf(S_{3d_B},S_{2d_B},a,X)\mid A=a\}.                 \tag{34}
\]
Consequently, conditional on the training observations, the nuisance drift in one arm is
\[
\begin{aligned}
 &E_E(\Delta h_\ell\mid A=a)
 +E_O\{(q+\Delta q_\ell)(Y-h-\Delta h_\ell)\mid A=a\}\\
 &\hspace{35mm}=-E_O(\Delta q_\ell\Delta h_\ell\mid A=a).   \tag{35}
\end{aligned}
\]
The two terms linear in \(\Delta h_\ell\) cancel by (34), and the terms multiplying \(Y-h\) vanish by (33).

Since \(\Delta q_\ell\) is an \(L_d\)-function, conditional expectation and Cauchy--Schwarz give
\[
\begin{aligned}
 |E_O(\Delta q_\ell\Delta h_\ell\mid A=a)|
 &\le C\|\Delta q_\ell\|_{2,O,q,d_B}
          \|T_{d_B}\Delta h_\ell\|_{2,O,q,d_B},\\
 |E_O(\Delta q_\ell\Delta h_\ell\mid A=a)|
 &\le C\|T_{d_B}^{\star}\Delta q_\ell\|_{2,O,h,d_B}
          \|\Delta h_\ell\|_{2,O,h,d_B}.                   \tag{36}
\end{aligned}
\]
The first line uses the observational strong-selection and weak-outcome errors.  In the second line, the artificial-law norms of the source-defined functions \(T_{d_B}^{\star}\Delta q_\ell\) and \(\Delta h_\ell\) control their observational norms because (31) keeps the observational mass of \(P_{B,d_B,\alpha_B}^{\circ}\) bounded away from zero.  The four maintained rates and the product-rate assumption make (35) \(o_P(B^{-1/2})\), uniformly over the fixed arms and folds.

The centered experimental nuisance perturbation is \(\Delta h_\ell\), whose conditional empirical fluctuation is \(o_P(B^{-1/2})\) by the artificial-law strong-outcome rate and conditional Chebyshev inequality.  The centered observational perturbation is
\[
 \Delta q_\ell(Y-h)-q\Delta h_\ell-\Delta q_\ell\Delta h_\ell. \tag{37}
\]
The multiplier condition makes the \(L_2(P_{B,d_B,\alpha_B}^{\circ})\)-norm of (37), after multiplication by \(\mathbf1\{G=O\}\), equal to \(o_P(1)\), so its centered foldwise empirical contribution is \(o_P(B^{-1/2})\).  Replacing population arm probabilities by empirical ones creates an \(O_P(B^{-1/2})\) factor.  For a nuisance term \(g_B\), the accompanying unscaled population error is bounded by
\[
 C|\widehat p_a-p_a|\,|P\{\mathbf1(A=a)g_B\}|
 \le C|\widehat p_a-p_a|\,\|g_B\|_2.                        \tag{38}
\]
The strong outcome rate handles \(g_B=\Delta h_\ell\); the multiplier condition handles \(q\Delta h_\ell\) and \(\Delta q_\ell\Delta h_\ell\); and \(\Delta q_\ell(Y-h)\) has zero arm mean by (33).  Hence every denominator-replacement error is \(o_P(B^{-1/2})\).  Training outside the observational evaluation fold and training the outcome bridge only on observational data supply the conditional independence used here.

Applying (32)--(38) and contrasting the arms yields
\[
 \widehat\tau_B(d_B,\alpha_B)-\theta_{d_B}(P)
 =\frac1{e_B}\sum_{i\in E_B}\phi_{E,d_B,i}
 +\frac1{o_B}\sum_{i\in O_B}\phi_{O,d_B,i}
 +o_P(B^{-1/2}).                                               \tag{39}
\]
This expansion is centered at the observed bridge contrast and uses no common-identification restriction.

The leading sums in (39) are independent and centered, with variances \(V_{E,d_B}/e_B\) and \(V_{O,d_B}/o_B\).  Take any subsequence and then a further subsequence on which \(d_B=d\) is constant, which is possible because \(\mathcal D\) is finite.  Put
\[
 s_{0,B}^2(d,\alpha_B)
 =B\left\{\frac{V_{E,d}}{e_B}+\frac{V_{O,d}}{o_B}\right\}.
\]
Nondegeneracy and (31) bound this variance above and away from zero.  After multiplication by \(\sqrt B/s_{0,B}\), the maximal coefficient of the independent triangular array is \(O(B^{-1/2})\), and the sum of variances is one.  For every \(\zeta>0\), the finite second moments of the two fixed-design source scores and the threshold tending to infinity give the written-out Lindeberg condition
\[
 \sum_j E\{X_{B,j}^2\mathbf1(|X_{B,j}|>\zeta)\}\longrightarrow0. \tag{40}
\]
The Lindeberg--Feller argument, obtained by the second-order expansion of the product characteristic function with its remainder bounded by (40), gives
\[
 \frac{\sqrt B}{s_{0,B}(d_B,\alpha_B)}
 \left\{
 \frac1{e_B}\sum_{i\in E_B}\phi_{E,d_B,i}
 +\frac1{o_B}\sum_{i\in O_B}\phi_{O,d_B,i}
 \right\}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal N(0,1).                  \tag{41}
\]
Every subsequence has such a further subsequence, so (41) holds along the original deterministic path.  Equations (39) and (41) prove the deterministic-path studentized limit.

\emph{Transfer through pilot selection.}  From (29), there is a deterministic \(t_B\downarrow0\) along the joint sequence such that
\[
 P\{|\widehat\alpha_m-\alpha_{widehat d_m}^*(P)|>t_B\}\longrightarrow0. \tag{42}
\]
The deterministic-path conclusions are uniform over
\[
 \{(d,\alpha):d\in\mathcal D,
 |\alpha-\alpha_d^*(P)|\le t_B\}.                            \tag{43}
\]
Otherwise, a deterministic violating pair could be chosen from (43) along a subsequence, contradicting the conclusion for every deterministic path.  Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}independent\text{-}pilot}\) permits conditioning on \((\widehat d_m,\widehat\alpha_m)\).  Thus (39)--(43) imply
\[
 \frac{\sqrt B\{\widehat\tau_B(\widehat d_m,\widehat\alpha_m)
 -\theta_{\widehat d_m}(P)\}}{s_{0,B}}
 \rightsquigarrow\mathcal N(0,1).                            \tag{44}
\]

The floor-count conclusion of Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}pilot\text{-}oracle\text{-}regret}\) gives
\[
 s_{0,B}^2-\mathcal V_{\widehat d_m}^*(P)=o_P(1).             \tag{45}
\]
On \(\mathsf D_B\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
 |\widehat s_B^2-s_{0,B}^2|
 \le{}&\frac{B}{N_{E,B}}
 |\widetilde V_{E,\widehat d_m,B}-V_{E,\widehat d_m}|\\
 &+\frac{B}{N_{O,B}}
 |\widetilde V_{O,\widehat d_m,B}-V_{O,\widehat d_m}|.      \tag{46}
\end{aligned}
\]
The coefficients are \(O_P(1)\) by (28)--(29), and the maintained main-study variance rate is \(o_P(1)\).  Combining (30), (45), and (46) proves
\[
 \widehat s_B^2-\mathcal V_{\widehat d_m}^*(P)=o_P(1).        \tag{47}
\]
Since \(\min_d\mathcal V_d^*(P)>0\), equations (30), (45), and (47) also yield
\[
 P(\mathsf G_{\mathrm{tot}})\longrightarrow1,
 \qquad \widehat s_B/s_{0,B}\longrightarrow_P1.             \tag{48}
\]
Combining (44) and (48) proves
\[
 \mathsf T_B\rightsquigarrow\mathcal N(0,1),                \tag{49}
\]
including the total zero extension outside \(\mathsf G_{\mathrm{tot}}\).

On \(\mathsf G_{\mathrm{tot}}\), the event \(\theta_{\widehat d_m}(P)\in I_B\) is exactly \(|\mathsf T_B|\le z_{1-\eta/2}\).  The complement of \(\mathsf G_{\mathrm{tot}}\) has probability tending to zero by (48), and the standard normal distribution is continuous at both critical values.  Equation (49) therefore gives
\[
 P\{\theta_{\widehat d_m}(P)\in I_B\}\longrightarrow1-\eta.
\]
Every step used only the sampling structure and the explicitly listed nuisance, product, multiplier, pilot-variance, and main-study-variance conditions.  In particular, the conclusion targets \(\theta_{\widehat d_m}(P)\); it becomes a statement about \(\tau\) only on the separately defined common-identification subclass.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Transfers a deterministic-path orthogonal expansion through an independent pilot-selected finite design and allocation, using only the selected main-study fits and a totalized variance estimator. \textit{Gap.} Imbens--Kallus--Mao--Wang fix the proxy system and source ratio; Bennett et al. and Zhang et al. address weak or nonunique bridge settings; Chernozhukov et al. supply cross-fitting. This theorem instead conditions on unique bridges and explicit learner rates and transfers inference through an independent-pilot random finite design and allocation. \textit{Consumer.} A downstream analyst obtains a Gaussian statistic and Wald interval for the selected observed bridge contrast, and for the causal effect on the common-identification subclass.

\begin{proposition}[prop:anchor-reduction]\label{prop:anchor-reduction}
For every \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\), if \(\mathcal D=\{d\}\), the main study fixes positive counts \(n_E,n_O\), and \(B=c_{E,d}n_E+c_{O,d}n_O\), then
\[
 \frac1B\mathcal V_d\!\left(\frac{c_{E,d}n_E}{B};P\right)
 =\frac{V_{E,d}}{n_E}+\frac{V_{O,d}}{n_O}.
\]
The right-hand side is the fixed-system two-source pathwise-gradient variance for \(\theta_d(P)\). If \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{id}}\) and the fixed-system Imbens--Kallus--Mao--Wang canonical-gradient conditions hold, it is also their causal semiparametric efficiency bound for \(\tau\); absent those additional conditions only the observable bridge-functional identity is asserted.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\) and the singleton design \(d\).  Put
\[
 \alpha=\frac{c_{E,d}n_E}{B}.
\]
Because \(B=c_{E,d}n_E+c_{O,d}n_O\) and both counts and costs are positive,
\[
 0<\alpha<1,
 \qquad
 1-\alpha=\frac{c_{O,d}n_O}{B}.
\]
Substitution into the design-value profile gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \frac1B\mathcal V_d(\alpha;P)
 &=\frac1B\left\{
 \frac{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}{c_{E,d}n_E/B}
 +\frac{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}}{c_{O,d}n_O/B}\right\}\\
 &=\frac{V_{E,d}}{n_E}+\frac{V_{O,d}}{n_O}.
\end{aligned}
\]
By Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}designwise\text{-}gradient}\), the last expression is the fixed-system two-source pathwise-gradient variance for \(\theta_d(P)\).  If \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{id}}\), the causal-identification premise gives \(\theta_d(P)=\tau\).  Under the additional fixed-system canonical-gradient premises, Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}ikmw\text{-}fixed\text{-}system\text{-}efficiency}\) identifies the corresponding two-source canonical-gradient variance as the causal semiparametric efficiency lower bound.  Without those causal-identification and canonical-gradient premises, the displayed equality remains only the observable bridge-functional identity.
\end{proof}
