\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Places the design calculation on the observable bridge model and reserves causal interpretation for the separate common-identification subclass. \textit{Gap.} The anchor fixes the short-term system and source ratio and does not optimize the finite catalogue under costs. \textit{Consumer.} Investigators can compare admissible measurement systems and allocate the main-study budget using source-specific gradient moments.

\begin{theorem}[thm:predictive-impossibility]\label{thm:predictive-impossibility}
For the transported Carneiro--Lee--Wilhelm population affine-linear measurement rule, the following impossibility holds. Fix \(B>0\). For every prescribed finite paired-cost disadvantage \(\chi>1\), every center \(P_{\chi,\epsilon}\), \(0<\epsilon<10^{-3}\), belongs to \(\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}\cap\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{CLW}}\), both bridge contrasts equal \(\tau=0\), and the named continuous selector chooses \(d_{\mathrm p}\) whereas both the arm-normalized and IKM-normalized bridge profiles choose \(d_{\mathrm e}\):
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)=\{d_{\mathrm p}\},
 \qquad
 D^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\{d_{\mathrm e}\}.                                      \tag{1}
\]
The paired costs are \(5\chi\) and \(5\), and
\[
 B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm p}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\frac32\frac{1-\epsilon^2}{1-\epsilon^2y_\chi^2}
 <\frac32<3
 =B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm e}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B). \tag{2}
\]
For each center there is a finite \(B_0(P_{\chi,\epsilon})\) such that the exact integer-budget selector also equals \(\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\) for every \(B\ge B_0(P_{\chi,\epsilon})\). Moreover,
\[
 V_{O,d_{\mathrm p}}
 =\frac{64}{75(10\chi-3)}\epsilon^{-2}+O_\chi(1),
 \qquad
 V_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac14V_{E,d},
 \qquad
 V_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac14V_{O,d},                    \tag{3}
\]
\[
 \mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\frac14\mathcal V_d^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon}),               \tag{4}
\]
and
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\frac{5\chi+1}{363(10\chi-3)}\epsilon^{-2}
 +O_\chi(\epsilon^{-1}).                                  \tag{5}
\]
Across the center family, any minimizing paired-cost factor satisfies
\[
 \chi_{\mathrm{opt}}(\epsilon)
 \sim \frac{\sqrt[3]{18}}{15}\epsilon^{-2/3},             \tag{6}
\]
and, for either named center ratio \(\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}\),
\[
 \epsilon^2\inf_{\chi>1}\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\frac1{726}+\frac{5\sqrt[3]{12}}{968}\epsilon^{2/3}
 +o(\epsilon^{2/3}).                                       \tag{7}
\]
Every \(P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}\) remains regular, CLW-admissible, and common-identifying, satisfies
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)=\{d_{\mathrm p}\},
 \qquad D^*(P)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\},
 \qquad
 \sup_{P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}}\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)=\infty, \tag{8}
\]
and has the law-specific eventual integer-selector conclusion. On \(\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}\),
\[
 D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\},
 \qquad
 \sup_{P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}}
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P;B)=\infty.          \tag{9}
\]
At every center the unrestricted raw-risk selector also chooses \(d_{\mathrm p}\), and its arm-normalized and IKM ratios equal the two named ratios; the generic open-region conclusions remain secondary. For each fixed \(\chi>1\), the center primitive cost vector lies in \(\mathcal C_\kappa\), has paired-cost ratio \(\chi\), and has a relatively open cost-manifold neighborhood preserving the strict cost and objective orderings for sufficiently small positive \(\epsilon\).

The obstruction concerns only the transported CLW population affine-linear residual-variance-over-sample-size rule with support mapped to \(M_d\). It does not refute Carneiro--Lee--Wilhelm's randomized-trial theorem, their empirical equation~(3.6), their OGA procedure, an arbitrary predictive method, or a bridge-role assignment by that paper. The IKM profile has its absolute causal-efficiency interpretation only under the separately cited fixed-system premises.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) proves every center assertion: membership in both law classes, common identification, the continuous CLW ordering, the two bridge-profile orderings, the source-score values, equality of the named center ratios, their fixed-\(\chi\) expansion, and the optimized sharp elbow.  In particular, its floor calculation proves more than the stated eventual claim:
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\{d_{\mathrm p}\}
 \quad\text{for every }B\ge5\chi,
\]
so one may take \(B_0(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=5\chi\).  At a center balanced treatment gives
\[
 V_{g,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac14V_{g,d},
 \qquad
 \mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\frac14\mathcal V_d^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon}),
 \qquad g\in\{E,O\},                                    \tag{37}
\]
which proves (3)--(4).  The exact center ratio from the sharp-elbow lemma expands as
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\frac{5\chi+1}{363(10\chi-3)}\epsilon^{-2}
 +O_\chi(\epsilon^{-1}),
\]
and its rescaled optimization proves (6)--(7).

Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}witness\text{-}stability}\) proves that regularity, CLW admissibility, common identification, the named ordering, and the arm-normalized bridge ordering persist in each constrained-model ball used to define \(\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}\).  Every center lies in its ball, and the ratio on the corresponding ball exceeds one half of a center ratio that is larger than \((\epsilon^{-2}+14)/726\).  Letting \(\epsilon\downarrow0\) proves (8).  The same lemma proves the stated law-specific eventual integer conclusion.

Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}ikmw\text{-}normalized\text{-}stability}\) preserves the strict IKM optimizer and more than one half of the diverging center IKM ratio.  Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}binary\text{-}ikmw\text{-}region}\) intersects that radius with the preceding stability radius, proving (9).  Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}ikmw\text{-}fixed\text{-}system\text{-}efficiency}\) supplies the absolute causal-efficiency interpretation only under its additional fixed-system premises.

At a center, the sharp-elbow lemma separately proves equality of the affine CLW residual and the unrestricted raw risk.  It follows that the raw-risk selector is \(\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\) and that its arm-normalized and IKM ratios equal the named ratios, without making any off-center substitution.

Finally, the primitive costs obey
\[
 c_1+c_2=\kappa,
 \qquad c_3+c_4=1<\kappa,
 \qquad C_{d_{\mathrm p}}=5\chi,
 \qquad C_{d_{\mathrm e}}=5,
\]
and their two sourcewise gaps equal \(5(\chi-1)/2>0\).  Thus the center primitive cost vector lies in \(\mathcal C_\kappa\) and has paired-cost ratio \(\chi\).  On the relative cap-feasible cost manifold, primitive costs, the two affine criteria, and both bridge profiles are continuous.  Their center gaps are strict.  Consequently a sufficiently small relative cost neighborhood preserves the strict sourcewise cost ordering, the named objective ordering, and both bridge-profile orderings for sufficiently small positive \(\epsilon\).  No step invokes the empirical least-squares rule, OGA, an arbitrary predictive method, or a bridge-role assignment by the comparator paper.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The named CLW specialization selects a predictively attractive but bridge-inefficient proxy design, with exact cost and ratio formulas. \textit{Gap.} Carneiro--Lee--Wilhelm do not analyze persistent-confounding bridges; the transport maps only measured support and retains their affine criterion. \textit{Consumer.} A measurement designer should compare bridge-value gaps instead of using affine residual fit as an information surrogate.

\begin{lemma}[lem:ikmw-fixed-design-limit]\label{lem:ikmw-fixed-design-limit}
For a fixed short-term-outcome system and source-conditional law \(P\), let \(n=n_E+n_O\) and define the artificial superpopulation law \(P_n^{\circ}\) by
\[
 P_n^{\circ}(G=E)=\frac{n_E}{n},
 \qquad
 P_n^{\circ}(G=O)=\frac{n_O}{n},
 \qquad
 P_n^{\circ}(\cdot\mid G=g)=P(\cdot\mid G=g).
\]
Every norm in the statement below is \(\|\cdot\|_2=\|\cdot\|_{L_2(P_n^{\circ})}\).  Use a fixed number \(L_0\ge2\) of observational-only cross-fitting folds, independent i.i.d. observations within the two mutually independent sources, \(n_E,n_O\to\infty\), and \(n_E/n_O\to\lambda\in(0,\infty)\).  Suppose Imbens--Kallus--Mao--Wang Theorem~4 statements 1 and 2, their square-integrable latent outcome and selection bridge premises, the corresponding observed moment equations, and both parts of their Assumption~8 hold with nuisance limits equal to the true bridges \(h_0\) and \(q_0\).  Thus, for every \(1\le\ell\le L_0\),
\[
 \|\mathsf T_0(\widehat h^{(-\ell)}-h_0)\|_2=O_P(\delta_{h,n}),
 \qquad
 \|\widehat h^{(-\ell)}-h_0\|_2=O_P(\rho_{h,n}),
\]
\[
 \|\mathsf T_0^\star(\widehat q^{(-\ell)}-q_0)\|_2=O_P(\delta_{q,n}),
 \qquad
 \|\widehat q^{(-\ell)}-q_0\|_2=O_P(\rho_{q,n}),
\]
all four rates tend to zero, and
\[
 \min\{\delta_{h,n}\rho_{q,n},\rho_{h,n}\delta_{q,n}\}
 =o(n^{-1/2}).
\]
Define the two score moments printed in their Theorem~5, kept distinct from this paper's arm-normalized \(V_{E,d}\) and \(V_{O,d}\), by
\[
 V_E^{\mathrm{IKM}}
 =\mathbb E_P\!\left[
 \left\{\frac{A-P(A=1\mid G=E)}{P(A=1\mid G=E)}
 \bigl(h_0(S_3,S_2,A,X)-\mu_0(A)\bigr)\right\}^2
 \mathrel{\Big|}G=E\right]
\]
and
\[
 V_O^{\mathrm{IKM}}
 =\mathbb E_P\!\left[
 \left\{\frac{A-P(A=1\mid G=O)}{P(A=1\mid G=O)}
 q_0(S_2,S_1,A,X)
 \bigl(Y-h_0(S_3,S_2,A,X)\bigr)\right\}^2
 \mathrel{\Big|}G=O\right].
\]
If these two moments are finite and strictly positive, their Theorem~5 states that
\[
 \sqrt n\,(\widehat\tau_{\mathrm{DR}}-\tau_0)
 \rightsquigarrow
 \mathcal N\!\left(0,
 \frac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}V_E^{\mathrm{IKM}}
 +(1+\lambda)V_O^{\mathrm{IKM}}\right).
\]
This cited statement records the source's printed normalization only.  It neither identifies \(V_g^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) with this paper's \(V_{g,d}\) nor supplies the arm-normalized fixed-path expansion used by the post-selection theorem.
\end{lemma}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This is a source-faithful comparison node that binds the artificial-superpopulation norm and preserves the source's printed score normalization. \textit{Gap.} It does not prove the arm-normalized expansion used by this note and is not a substitute for that derivation. \textit{Consumer.} Readers can audit exactly what is imported from the fixed-system paper and what is newly proved here.

\begin{lemma}[lem:ikmw-fixed-system-efficiency]\label{lem:ikmw-fixed-system-efficiency}
For a fixed short-term-outcome system and a design \(d\), let \(V_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) and \(V_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) denote the two source-score second moments printed in Imbens--Kallus--Mao--Wang Theorem~5 for the causal target \(\tau\). Suppose the conditions of their Theorem~7 hold: their Assumptions~1--4 and~6 define the local semiparametric model; at the law under evaluation, their Assumptions~6 and~7 admit unique square-integrable outcome and selection bridges; the corresponding conditional-expectation operator is bijective; and \(V_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) and \(V_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) are finite. Suppose also that \(n_E,n_O\to\infty\) and \(n_E/n_O\to\lambda\in(0,\infty)\). Then their Theorem~7 gives the local efficiency lower bound
\[
 \sigma_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^2
 =\frac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}V_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}
 +(1+\lambda)V_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}
 =\lim (n_E+n_O)\left(
 \frac{V_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}}{n_E}
 +\frac{V_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}}{n_O}
 \right).
\]
The proof of their Theorem~7 identifies the full two-source influence function assembled from the experimental and observational source terms as the efficient influence function, equivalently the canonical gradient, for \(\tau\); the two conditional source-score terms are components of that influence function rather than separate canonical gradients.

For attainment, additionally impose the remaining sampling and estimation premises of their Theorem~5: independent identically distributed observations within the two sources, both identification-and-consistency premises in statements~1 and~2 of their Theorem~4, cross-fitting, and both parts of their Assumption~8 with nuisance limits equal to the true bridges, all weak- and strong-metric errors vanishing, and the minimum of the outcome-weak--selection-strong and outcome-strong--selection-weak error products equal to \(o((n_E+n_O)^{-1/2})\). Under these premises and the same fixed-ratio regime, Theorem~5 gives asymptotic normality with variance \(\sigma_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^2\), while Theorem~7 identifies that variance as the efficiency lower bound; hence their doubly robust estimator attains the bound for \(\tau\). Equivalently, on the original estimator scale, the leading variance is \(V_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}/n_E+V_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}/n_O\).
\end{lemma}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This cited leaf records the published IKM-normalized fixed-ratio efficiency and attainment result, including its explicit \(\lambda\)-dependent variance. \textit{Gap.} The source establishes efficiency for the assembled two-source influence function; it does not make the two conditional source terms separate canonical gradients or justify arbitrary source-ratio sequences. \textit{Consumer.} The binary calculation and every absolute causal-efficiency interpretation use this leaf only after proving the normalization and all causal-identification premises.

\begin{lemma}[lem:second-order-allocation-loss]\label{lem:second-order-allocation-loss}
Let \(\mathcal D\) be finite and nonempty. Suppose the costs and population variance components obey
\[
0<\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\min\{c_{E,d},c_{O,d},V_{E,d},V_{O,d}\}
\le
\max_{d\in\mathcal D}\max\{c_{E,d},c_{O,d},V_{E,d},V_{O,d}\}<\infty.
\]
Suppose
\[
\max_{d\in\mathcal D}\max\bigl\{
|\widehat V_{E,d}-V_{E,d}|,
|\widehat V_{O,d}-V_{O,d}|\bigr\}=O_P(r_{V,m}),
\qquad r_{V,m}=o(1),
\qquad \underline v_m\downarrow0,
\]
and define \(\widehat\alpha_{d,m}\) by the full-catalogue pilot selector. For every \(d\) inside the following maximum, assign the entire floor-count expression the value zero whenever either floor count is zero. Then, along every joint sequence \(m\to\infty\) and \(B\to\infty\),
\[
\max_{d\in\mathcal D}\left|B\left\{
\frac{V_{E,d}}{\lfloor\widehat\alpha_{d,m}B/c_{E,d}\rfloor}
+\frac{V_{O,d}}{\lfloor(1-\widehat\alpha_{d,m})B/c_{O,d}\rfloor}
\right\}-\mathcal V_d^*(P)\right|
=O_P\!\left(r_{V,m}^2+B^{-1}\right).
\]
In particular, allocation estimation enters the profiled value only at second order, and the asymptotically inactive truncation contributes no term to the sharp rate.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write
\[
 Z_m=\max_{d\in\mathcal D}\max\{|\widehat V_{E,d}-V_{E,d}|,
 |\widehat V_{O,d}-V_{O,d}|\}.
\]
By hypothesis, \(Z_m=O_P(r_{V,m})=o_P(1)\).  Finiteness of \(\mathcal D\) and the displayed uniform bounds furnish constants \(0<\nu_-<\nu_+<\infty\) containing every population variance and every cost.  On the event
\[
 \mathcal E_m=\{Z_m<\nu_-/2\}\cap\{\underline v_m<\nu_-/2\}, \tag{1}
\]
whose probability tends to one, every estimated variance is positive and exceeds \(\underline v_m\).  Hence the truncation in \(\widehat\alpha_{d,m}\) is inactive.

For
\[
 A_d(x,y)=\frac{\sqrt{c_{E,d}x}}
 {\sqrt{c_{E,d}x}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}y}},
\]
the gradients are uniformly bounded on a common compact rectangle around all population pairs.  The mean-value theorem and (1) therefore yield
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}|\widehat\alpha_{d,m}-\alpha_d^*(P)|
 =O_P(r_{V,m}). \tag{2}
\]
The population optimal shares form a finite subset of \((0,1)\); hence there is \(a_0>0\) such that, with probability tending to one,
\[
 a_0\le\widehat\alpha_{d,m}\le1-a_0
 \qquad(d\in\mathcal D). \tag{3}
\]

For fixed \(d\), set \(a=c_{E,d}V_{E,d}\), \(b=c_{O,d}V_{O,d}\), and \(F_d(u)=a/u+b/(1-u)\).  The budget-profile theorem gives \(u_d^*=\alpha_d^*(P)=\sqrt a/(\sqrt a+\sqrt b)\) and \(\mathcal V_d^*(P)=(\sqrt a+\sqrt b)^2\).  A direct calculation gives the exact loss identity
\[
 F_d(u)-\mathcal V_d^*(P)
 =\mathcal V_d^*(P)\frac{(u-u_d^*)^2}{u(1-u)}. \tag{4}
\]
Combining (2)--(4), uniformly over the finite design family, gives
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}
 |F_d(\widehat\alpha_{d,m})-\mathcal V_d^*(P)|
 =O_P(r_{V,m}^2). \tag{5}
\]

On the event (3), both continuous source counts are at least a positive constant times \(B\).  For all sufficiently large \(B\), the floor inequality used in Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}budget\text{-}profile}\) applies uniformly and gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
 0\le{}&B\left\{
 \frac{V_{E,d}}{\lfloor\widehat\alpha_{d,m}B/c_{E,d}\rfloor}
 +\frac{V_{O,d}}{\lfloor(1-\widehat\alpha_{d,m})B/c_{O,d}\rfloor}
 \right\}-F_d(\widehat\alpha_{d,m})\\
 &\le\frac{C}{B}
\end{aligned} \tag{6}
\]
with one finite constant \(C\) for all \(d\).  Equations (5)--(6) prove the asserted \(O_P(r_{V,m}^2+B^{-1})\) bound.  The stipulated zero convention matters only off an event whose probability tends to zero, so it does not alter this stochastic order.  Equation (1) also shows that truncation is asymptotically inactive, which proves the final assertion.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The explicit finite-family expansion shows that estimating the square-root allocation changes the profiled value only quadratically, apart from integer rounding. \textit{Gap.} The fixed-ratio anchor does not quantify the cost of learning the source allocation from a pilot. \textit{Consumer.} The pilot designer can separate first-order measurement-system regret from second-order allocation error.

\begin{proposition}[prop:postselection-wald-ate-corollary]\label{prop:postselection-wald-ate-corollary}
For every \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{id}}\), under the sampling and maintained estimator-level conditions of Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}postselection\text{-}wald}\),
\[
 \frac{\sqrt B(\widehat\tau_B-\tau)}{\widehat s_B}
 \rightsquigarrow\mathcal N(0,1),
 \qquad
 P\{\tau\in I_B\}\longrightarrow1-\eta,
\]
with the same total-event zero extension as in that theorem.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Let \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{id}}\).  By Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}common\text{-}identification\text{-}class}\),
\[
 \theta_d(P)=\tau\qquad\text{for every }d\in\mathcal D.
\]
In particular, \(\theta_{\widehat d_m}(P)=\tau\) for every pilot realization.  Therefore the studentized statistic in Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}postselection\text{-}wald}\) is exactly
\[
 \frac{\sqrt B(\widehat\tau_B-\tau)}{\widehat s_B}
\]
on \(\mathsf G_{\mathrm{tot}}\), with the same zero extension on its complement.  The theorem's normal limit consequently gives the first conclusion.  Likewise, on \(\mathsf G_{\mathrm{tot}}\), its coverage event \(\{\theta_{\widehat d_m}(P)\in I_B\}\) equals \(\{\tau\in I_B\}\); the complement of \(\mathsf G_{\mathrm{tot}}\) has probability tending to zero.  Hence the coverage conclusion of that theorem gives
\[
 P\{\tau\in I_B\}\longrightarrow1-\eta.
\]
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The corollary translates selected-contrast inference to ATE inference exactly on the common-identification class and inherits every maintained condition of the parent Wald theorem. \textit{Gap.} The equality \(\theta_d(P)=\tau\) is not available on the whole observable or causal regular class. \textit{Consumer.} Causal analysts obtain the exact additional identification restriction required for ATE coverage, without changing the selected estimator or studentizer.

\begin{theorem}[thm:finite-dimensional-selected-path-attainability]\label{thm:finite-dimensional-selected-path-attainability}
Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\).  The full-catalogue pilot \(\mathcal P_m\) is independent of the main study.  Within the pilot and within the main study, the experimental and observational source samples are mutually independent and consist of independent and identically distributed observations from their respective source-conditional laws.  For each \(d\in\mathcal D\), write \(H_d=(S_{3d},S_{2d},A,X)\) and \(L_d=(S_{2d},S_{1d},A,X)\).  Suppose the following fixed-dimensional conditions hold.

For each \((d,a)\), there are known, bounded, finite-dimensional bridge bases \(b^h_{d,a}(H_d)\) and \(b^q_{d,a}(L_d)\), and known bounded test bases \(z^h_{d,a}(L_d)\) and \(z^q_{d,a}(H_d)\), whose dimensions do not depend on \(m\) or \(B\), such that
\[
 h_d(H_d)=b^h_{d,a}(H_d)^\top\beta_{d,a},
 \qquad
 q_d(L_d)=b^q_{d,a}(L_d)^\top\gamma_{d,a}
 \quad\text{on }\{A=a\}.                                    \tag{1}
\]
The square population moment matrices
\[
 M^h_{d,a}=E_P\{z^h_{d,a}(L_d)b^h_{d,a}(H_d)^\top\mid A=a,G=O\},
 \qquad
 M^q_{d,a}=E_P\{z^q_{d,a}(H_d)b^q_{d,a}(L_d)^\top\mid A=a,G=O\} \tag{2}
\]
have smallest singular values bounded below by a constant \(c_0>0\), uniformly over the finite set of \((d,a)\); the corresponding basis Gram matrices have eigenvalues in \([c_0,c_0^{-1}]\).  The exact moment equations are
\[
 M^h_{d,a}\beta_{d,a}
 =E_P\{z^h_{d,a}(L_d)Y\mid A=a,G=O\},
 \qquad
 M^q_{d,a}\gamma_{d,a}
 =E_P\{z^q_{d,a}(H_d)\mid A=a,G=E\}.                         \tag{3}
\]
There are \(\delta>0\) and \(C_0<\infty\) such that all four bases are bounded by \(C_0\), \(E_P(|Y|^{4+\delta}\mid G=O)\le C_0\), and every source and source-arm probability is at least \(c_0\).  Pilot source-arm sizes, main-study source-arm sizes, and every training and evaluation fold size are proportional to \(m\) or \(B\), as appropriate, with fixed \(L\).

On each training sample, form the empirical conditional analogues of (2)--(3) and set
\[
 \widehat\beta_{d,a}=\widehat M_{d,a}^{h,-1}\widehat g^h_{d,a},
 \qquad
 \widehat\gamma_{d,a}=\widehat M_{d,a}^{q,-1}\widehat g^q_{d,a}, \tag{4}
\]
with value zero when a displayed empirical matrix is singular.  Use the resulting fold-external linear bridge fits in the pilot and main study, and use the empirical plug-in source-score second moments specified in Definitions~\(\mathrm{def{:}pilot\text{-}selector}\) and \(\mathrm{def{:}selected\text{-}wald\text{-}variance}\).

Then, uniformly over the finite catalogue and the fixed folds,
\[
 \|\widehat h_{d,B}^{(-\ell)}-h_d\|_{2,B}^{\circ}
 +\|T_d(\widehat h_{d,B}^{(-\ell)}-h_d)\|_{2,O,q,d}
 =O_P(B^{-1/2}),                                               \tag{5}
\]
\[
 \|\widehat q_{d,B}^{(-\ell)}-q_d\|_{2,O,q,d}
 +\|T_d^\star(\widehat q_{d,B}^{(-\ell)}-q_d)\|_{2,B}^{\circ}
 =O_P(B^{-1/2}).                                               \tag{6}
\]
Both weak--strong products are \(O_P(B^{-1})=o_P(B^{-1/2})\), the multiplier expression in Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}postselection\text{-}multiplier\text{-}condition}\) is \(O_P(B^{-1/2})=o_P(1)\), and
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}\max\bigl\{
 |\widehat V_{E,d}-V_{E,d}|,
 |\widehat V_{O,d}-V_{O,d}|\bigr\}=O_P(m^{-1/2}),
 \qquad
 \max\bigl\{
 |\widetilde V_{E,\widehat d_m,B}-V_{E,\widehat d_m}|,
 |\widetilde V_{O,\widehat d_m,B}-V_{O,\widehat d_m}|\bigr\}
 =O_P(B^{-1/2}).                                               \tag{7}
\]
Consequently the observational-norm weak-outcome and strong-selection conditions, the retained artificial-law strong-outcome and weak-selection conditions, and all product, multiplier, pilot-variance, and main-study-variance conditions used by Theorems~\(\mathrm{thm{:}pilot\text{-}oracle\text{-}regret}\) and \(\mathrm{thm{:}postselection\text{-}wald}\) are verified on this fixed-dimensional subclass, with \(r_{V,m}=m^{-1/2}\), \(r_{\widetilde V,B}=B^{-1/2}\), and all four bridge rates of order \(B^{-1/2}\).  Hence the oracle-regret, selected-design limit law, and Wald-coverage conclusions of those theorems hold without separately maintaining those rate conditions on this subclass.  No such conclusion is asserted for all of \(\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
We prove the proposed support-respecting statement.  All stochastic orders below are uniform because \(\mathcal D\), the treatment set, all basis dimensions, and the fold count are finite and fixed.

Fix \((d,a)\), and let \(n\) be the size of a relevant training source-arm sample.  The new sampling premise makes the observations within that source independent and identically distributed.  Bounded bases, \(E_P(|Y|^{4+\delta}\mid G=O)<\infty\), source-arm positivity, and the proportional-size condition therefore give, entry by entry,
\[
 \widehat M^h_{d,a}-M^h_{d,a}=O_P(n^{-1/2}),
 \qquad
 \widehat g^h_{d,a}-g^h_{d,a}=O_P(n^{-1/2}),                 \tag{8}
\]
by Chebyshev's inequality.  The same conclusion holds for \(\widehat M^q_{d,a}-M^q_{d,a}\) and \(\widehat g^q_{d,a}-g^q_{d,a}\).  The latter two estimates may use different sources, but mutual independence of the source samples and the union bound preserve the joint order.  A finite union bound makes (8) simultaneous over designs, arms, and folds.

On the event \(\|\widehat M^h_{d,a}-M^h_{d,a}\|_{\mathrm{op}}<c_0/2\),
\[
 \|\widehat M^h_{d,a}x\|
 \ge \|M^h_{d,a}x\|
      -\|\widehat M^h_{d,a}-M^h_{d,a}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|x\|
 \ge \frac{c_0}{2}\|x\|.                                  \tag{9}
\]
Hence \(\widehat M^h_{d,a}\) is nonsingular and \(\|\widehat M^{h,-1}_{d,a}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le2/c_0\); the same argument applies to \(\widehat M^q_{d,a}\).  These events hold jointly with probability tending to one.  On them, the exact population equations yield
\[
 \widehat\beta_{d,a}-\beta_{d,a}
 =\widehat M_{d,a}^{h,-1}
 \left\{(\widehat g^h_{d,a}-g^h_{d,a})
 -(\widehat M^h_{d,a}-M^h_{d,a})\beta_{d,a}\right\},       \tag{10}
\]
with the analogous identity for \(\widehat\gamma_{d,a}-\gamma_{d,a}\).  The population coefficient vectors are bounded uniformly over the finite collection: the population inverses have norm at most \(c_0^{-1}\), while the outcome and selection right-hand sides are bounded by the moment and basis envelopes.  Consequently,
\[
 \max_{d,a,\ell}\left\{
 \|\widehat\beta_{d,a}^{(-\ell)}-\beta_{d,a}\|
 +\|\widehat\gamma_{d,a}^{(-\ell)}-\gamma_{d,a}\|
 \right\}=O_P(n^{-1/2}).                                    \tag{11}
\]
The stipulated zero value on singular empirical matrices is asymptotically irrelevant.

Bounded bases turn (11) into the pointwise envelopes
\[
 |\widehat h_d-h_d|\le C\|\widehat\beta_d-\beta_d\|,
 \qquad
 |\widehat q_d-q_d|\le C\|\widehat\gamma_d-\gamma_d\|.    \tag{12}
\]
Taking \(n\asymp B\), the first envelope gives
\[
 \|\widehat h_{d,B}^{(-\ell)}-h_d\|_{2,B}^{\circ}
 =O_P(B^{-1/2}).                                               \tag{13}
\]
Conditional expectation is an \(L_2\)-contraction on the observational source, so
\[
 \|T_d(\widehat h_{d,B}^{(-\ell)}-h_d)\|_{2,O,q,d}
 \le \|\widehat h_{d,B}^{(-\ell)}-h_d\|_{2,O,h,d}
 =O_P(B^{-1/2}).                                               \tag{14}
\]
The second envelope directly gives
\[
 \|\widehat q_{d,B}^{(-\ell)}-q_d\|_{2,O,q,d}
 =O_P(B^{-1/2}).                                               \tag{15}
\]
Moreover, every source-defined version of \(T_d^\star(\widehat q_d-q_d)\) is bounded in absolute value by the right-hand envelope in (12), because conditional expectation preserves a pointwise bound.  Therefore
\[
 \|T_d^\star(\widehat q_{d,B}^{(-\ell)}-q_d)\|_{2,B}^{\circ}
 =O_P(B^{-1/2}).                                               \tag{16}
\]
Equations (13)--(16) prove (5)--(6), and either weak--strong product is \(O_P(B^{-1})=o_P(B^{-1/2})\).

Write \(\Delta h_{B,\ell}=\widehat h_{d,B}^{(-\ell)}-h_d\) and \(\Delta q_{B,\ell}=\widehat q_{d,B}^{(-\ell)}-q_d\).  The population bridges are bounded because their bases and coefficients are bounded.  Equations (11)--(12), the finite second moment of \(Y\), and Cauchy--Schwarz imply
\[
 \|\mathbf1\{G=O\}\Delta q_{B,\ell}(Y-h_d)\|_{2,B}^{\circ}
 +\|\mathbf1\{G=O\}q_d\Delta h_{B,\ell}\|_{2,B}^{\circ}
 =O_P(B^{-1/2}),                                               \tag{17}
\]
and
\[
 \|\mathbf1\{G=O\}\Delta q_{B,\ell}\Delta h_{B,\ell}\|_{2,B}^{\circ}
 =O_P(B^{-1}).                                                 \tag{18}
\]
Thus the multiplier expression is \(O_P(B^{-1/2})=o_P(1)\).

We next isolate the experimental centering coordinate omitted by the earlier argument.  For an experimental evaluation sample in arm \(a\), let \(P_{E,a}\) denote population arm-conditional expectation and \(\widehat P_{E,a}\) its empirical analogue.  For every fold-external outcome-bridge fit, the identity
\[
 \widehat\mu_{d,a}^{(-\ell)}-\mu_{d,a}
 =P_{E,a}(\widehat h_d^{(-\ell)}-h_d)
 +(\widehat P_{E,a}-P_{E,a})h_d
 +(\widehat P_{E,a}-P_{E,a})(\widehat h_d^{(-\ell)}-h_d)    \tag{19}
\]
is exact.  The first term is \(O_P(n^{-1/2})\) by (11) and bounded population basis means.  The second is \(O_P(n^{-1/2})\) by independent and identically distributed experimental sampling and boundedness of \(h_d\).  For the third term, write the fitted difference as the bounded basis multiplied by \(\widehat\beta_{d,a}^{(-\ell)}-\beta_{d,a}\).  The coefficient difference is \(O_P(n^{-1/2})\) by (11), and the empirical-minus-population basis mean is \(O_P(n^{-1/2})\).  The outcome-bridge fit is trained on observational data and is independent of the experimental evaluation sample, so their product is \(O_P(n^{-1})\).  Therefore, uniformly over the finite designs, arms, and folds,
\[
 \widehat\mu_{d,a}^{(-\ell)}-\mu_{d,a}=O_P(n^{-1/2}).        \tag{20}
\]
In particular, the load-bearing term \(P_{E,a}(\widehat h-h)\) has not been discarded.

The empirical arm probabilities obey \(\widehat p_{g,a}-p_{g,a}=O_P(n^{-1/2})\), and source-arm positivity makes their inverses stochastically bounded.  For one fold, the experimental score difference has the exact form
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \widehat\phi_{E,d}-\phi_{E,d}
 =\sum_{a=0}^1(-1)^{1-a}\mathbf1\{A=a\}\bigg[&
 \left(\widehat p_{E,a}^{-1}-p_{E,a}^{-1}\right)
 \{h_d(a)-\mu_{d,a}\}\\
 &+\widehat p_{E,a}^{-1}
 \{\Delta h_{d,a}-(\widehat\mu_{d,a}^{(-\ell)}-\mu_{d,a})\}
 \bigg],                                                     \tag{21}
\end{aligned}
\]
where the suppressed arguments agree with the theorem's score definition.  Equations (11)--(12) and (20), together with the arm-probability rate, imply
\[
 \|\widehat\phi_{E,d}-\phi_{E,d}\|_2=O_P(n^{-1/2}).         \tag{22}
\]
Thus the experimental score error is controlled jointly by coefficient error, arm-probability error, and the full three-term centering error.

For the observational score, direct subtraction gives terms proportional to the arm-probability error and to
\[
 \Delta q_d(Y-h_d)-q_d\Delta h_d-\Delta q_d\Delta h_d.       \tag{23}
\]
Boundedness of \(q_d,h_d\), (11)--(12), and \(E_P(Y^2\mid G=O)<\infty\) imply
\[
 \|\widehat\phi_{O,d}-\phi_{O,d}\|_2=O_P(n^{-1/2}).         \tag{24}
\]
The experimental population score is bounded, and the observational population score has a finite fourth moment because \(q_d,h_d\) are bounded and \(E_P(|Y|^{4+\delta}\mid G=O)<\infty\).

Let \(\mathbb P_n\) be an evaluation-source empirical measure.  For either source and any fixed design,
\[
\begin{aligned}
 |\mathbb P_n\widehat\phi_{g,d}^2-P\phi_{g,d}^2|
 \le{}&| (\mathbb P_n-P)\phi_{g,d}^2|\\
 &+\{\mathbb P_n(\widehat\phi_{g,d}-\phi_{g,d})^2\}^{1/2}
   \{\mathbb P_n(\widehat\phi_{g,d}+\phi_{g,d})^2\}^{1/2}. \tag{25}
\end{aligned}
\]
The first term is \(O_P(n^{-1/2})\) by Chebyshev's inequality applied to \(\phi_{g,d}^2\).  Equations (22)--(24), their bounded-basis envelopes, and the empirical second-moment bound for \(Y\) make the second term \(O_P(n^{-1/2})\), even though the empirical arm proportions and centering means are computed from the evaluation sample.  Fixed-fold averaging and a finite union bound preserve the rate.  With \(n\asymp m\) in the pilot and \(n\asymp B\) in the main study, (25) yields
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}\max\bigl\{
 |\widehat V_{E,d}-V_{E,d}|,
 |\widehat V_{O,d}-V_{O,d}|\bigr\}=O_P(m^{-1/2})             \tag{26}
\]
and, first uniformly in \(d\) and hence also at the pilot-selected random design,
\[
 \max_{g\in\{E,O\}}
 |\widetilde V_{g,\widehat d_m,B}-V_{g,\widehat d_m}|
 =O_P(B^{-1/2}).                                               \tag{27}
\]

Equations (13)--(18) and (26)--(27) verify all four bridge rates, both product conditions, the multiplier condition, and both variance-rate conditions with
\[
 r_{V,m}=m^{-1/2},\qquad r_{\widetilde V,B}=B^{-1/2}.
\]
They also verify the associated vanishing conditions.  The pilot is independent of the main study by the newly stated premise, and the two main-study sources have the independent and identically distributed sampling structure required by Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}postselection\text{-}wald}\).  We may therefore apply Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}pilot\text{-}oracle\text{-}regret}\) and Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}postselection\text{-}wald}\), which give the asserted oracle regret, selected-design normal limit, and Wald coverage.  The argument depends on the fixed-dimensional basis, rank, moment, positivity, sampling, and fold-size conditions stated in the proposed theorem, so it does not extend these conclusions to all of \(\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\).
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Provides a primitive bounded finite-dimensional regime in which every maintained bridge, product, multiplier, and variance-rate condition used downstream is derived rather than assumed. \textit{Gap.} The repaired statement now explicitly requires pilot--main independence, mutually independent sources, and independent and identically distributed observations within each source; the proof retains the full three-term experimental centering error. \textit{Consumer.} An analyst using bounded fixed bridge bases and uniformly nonsingular moments can invoke the pilot-regret and selected-design Wald theorems with root-sample-size rates.

\begin{lemma}[lem:clw-population-design-rule]\label{lem:clw-population-design-rule}
Under Assumption~3.1 of Carneiro--Lee--Wilhelm, for a completely randomized treatment with arm-homoskedastic residual variance, Lemma~3.1 and equations~(3.3)--(3.5) show that, under equal treatment allocation, choosing the sample size \(n\) and coefficient support \(\mathcal I(\gamma)\) to minimize the conditional mean-squared error subject to \(c(\mathcal I(\gamma),n)\le B\) is equivalent to minimizing \(\sigma^2(\gamma)/n\) under the same constraint, where \(\sigma^2(\gamma)\) is the residual variance from predicting the outcome by the selected covariates. Their equation~(3.6) is the corresponding pre-experimental empirical squared-error problem.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[thm:sharp-predictive-operator-certificate]\label{thm:sharp-predictive-operator-certificate}
Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\), and abbreviate
\[
 p_d=R_d(P),
 \qquad v_d=\mathcal V_d^*(P).
\]
Define the raw-prediction-order relation and its order-restricted design-value constant by
\[
 \mathcal O_P=\{(d,d')\in\mathcal D^2:p_d\le p_{d'}\},
 \qquad
 C_{\mathrm{ord}}(P)=\max_{(d,d')\in\mathcal O_P}\frac{v_d}{v_{d'}}. \tag{1}
\]
Then \(1\le C_{\mathrm{ord}}(P)<\infty\), and, for every \(C\ge0\),
\[
 \bigl[p_d\le p_{d'}\Longrightarrow v_d\le C v_{d'}
 \text{ for all }d,d'\in\mathcal D\bigr]
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad C\ge C_{\mathrm{ord}}(P). \tag{2}
\]
The smallest constant for the actual best-tie raw prediction-risk rule is
\[
 C_{\mathrm{sel}}(P)
 =\frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)}v_d}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}v_{d'}}
 =\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P),
 \qquad
 1\le C_{\mathrm{sel}}(P)\le C_{\mathrm{ord}}(P)<\infty. \tag{3}
\]
Consequently, for any nonempty subclass \(\mathcal Q\subseteq\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\), a finite constant uniform over \(\mathcal Q\) exists for the best-tie rule if and only if \(\sup_{P\in\mathcal Q}\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)<\infty\); for all pairwise raw-risk-order implications it exists if and only if \(\sup_{P\in\mathcal Q}C_{\mathrm{ord}}(P)<\infty\). Thus (1), rather than an operator norm, is the weakest exact lawwise comparison for selection by raw population prediction risk. The design-specific operators \(T_d\) enter (1) through the bridges and hence through \(v_d\), but no further comparison of the operators is necessary for lawwise finiteness.

More specifically, consider any numerical realization of the handle \(\mathsf H_{\mathrm{comp}}\) with the following properties. For each \(d\), it supplies a real vector space \(\mathcal H_d\), a cone \(\mathcal X_d(P)\subseteq\mathcal H_d\setminus\{0\}\) containing a transported law-specific direction \(x_d(P)\), a positive prediction quadratic form \(Q_{R,d}\) on that cone, and two nonnegative source-score quadratic forms \(Q_{E,d}\) and \(Q_{O,d}\), with
\[
 p_d=Q_{R,d}\{x_d(P)\},
 \qquad v_d=F_d\{x_d(P)\}, \tag{4}
\]
where
\[
 F_d(x)=\left\{\sqrt{c_{E,d}Q_{E,d}(x)}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}Q_{O,d}(x)}\right\}^2. \tag{5}
\]
On the bridge-differentiable submodel class of Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}bridge\text{-}differentiable\text{-}submodel}\), these source scores are gradient components; outside that class, only the source-score interpretation is asserted. Set
\[
 \Lambda_-(P)=\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\inf_{x\in\mathcal X_d(P)}\frac{F_d(x)}{Q_{R,d}(x)},
 \qquad
 \Lambda_+(P)=\max_{d\in\mathcal D}\sup_{x\in\mathcal X_d(P)}\frac{F_d(x)}{Q_{R,d}(x)}. \tag{6}
\]
The weakest two-sided quadratic-form envelope on the chosen cones is precisely
\[
 0<\Lambda_-(P)\le\Lambda_+(P)<\infty, \tag{7}
\]
and it implies the valid, generally conservative certificate
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)\le C_{\mathrm{ord}}(P)\le\frac{\Lambda_+(P)}{\Lambda_-(P)}. \tag{8}
\]

The sharp constant in (2) or (3) is not, in general, a largest generalized eigenvalue. Indeed,
\[
 F_d(x)=\inf_{0<\alpha<1}\left\{
 \frac{c_{E,d}Q_{E,d}(x)}{\alpha}
 +\frac{c_{O,d}Q_{O,d}(x)}{1-\alpha}\right\}, \tag{9}
\]
so the profiled numerator is generally not a quadratic form. If \(\mathcal H_d\) is finite dimensional, \(Q_{R,d}\) is positive definite, and \(\mathcal X_d(P)=\mathcal H_d\setminus\{0\}\), a largest generalized eigenvalue at a fixed \(\alpha\) gives only a one-sided full-space envelope. Selection requires both the upper and lower envelopes in (6), and equality in (8) additionally requires saturation by law-realized, raw-risk-ordered directions. Hence equality with a largest generalized eigenvalue can hold only after adding a common-space representation, a normalization of the positive lower envelope, and the required saturation; none follows from observable-law operator bijectivity or from the raw-risk ordering alone.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Proof.}
Write \(p_d=R_d(P)\) and \(v_d=\mathcal V_d^*(P)\).  The family \(\mathcal D\) is finite and nonempty.  Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}predictive\text{-}benchmark}\) gives \(0\le p_d<\infty\).  By Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}nondegenerate\text{-}components}\), the costs are positive and \(0<V_{E,d},V_{O,d}<\infty\).  The profile formula in Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}budget\text{-}profile}\) therefore gives
\[
 0<v_d=
 \left(\sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}+
       \sqrt{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}}\right)^2<\infty .             \tag{10}
\]
These are the support and boundary facts needed below: all denominators involving \(v_d\) are strictly positive, every displayed finite-set minimum or maximum is attained, and \(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)\) is nonempty.

The order set \(\mathcal O_P\) is finite and nonempty because it contains every diagonal pair \((d,d)\).  Every ratio \(v_d/v_{d'}\) on it is positive and finite, while each diagonal ratio is one.  Thus
\[
 1\le C_{\mathrm{ord}}(P)<\infty .                         \tag{11}
\]
For \(C\ge0\), the implication in equation~\((2)\) holds for all \(d,d'\) exactly when
\[
 \frac{v_d}{v_{d'}}\le C
 \quad\text{for every }(d,d')\in\mathcal O_P,             \tag{12}
\]
where division is legitimate by equation~\((10)\).  Since the index set in equation~\((12)\) is finite, equation~\((12)\) is equivalent to \(C_{\mathrm{ord}}(P)\le C\).  This proves equations~\((1)\)--\((2)\) and the claimed sharpness of the pairwise constant.

Let \(d_{\mathrm{sel}}\) minimize \(v_d\) over \(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)\), and let \(d_*\) minimize \(v_d\) over \(\mathcal D\).  The best-tie selector has the exact multiplicative loss
\[
 \frac{v_{d_{\mathrm{sel}}}}{v_{d_*}}
 =\frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)}v_d}
        {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}v_{d'}}
 =\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P).                                \tag{13}
\]
Consequently the least \(C\) bounding the selected value by \(C\min_{d'}v_{d'}\) is the ratio in equation~\((13)\) itself.  Since \(d_*\) is globally optimal, this ratio is at least one.  Since \(d_{\mathrm{sel}}\) minimizes prediction risk whereas \(d_*\) is merely an admissible design,
\[
 p_{d_{\mathrm{sel}}}\le p_{d_*};
\]
hence \((d_{\mathrm{sel}},d_*)\in\mathcal O_P\), and
\[
 1\le C_{\mathrm{sel}}(P)=\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)
 =\frac{v_{d_{\mathrm{sel}}}}{v_{d_*}}
 \le C_{\mathrm{ord}}(P).                                 \tag{14}
\]
Together with equation~\((11)\), this proves equation~\((3)\).

Now let \(\mathcal Q\) be nonempty.  If one finite constant works for the best-tie rule at every \(P\in\mathcal Q\), equation~\((13)\) shows that the supremum of \(\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)\) over \(\mathcal Q\) is bounded by that constant.  Conversely, if that supremum is finite, choosing it as \(C\) gives the uniform bound.  The identical argument using the exact equivalence in equation~\((12)\) proves the pairwise assertion with \(C_{\mathrm{ord}}(P)\).  This establishes the two uniform if-and-only-if statements.  It also shows why \(C_{\mathrm{ord}}(P)\), which uses only the realized prediction ordering and realized values, is the weakest exact lawwise pairwise comparison.  The operators \(T_d\) affect those realized values through the design-specific bridges, but finiteness in equation~\((11)\) needs no comparison between different operators.

We next prove the quadratic-form certificate.  For any numerical realization specified in the theorem, put
\[
 r_d(x)=\frac{F_d(x)}{Q_{R,d}(x)},
 \qquad x\in\mathcal X_d(P).                              \tag{15}
\]
The denominator is strictly positive by the stipulated positivity of \(Q_{R,d}\) on the nonzero cone.  By the definitions in equation~\((6)\), \(\Lambda_-(P)\) is the greatest constant \(L\) such that
\[
 LQ_{R,d}(x)\le F_d(x)
 \quad\text{for every }d\in\mathcal D,
 \ x\in\mathcal X_d(P),                                  \tag{16}
\]
and \(\Lambda_+(P)\) is the least constant \(U\) such that
\[
 F_d(x)\le UQ_{R,d}(x)
 \quad\text{for every }d\in\mathcal D,
 \ x\in\mathcal X_d(P).                                  \tag{17}
\]
Indeed, equations~\((16)\)--\((17)\) are respectively equivalent to \(L\le\inf_xr_d(x)\) for every \(d\), and to \(U\ge\sup_xr_d(x)\) for every \(d\).  Thus a strictly positive finite two-sided envelope exists if and only if
\[
 0<\Lambda_-(P)\le\Lambda_+(P)<\infty,                   \tag{18}
\]
and the two endpoints in equation~\((18)\) are its sharp global lower and upper coefficients on the chosen cones.  This proves the asserted ``weakest envelope'' characterization.

Apply equations~\((16)\)--\((17)\) to the transported directions \(x_d(P)\).  Equations~\((4)\) and~\((18)\) yield, for every \(d\),
\[
 \Lambda_-(P)p_d\le v_d\le\Lambda_+(P)p_d.               \tag{19}
\]
In particular, equation~\((19)\) also forces every \(p_d\) in this numerical realization to be positive.  If \((d,d')\in\mathcal O_P\), then
\[
 \frac{v_d}{v_{d'}}
 \le
 \frac{\Lambda_+(P)p_d}{\Lambda_-(P)p_{d'}}
 \le \frac{\Lambda_+(P)}{\Lambda_-(P)}.                  \tag{20}
\]
Taking the maximum over \(\mathcal O_P\), and combining the result with equation~\((14)\), proves equation~\((8)\).

It remains to justify the profiling and eigenvalue assertions.  For fixed \(d\) and \(x\), set
\[
 a=c_{E,d}Q_{E,d}(x),\qquad b=c_{O,d}Q_{O,d}(x),
\]
so \(a,b\ge0\).  For every \(0<\alpha<1\), direct expansion gives
\[
 \frac a\alpha+\frac b{1-\alpha}-(\sqrt a+\sqrt b)^2
 =\frac{\{(1-\alpha)\sqrt a-\alpha\sqrt b\}^2}
        {\alpha(1-\alpha)}\ge0.                           \tag{21}
\]
If \(a,b>0\), equality occurs at
\(
 \alpha=\sqrt a/(\sqrt a+\sqrt b)
\).
If \(a=0<b\), or \(b=0<a\), the lower bound is approached as \(\alpha\downarrow0\), or \(\alpha\uparrow1\), respectively; if \(a=b=0\), equality holds for every \(\alpha\).  Taking the infimum in equation~\((21)\) proves equation~\((9)\) in every boundary case.

The profiled map need not itself be quadratic.  For example, on \(\mathbb R^2\), take unit costs,
\[
 Q_E(x_1,x_2)=x_1^2,
 \qquad Q_O(x_1,x_2)=x_2^2.
\]
Then
\[
 F(x_1,x_2)=(|x_1|+|x_2|)^2.
\]
For the coordinate vectors \(e_1,e_2\),
\[
 F(e_1+e_2)+F(e_1-e_2)=8
 \ne4=2F(e_1)+2F(e_2),                                   \tag{22}
\]
contradicting the parallelogram identity satisfied by every quadratic form.  Thus a generalized-eigenvalue representation is unavailable in general.

Even in the finite-dimensional full-space case, at a fixed \(\alpha\) the quadratic numerator
\[
 G_{d,\alpha}(x)=
 \frac{c_{E,d}Q_{E,d}(x)}\alpha+
 \frac{c_{O,d}Q_{O,d}(x)}{1-\alpha}
\]
satisfies \(F_d(x)\le G_{d,\alpha}(x)\) by equation~\((9)\).  Hence the largest generalized Rayleigh quotient of \(G_{d,\alpha}\) relative to positive-definite \(Q_{R,d}\) supplies an upper full-space envelope only.  It supplies neither the strictly positive lower coefficient in equation~\((16)\) nor equality in the two successive inequalities of equation~\((20)\).  Equality in equation~\((20)\) requires a law-realized ordered pair whose numerator direction attains \(\Lambda_+(P)\), whose denominator direction attains \(\Lambda_-(P)\), and whose prediction risks saturate the ordering.  Comparing such directions by one eigenproblem also requires placing the design-specific forms on a common space and normalizing by the positive lower envelope.  Bijectivity of each \(T_d\) gives none of these cross-design metric or attainment properties, and the scalar ordering \(p_d\le p_{d'}\) gives none either.  This proves the final assertions. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Gives exact finite-catalogue constants for raw-risk ordering and separates them from conservative quadratic-form envelopes. \textit{Gap.} A generalized eigenvalue is neither automatically sharp nor sufficient after budget profiling. \textit{Consumer.} Analysts can compute exact lawwise constants by finite enumeration and audit any proposed operator envelope.

\begin{theorem}[thm:treatment-aware-predictive-operator-certificate]\label{thm:treatment-aware-predictive-operator-certificate}
Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\). For either positive design-value profile
\[
 w_d=\mathcal V_d^*(P)
 \quad\text{or}\quad
 w_d=\mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P),
\]
the least constant making every treatment-aware prediction-order implication
\[
 R_d^A(P)\le R_{d'}^A(P)\Longrightarrow w_d\le Cw_{d'}
 \qquad(d,d'\in\mathcal D)
\]
valid is
\[
 \max_{\substack{d,d'\in\mathcal D:\,R_d^A(P)\le R_{d'}^A(P)}}
 \frac{w_d}{w_{d'}}. \tag{1}
\]
For the arm-normalized profile, the smallest constant for the actual best-tie selector is \(\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)\); for the IKM-normalized profile it is \(\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{A,\mathrm{IKM}}(P)\). Each is at most (1) for its profile and is finite at a fixed regular law. Consequently, a uniform constant over a nonempty law class exists exactly when the supremum of the corresponding best-tie ratio is finite.

If a numerical realization of \(\mathsf H_{\mathrm{comp}}\) represents \(R_d^A(P)\) by a positive quadratic form \(Q_{R,d}^A\{x_d(P)\}\) and represents the arm-normalized value by the profiled source-score form \(F_d\{x_d(P)\}\), then the two-sided envelope
\[
 0<\Lambda_-^A(P):=\min_d\inf_x\frac{F_d(x)}{Q_{R,d}^A(x)}
 \le
 \max_d\sup_x\frac{F_d(x)}{Q_{R,d}^A(x)}=:\Lambda_+^A(P)<\infty
\]
implies
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)
 \le\max_{\substack{d,d':\,R_d^A(P)\le R_{d'}^A(P)}}
 \frac{\mathcal V_d^*(P)}{\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)}
 \le\frac{\Lambda_+^A(P)}{\Lambda_-^A(P)}. \tag{2}
\]
As for the unadjusted rule, this envelope is generally conservative and the sharp constant is not generally a largest generalized eigenvalue, because profiling the two source terms produces a nonquadratic numerator and equality additionally requires saturation by law-realized, treatment-aware-risk-ordered directions.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Proof.}
Fix one of the two profiles in the statement and denote it by \(w_d\).  For the arm-normalized choice, positivity and finiteness follow from Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}nondegenerate\text{-}components}\), positive costs, and Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}budget\text{-}profile}\).  For the IKM-normalized choice, Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}ikmw\text{-}normalized\text{-}design\text{-}value}\) gives
\[
 V_{g,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=(1-p_{g,1})^2V_{g,d}.
\]
Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}source\text{-}arm\text{-}positivity}\) makes each factor \(1-p_{g,1}\) strictly positive, and Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}nondegenerate\text{-}components}\) makes \(V_{g,d}\) finite and strictly positive.  Consequently
\[
 0<w_d<\infty
 \qquad\text{for every }d\in\mathcal D                 \tag{10}
\]
under either profile.  The finite nonempty family \(\mathcal D\) therefore has nonempty minimizer sets, and every ratio below is well-defined.

Put \(r_d=R_d^A(P)\) and
\[
 \mathcal O_P^A=\{(d,d')\in\mathcal D^2:r_d\le r_{d'}\}.
\]
This set is finite and contains all diagonal pairs.  For \(C\ge0\), all treatment-aware order implications hold exactly when
\[
 \frac{w_d}{w_{d'}}\le C
 \qquad\text{for every }(d,d')\in\mathcal O_P^A.          \tag{11}
\]
By finiteness, equation~\((11)\) is equivalent to
\[
 C\ge
 \max_{(d,d')\in\mathcal O_P^A}\frac{w_d}{w_{d'}},       \tag{12}
\]
which proves equation~\((1)\) and its least-constant assertion.

Choose \(d_{\mathrm{sel}}\) to minimize \(w_d\) over the treatment-aware prediction minimizers \(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)\), and choose \(d_*\) to minimize \(w_d\) over all of \(\mathcal D\).  Then
\[
 r_{d_{\mathrm{sel}}}\le r_{d_*},
 \qquad
 1\le\frac{w_{d_{\mathrm{sel}}}}{w_{d_*}}
 \le \max_{(d,d')\in\mathcal O_P^A}\frac{w_d}{w_{d'}}.  \tag{13}
\]
For \(w_d=\mathcal V_d^*(P)\), the middle ratio in equation~\((13)\) is exactly \(\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)\) by Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}predictive\text{-}benchmark}\).  For \(w_d=\mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)\), it is exactly \(\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{A,\mathrm{IKM}}(P)\) by Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}ikmw\text{-}normalized\text{-}design\text{-}value}\).  Thus each named ratio is finite at the fixed law and is bounded by equation~\((1)\).  Over a nonempty law class, any finite uniform constant bounds the corresponding exact ratio; conversely, the finite supremum of those ratios is itself a uniform constant.  This proves the stated uniform equivalence.

For the numerical envelope, define
\[
 s_d(x)=\frac{F_d(x)}{Q_{R,d}^A(x)}.
\]
Positivity of the prediction form on the chosen nonzero cone makes the denominator positive.  The definitions of \(\Lambda_-^A(P)\) and \(\Lambda_+^A(P)\) therefore imply
\[
 \Lambda_-^A(P)Q_{R,d}^A(x)
 \le F_d(x)
 \le\Lambda_+^A(P)Q_{R,d}^A(x)                          \tag{14}
\]
for every represented \(d\) and every cone direction \(x\).  Evaluating equation~\((14)\) at \(x_d(P)\) gives
\[
 \Lambda_-^A(P)R_d^A(P)
 \le\mathcal V_d^*(P)
 \le\Lambda_+^A(P)R_d^A(P).                              \tag{15}
\]
If \(R_d^A(P)\le R_{d'}^A(P)\), then equation~\((15)\) yields
\[
 \frac{\mathcal V_d^*(P)}{\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)}
 \le
 \frac{\Lambda_+^A(P)R_d^A(P)}
      {\Lambda_-^A(P)R_{d'}^A(P)}
 \le\frac{\Lambda_+^A(P)}{\Lambda_-^A(P)}.              \tag{16}
\]
Taking the maximum over the treatment-aware order relation and using equation~\((13)\) proves equation~\((2)\).

Finally, Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}sharp\text{-}predictive\text{-}operator\text{-}certificate}\) proves algebraically that profiling the two source-score quadratic forms produces
\[
 F_d(x)=\inf_{0<\alpha<1}
 \left\{
 \frac{c_{E,d}Q_{E,d}(x)}\alpha+
 \frac{c_{O,d}Q_{O,d}(x)}{1-\alpha}
 \right\},                                                \tag{17}
\]
which is not a quadratic form in general.  Its explicit parallelogram-law counterexample applies unchanged after replacing the prediction denominator by \(Q_{R,d}^A\).  Moreover, equality in equation~\((16)\) requires simultaneous attainment of the upper and lower envelope endpoints by law-realized directions and saturation of the treatment-aware risk order.  A largest generalized eigenvalue at fixed \(\alpha\) supplies only an upper full-space quadratic envelope and does not supply these conditions.  Hence the two-sided certificate can be conservative and the sharp treatment-aware selection constant is not generally a largest generalized eigenvalue. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This theorem gives the exact order-restricted constant for treatment-aware prediction and the valid two-sided quadratic-form certificate for the arm-normalized profile. \textit{Gap.} A largest generalized eigenvalue is insufficient because design profiling is nonquadratic and selection requires a positive lower envelope plus saturation. \textit{Consumer.} It characterizes precisely what additional law-class comparison would be required to rescue a finite treatment-aware prediction guarantee.

\begin{lemma}[lem:binary-witness-stability]\label{lem:binary-witness-stability}
Fix \(\chi>1\) and \(B>0\). Every center \(P_\epsilon\), \(0<\epsilon<10^{-3}\), belongs to \(\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}\cap\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{CLW}}\), both bridge contrasts equal \(\tau=0\),
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P_\epsilon;B)
 =\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_\epsilon)=\{d_{\mathrm p}\},
 \qquad D^*(P_\epsilon)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\},               \tag{1}
\]
and the paired costs and named criteria satisfy
\[
 C_{d_{\mathrm p}}=5\chi,
 \qquad C_{d_{\mathrm e}}=5,
\]
\[
 B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm p}}(P_\epsilon;B)
 =\frac32\frac{1-\epsilon^2}{1-\epsilon^2y_\chi^2}
 <\frac32<3
 =B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm e}}(P_\epsilon;B).      \tag{2}
\]
The variance values and fixed-\(\chi\) expansion are
\[
 V_{O,d_{\mathrm p}}
 =\frac{64}{75(10\chi-3)}\epsilon^{-2}
 +\frac{896}{75(10\chi-3)},
 \qquad
 \mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm e}}^*(P_\epsilon)=\frac{3872}{25},
\]
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P_\epsilon;B)
 =\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_\epsilon)
 =\frac{5\chi+1}{363(10\chi-3)}\epsilon^{-2}
 +O_\chi(\epsilon^{-1}).                                  \tag{3}
\]
For each fixed \((\chi,\epsilon)\), there is \(r_\epsilon>0\) such that every \(P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{bin}}(\chi)\) with \(\|P-P_\epsilon\|_{\mathrm{cell}}<r_\epsilon\) belongs to \(\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}\cap\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{CLW}}\), has both bridge contrasts equal to \(\tau\), and satisfies
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)
 =\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)=\{d_{\mathrm p}\},
 \qquad D^*(P)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\},                         \tag{4}
\]
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)>\frac12\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P_\epsilon;B),
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)>\frac12\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_\epsilon). \tag{5}
\]
For every fixed law in this ball, there is a finite \(B_0(P)\) such that
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)=\{d_{\mathrm p}\}
 \quad\text{for every }B\ge B_0(P).                       \tag{6}
\]
No equality between \(r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)\) and \(R_d(P)\) is asserted away from the centers.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
All center assertions follow from Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\).  We prove the neighborhood claims while keeping the CLW affine residual and unrestricted risk distinct away from the centers.

The center is in the relative interior of the finite-cell parameterization.  Strict cell positivity, source and treatment overlap, and nonzero determinants of the finite completeness and bridge-operator matrices are open conditions.  Matrix inversion is continuous away from zero determinant.  The constrained factorization continues to impose consistency, experimental randomization, latent observational exchangeability, source-invariant proxy kernels, source support, and conditional proxy independence.  It therefore preserves all causal and sequential restrictions on a sufficiently small relative ball.  Bridge coefficients, source-score moments, and positive profiles are rational functions of positive cell probabilities and nonsingular finite matrices, so they are continuous.  A further shrinking preserves finite positive component variances.

Common identification is structural in this constrained family.  Because \(Y(1)=Y(0)=U\), \(\tau=0\).  For each arm, the bridge equation, sequential restriction, and early-proxy completeness imply
\[
 \mathbb E_P\{h_d(S_{3d},a)\mid U,A=a,G=O\}
 =\mathbb E_P(Y\mid U,A=a,G=O)=U.
\]
Source invariance and experimental randomization then yield
\[
 \mu_{d,a}
 =\mathbb E_P\{h_d(S_{3d},a)\mid A=a,G=E\}
 =\mathbb E_P(U),
\]
independently of \(a\).  Hence \(\theta_d(P)=0=\tau\) throughout the ball.

Every entry of
\[
 \Gamma_d(P)=\mathbb E_P(\xi_d\xi_d^\top\mid G=O),
 \qquad g_d(P)=\mathbb E_P(\xi_dY\mid G=O)
\]
is continuous in the cell vector.  The full determinant remains nonzero after shrinking, and
\[
 r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)=
 \mathbb E_P(Y^2\mid G=O)
 -g_d(P)^\top\Gamma_d(P)^{-1}g_d(P)                       \tag{32}
\]
is continuous.  Separately,
\[
 R_d(P)=\mathbb E_P\{\operatorname{Var}_P(Y\mid X,Z_{M_d},G=O)\mid G=O\}
\]
is a finite sum of cell masses and conditional-moment ratios with positive denominators, hence continuous without being equated to (32).

The named CLW, raw-risk, and bridge-value gaps are strict at the center.  Finiteness of \(\mathcal D\) permits one common radius preserving all three orderings, and continuity of their positive ratio denominators permits a further shrinking that proves the two one-half bounds.

The same shrinking may impose the stronger, budget-free margin
\[
 2C_{d_{\mathrm p}}r_{d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)
 <C_{d_{\mathrm e}}r_{d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P),   \tag{33}
\]
because at the center its left side is strictly less than \(3\), while its right side equals \(3\).  For every law in this smaller ball and every \(B\ge C_{d_{\mathrm p}}=5\chi\), the elementary floor inequalities used in the sharp-elbow proof imply
\[
\begin{aligned}
 B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)
 &<2C_{d_{\mathrm p}}r_{d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)\\
 &<C_{d_{\mathrm e}}r_{d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)
 \le B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B).
\end{aligned}                                               \tag{34}
\]
Thus the exact integer selector is \(\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\) for every \(B\ge5\chi\).  In particular, the weaker statement follows with \(B_0(P)=5\chi\).  This proves every asserted conclusion.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Continuity on the constrained finite-cell model turns the center separation into a relatively open regular common-identification obstruction. \textit{Gap.} The stability is relative to the factorized finite-cell model and does not assert ambient-simplex openness. \textit{Consumer.} It certifies that the failure is not confined to an isolated law.

\begin{lemma}[lem:sharp-uniform-binary-elbow]\label{lem:sharp-uniform-binary-elbow}
Fix \(\chi>1\), \(0<\epsilon<10^{-3}\), and \(B>0\), and write \(P_{\chi,\epsilon}=P_\epsilon\). For a binary center design with early and late signals \(x,y\in(0,1)\), the nonredundant affine regressor is \(\xi_d=(1,W_x,W_y)^\top\), its full Gram matrix and determinant are
\[
 \Gamma_d(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=
 \begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\0&1&xy\\0&xy&1\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 \det\Gamma_d(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=1-x^2y^2>0,              \tag{1}
\]
and its population affine projection has intercept zero and coefficients
\[
 \beta_1=\frac{x(1-y^2)}{1-x^2y^2},
 \qquad
 \beta_2=\frac{y(1-x^2)}{1-x^2y^2}.                       \tag{2}
\]
Consequently the distinct CLW affine residual and the unrestricted unadjusted risk agree at the center, with
\[
 r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =R_d(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =R(x,y)=\frac{(1-x^2)(1-y^2)}{1-x^2y^2}.                 \tag{3}
\]
Thus \(P_{\chi,\epsilon}\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}\cap\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{CLW}}\), both bridge contrasts equal \(\tau=0\), and
\[
 B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm p}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\frac32\frac{1-\epsilon^2}{1-\epsilon^2(1-3/(10\chi))}
 <\frac32<3
 =B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm e}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B). \tag{4}
\]
At the same centers,
\[
 R_{d_{\mathrm p}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\frac{3}{10\chi}\frac{1-\epsilon^2}
 {1-\epsilon^2(1-3/(10\chi))}
 <\frac{3}{10\chi}<\frac3{10}<\frac35
 =R_{d_{\mathrm e}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon}).                  \tag{5}
\]
Hence
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\{d_{\mathrm p}\},
 \qquad
 D^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=\{d_{\mathrm e}\}.              \tag{6}
\]
There is a finite \(B_0(P_{\chi,\epsilon})\) such that
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\{d_{\mathrm p}\}
 \quad\text{for every }B\ge B_0(P_{\chi,\epsilon}).      \tag{7}
\]
The selected design is costlier in each source:
\[
 c_{E,d_{\mathrm p}}-c_{E,d_{\mathrm e}}
 =c_{O,d_{\mathrm p}}-c_{O,d_{\mathrm e}}
 =\frac{5(\chi-1)}2>0.
\]
Moreover,
\[
 V_{E,d_{\mathrm p}}=\frac{40\chi}{10\chi-3},
 \qquad
 V_{O,d_{\mathrm p}}
 =\frac{64}{75(10\chi-3)}(\epsilon^{-2}+14),
 \qquad
 \mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm e}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=\frac{3872}{25}.
\]
All four named-CLW and unadjusted-risk center ratios agree:
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 &=\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})\\
 &=\frac{25}{3872}
 \left\{
 \sqrt{\frac{20\chi(5\chi-1)}{10\chi-3}}
 +\sqrt{\frac{32(5\chi+1)}{75(10\chi-3)}
 (\epsilon^{-2}+14)}
 \right\}^{2}.                                            \tag{8}
\end{aligned}
\]
Every ratio in (8) exceeds \((\epsilon^{-2}+14)/726\). For all sufficiently small \(\epsilon\), its infimum over \(\chi>1\) is attained; every minimizer satisfies
\[
 \chi_{\mathrm{opt}}(\epsilon)
 \sim\frac{\sqrt[3]{18}}{15}\epsilon^{-2/3},             \tag{9}
\]
and the sharp optimized elbow is
\[
 \epsilon^2\inf_{\chi>1}\rho(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\frac1{726}+\frac{5\sqrt[3]{12}}{968}\epsilon^{2/3}
 +o(\epsilon^{2/3}),                                      \tag{10}
\]
where \(\rho\) is any of the four center ratios in (8).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}clw\text{-}population\text{-}design\text{-}rule}\), the transported balanced-treatment population criterion is affine-linear residual variance divided by sample size.  Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}clw\text{-}linear\text{-}cost\text{-}selector}\) maps support only to \(M_d\) and imposes \(c(M_d,n)=nC_d\), so its continuous value is \(C_dr_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)/B\).

Because \(X\) is constant, \(X_{\mathrm{nr}}\) is zero-dimensional and \(\xi_d=(1,W_x,W_y)^\top\).  Symmetry and conditional independence given \(U\) yield
\[
 \mathbb E(U)=\mathbb E(W_x)=\mathbb E(W_y)=0,
 \quad \mathbb E(UW_x)=x,
 \quad \mathbb E(UW_y)=y,
 \quad \mathbb E(W_xW_y)=xy.                              \tag{11}
\]
Thus
\[
 \Gamma_d(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=
 \begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\0&1&xy\\0&xy&1\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 \det\Gamma_d(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=1-x^2y^2>0,
 \qquad
 g_d(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=(0,x,y)^\top.                    \tag{12}
\]
Inverting the lower \(2\)-by-\(2\) block gives an intercept of zero and
\[
 \beta_1=\frac{x(1-y^2)}{1-x^2y^2},
 \qquad
 \beta_2=\frac{y(1-x^2)}{1-x^2y^2}.                       \tag{13}
\]
The projection identity gives
\[
 r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =1-(x,y)
 \begin{pmatrix}1&xy\\xy&1\end{pmatrix}^{-1}
 \begin{pmatrix}x\\y\end{pmatrix}
 =\frac{(1-x^2)(1-y^2)}{1-x^2y^2}.                        \tag{14}
\]
For \(r,s\in\{-1,1\}\), direct summation over \(U\) gives
\[
 P(W_x=r,W_y=s\mid G=O)=\frac{1+xyrs}{4},
 \qquad
 \mathbb E(U\mid W_x=r,W_y=s,G=O)=\frac{xr+ys}{1+xyrs}.
\]
Since \(U^2=1\), conditional-mean projection therefore gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
 R_d(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 &=1-\frac12\left\{
 \frac{(x+y)^2}{1+xy}+\frac{(x-y)^2}{1-xy}\right\}\\
 &=\frac{(1-x^2)(1-y^2)}{1-x^2y^2}
 =r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon}).                 \tag{15}
\end{aligned}
\]
This equality has been proved only at the symmetric centers.  Moreover, \(Y(1)=Y(0)=U\) and \(Z(1)=Z(0)\), so the transported affine residual distribution is the same in both randomized arms.  Finite support gives the moment conditions, and (12) gives full-Gram CLW admissibility.

We verify causal regularity separately.  Every latent center cell is strictly positive.  Consistency is built into the potential variables.  In source \(E\), \(T\) is independent of \((U,Z(0),Z(1),Y(0),Y(1))\); in source \(O\), conditioning on \(U\) makes treatment independent of the potential variables, and the treatment probabilities lie in \([3/8,5/8]\).  The law of \((U,Z(0),Z(1),X)\) is source-invariant, all required source-support ratios are finite, and conditional proxy independence given \(U\) proves the sequential-outcome restriction.  For signals \(x,y\ne0\), the binary matrices in the early- and late-proxy completeness conditions have nonzero determinants proportional to \(x(1-1/16)\) and \(y(1-1/16)\); the matrix representing \(T_d\) has determinant proportional to \(xy(1-1/16)\).  Hence both completeness restrictions and bijectivity hold.

The bridge candidates in Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}binary\text{-}witness}\) solve their equations.  Conditional proxy independence gives
\[
 \mathbb E(W_y/y\mid W_x,A,G=O)
 =\mathbb E(U\mid W_x,A,G=O)=\mathbb E(Y\mid W_x,A,G=O).
\]
Writing \(t=2a-1\), Bayes' rule gives
\[
 \mathbb E(W_x\mid W_y=l,T=t,G=O)
 =x\frac{t/4+yl}{1+tyl/4},
\]
and hence
\[
 \mathbb E\!\left[
 \frac{16}{15}\left(1-\frac{tW_x}{4x}\right)
 \mathrel{\Big|}W_y=l,T=t,G=O\right]
 =\frac1{1+tyl/4}.                                        \tag{16}
\]
The last expression is the experimental-to-observational density ratio for \(W_y\) within arm \(a\), proving selection-bridge existence.  The explicit moments below are finite and positive.  Thus the center belongs to \(\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}\cap\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{CLW}}\).  Finally, experimental randomization and \(\mathbb E(U)=0\) give \(\mu_{d,a}=0\) for both arms, while \(Y(1)-Y(0)=0\), so \(\theta_d(P)=\tau=0\).

Substitute \((x,y)=(\epsilon,y_\chi)\) for \(d_{\mathrm p}\) and \((x,y)=(1/2,1/2)\) for \(d_{\mathrm e}\).  Equations (14)--(15) give
\[
 r_{d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{lin}}=R_{d_{\mathrm p}}
 =\frac{3}{10\chi}
 \frac{1-\epsilon^2}{1-\epsilon^2(1-3/(10\chi))},
 \qquad
 r_{d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{lin}}=R_{d_{\mathrm e}}=\frac35. \tag{17}
\]
Since \(C_{d_{\mathrm p}}=5\chi\) and \(C_{d_{\mathrm e}}=5\),
\[
 B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm p}}
 =\frac32\frac{1-\epsilon^2}{1-\epsilon^2y_\chi^2}
 <\frac32<3=B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm e}}.           \tag{18}
\]
The inequality is strict because \(0<y_\chi<1\).  Equation (17) also gives the risk chain in the statement, so both continuous selectors choose \(d_{\mathrm p}\).

The integer conclusion is stronger than the stated eventual result.  For every \(z\ge1\), with \(k=\lfloor z\rfloor\ge1\),
\[
 z<k+1\le2k,
 \qquad\text{hence}\qquad \frac z{\lfloor z\rfloor}<2,   \tag{19}
\]
while \(\lfloor z\rfloor\le z\) implies \(z/\lfloor z\rfloor\ge1\).  If \(B\ge C_{d_{\mathrm p}}=5\chi\), both integer counts are positive and (17)--(19) imply
\[
\begin{aligned}
 B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{int}}
 &<2C_{d_{\mathrm p}}r_{d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{lin}}<3,\\
 B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{int}}
 &\ge C_{d_{\mathrm e}}r_{d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{lin}}=3.
\end{aligned}                                               \tag{20}
\]
Thus \(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}=\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\) for every \(B\ge5\chi\), and the statement's threshold may be taken as \(B_0(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=5\chi\).  The primitive costs also give
\[
 c_{E,d_{\mathrm p}}-c_{E,d_{\mathrm e}}
 =c_{O,d_{\mathrm p}}-c_{O,d_{\mathrm e}}
 =\frac{5(\chi-1)}2>0.                                    \tag{21}
\]

For the source-score moments, write \(t=2a-1\).  Since \(h_d(W_y,a)=W_y/y\), \(\mu_{d,a}=0\), and \(p_{g,a}=1/2\),
\[
 \phi_{E,d}=\frac{2TW_y}{y},
 \qquad V_{E,d}=\frac4{y^2}.                              \tag{22}
\]
The observational score is
\[
 \phi_{O,d}=2T\frac{16}{15}
 \left(1-\frac{TW_x}{4x}\right)
 \left(U-\frac{W_y}{y}\right).
\]
Conditional on \(U\), \((T,W_x)\) is independent of \(W_y\), and
\[
 \mathbb E\!\left[\left(1-\frac{TW_x}{4x}\right)^2\middle|U\right]
 =\frac{1+14x^2}{16x^2},
 \qquad
 \mathbb E\!\left[\left(U-\frac{W_y}{y}\right)^2\middle|U\right]
 =\frac{1-y^2}{y^2}.
\]
Therefore
\[
 V_{O,d}=\frac{64(1-y^2)(1+14x^2)}{225x^2y^2}.             \tag{23}
\]
At the two designs,
\[
 V_{E,d_{\mathrm p}}=\frac{40\chi}{10\chi-3},
 \qquad
 V_{O,d_{\mathrm p}}=
 \frac{64}{75(10\chi-3)}(\epsilon^{-2}+14),              \tag{24}
\]
\[
 V_{E,d_{\mathrm e}}=16,
 \qquad V_{O,d_{\mathrm e}}=\frac{384}{25}.
\]
Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}budget\text{-}profile}\) now gives
\[
 \mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm e}}^*
 =\left(\sqrt{2\cdot16}+\sqrt{3\cdot384/25}\right)^2
 =\frac{3872}{25},                                        \tag{25}
\]
and
\[
 \frac{\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm p}}^*}{\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm e}}^*}
 =\frac{25}{3872}
 \left\{
 \sqrt{\frac{20\chi(5\chi-1)}{10\chi-3}}
 +\sqrt{\frac{32(5\chi+1)}{75(10\chi-3)}
 (\epsilon^{-2}+14)}
 \right\}^{2}.                                            \tag{26}
\]
Dropping the first nonnegative square-root term yields
\[
 \frac{\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm p}}^*}{\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm e}}^*}
 \ge\frac{5\chi+1}{363(10\chi-3)}(\epsilon^{-2}+14)
 >\frac{\epsilon^{-2}+14}{726}>1.                         \tag{27}
\]
Hence \(D^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=\{d_{\mathrm e}\}\), and the named continuous and raw-risk ratios equal (26).

At every center \(p_{E,1}=p_{O,1}=1/2\).  Thus
\[
 V_{g,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac14V_{g,d},
 \qquad
 \mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*=\frac14\mathcal V_d^*,
 \qquad g\in\{E,O\}.                                     \tag{28}
\]
The common factor preserves the optimizer and cancels from all best-tie ratios.  For fixed \(\chi\), expanding (26) gives
\[
 \rho(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\frac{5\chi+1}{363(10\chi-3)}\epsilon^{-2}
 +O_\chi(\epsilon^{-1}).                                  \tag{29}
\]

For the optimization, define
\[
 A(\chi)=10\chi+1+\frac3{10\chi-3},
 \qquad
 D(\chi)=\frac{16}{75}+\frac{16}{15(10\chi-3)},
 \qquad C_0=\frac{25}{3872}.
\]
Equation (26) is equivalent to
\[
 \epsilon^2\rho=C_0\left[
 \epsilon^2A(\chi)+D(\chi)(1+14\epsilon^2)
 +2\epsilon\sqrt{1+14\epsilon^2}\sqrt{A(\chi)D(\chi)}
 \right].                                                  \tag{30}
\]
Set \(\chi=k\epsilon^{-2/3}\).  Locally uniformly for \(k\) in compact subsets of \((0,\infty)\),
\[
 \epsilon^2\rho
 =\frac1{726}+C_0\epsilon^{2/3}
 \left\{2\sqrt{\frac{32k}{15}}+\frac8{75k}\right\}
 +o(\epsilon^{2/3}).                                      \tag{31}
\]
The positive terms in (30) make the rescaled objective coercive as \(k\downarrow0\) or \(k\uparrow\infty\); comparison with a fixed positive \(k\) excludes \(\chi\downarrow1\) for small \(\epsilon\).  Hence every rescaled minimizer lies in one compact subset of \((0,\infty)\).  The derivative of the bracket in (31) is
\[
 \sqrt{\frac{32}{15}}k^{-1/2}-\frac8{75}k^{-2},
\]
which vanishes uniquely at \(k_\star=\sqrt[3]{18}/15\).  At that point
\[
 C_0\left\{2\sqrt{\frac{32k_\star}{15}}+\frac8{75k_\star}\right\}
 =\frac{5\sqrt[3]{12}}{968}.
\]
This proves (9)--(10), including the assertion for every minimizing factor.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The full-Gram center calculation supplies the exact affine residual, bridge-score values, selector ratio, fixed-\(\chi\) coefficient, and optimized elbow inherited by the continuous and integer results. \textit{Gap.} The calculation keeps the CLW affine residual distinct from unrestricted prediction risk away from the symmetric centers. \textit{Consumer.} The exact formulas let a designer audit which proxy signal drives predictive fit and which drives the bridge correction.

\begin{lemma}[lem:binary-ikmw-normalized-stability]\label{lem:binary-ikmw-normalized-stability}
Fix any prescribed \(\chi>1\). Under the IKM score and profile normalization, every binary center law satisfies, for \(d\in\mathcal D\),
\[
 \psi_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac12\phi_{E,d},
 \qquad
 \psi_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac12\phi_{O,d},
 \qquad
 V_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac14V_{E,d},
 \qquad
 V_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac14V_{O,d},
\]
\[
 \mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_\epsilon)
 =\frac14\mathcal V_d^*(P_\epsilon).
\]
Consequently \(D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_\epsilon)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P_\epsilon)=\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_\epsilon)\). For each fixed \(0<\epsilon<10^{-3}\), there is \(r_\epsilon^{\mathrm{IKM}}>0\) such that every law \(P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{bin}}(\chi)\) with \(\|P-P_\epsilon\|_{\mathrm{cell}}<r_\epsilon^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) is regular, both admissible bridge contrasts equal \(\tau\), \(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)=\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\), \(D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\}\), and
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P)
 >\frac12\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P_\epsilon).
\]
On this neighborhood, the IKM-normalized profile is the profiled local causal semiparametric efficiency bound under the fixed-system conditions in Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}ikmw\text{-}fixed\text{-}system\text{-}efficiency}\).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
At a center, complete randomization in source \(E\), symmetry in source \(O\), and \(P(T=1\mid G=g)=1/2\) give \(p_{E,1}=p_{O,1}=1/2\).  Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}ikmw\text{-}normalized\text{-}design\text{-}value}\) therefore yields, design by design,
\[
 \psi_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac12\phi_{E,d},
 \qquad
 \psi_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac12\phi_{O,d},
\]
and hence
\[
 V_{E,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac14V_{E,d},
 \qquad
 V_{O,d}^{\mathrm{IKM}}=\frac14V_{O,d}.
\]
Substitution into the profiled formula gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_\epsilon)
 &=\left\{
 \sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}/4}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}/4}
 \right\}^2\\
 &=\frac14\mathcal V_d^*(P_\epsilon).                    \tag{35}
\end{aligned}
\]
Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) gives \(D^*(P_\epsilon)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\}\), \(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_\epsilon)=\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\), and positive finite profiles.  The common factor in (35) preserves the optimizer and cancels from the best-tie ratio, proving
\[
 D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_\epsilon)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\},
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P_\epsilon)
 =\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_\epsilon).                       \tag{36}
\]

For the neighborhood assertion, start with the radius supplied by Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}witness\text{-}stability}\).  On that relative finite-cell ball, regularity, common identification, and the strict raw-risk selector identity already hold.  Source arm probabilities are finite sums of positive cells and hence continuous.  The bridge coefficients are obtained from nonsingular finite matrices, so they, the two source-score moments, and the IKM-normalized profiles are continuous functions of the cell vector.  Their denominators remain strictly positive after shrinking.  Since the IKM optimizer gap in (36) is strict, there is a smaller radius on which \(D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\}\).  Since the center ratio is positive and finite, continuity permits a further shrinking such that
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P)
 >\frac12\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P_\epsilon).
\]
Taking the intersection of these finitely many relative neighborhoods defines a positive \(r_\epsilon^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) and preserves every inherited conclusion.

Finally, Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}ikmw\text{-}fixed\text{-}system\text{-}efficiency}\) states that, whenever its additional fixed-system regularity, canonical-gradient, sampling-ratio, and bridge-estimation premises hold for the design under evaluation, the IKM source moments form the local causal semiparametric efficiency bound and the profiled cost allocation profiles that bound.  Applying that conditional statement designwise proves the last sentence; no such absolute interpretation is inferred merely from finite-cell continuity.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Balanced treatment multiplies both source moments by one quarter, so profiling multiplies every center design value by one quarter and leaves every named or generic best-tie ratio with the same selected singleton unchanged. \textit{Gap.} The IKM-normalized profile has an absolute causal-efficiency interpretation only under the separately cited fixed-system premises. \textit{Consumer.} The lemma supplies the IKM stability radius used by the named and generic open-region impossibility claims.

\begin{theorem}[thm:finite-catalogue-predictive-guarantee]\label{thm:finite-catalogue-predictive-guarantee}
For every \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\), the exact fixed-catalogue raw-risk-selection identity is
\[
 1\le\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)
 =\frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)}
 (\sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}})^2}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}
 (\sqrt{c_{E,d'}V_{E,d'}}+\sqrt{c_{O,d'}V_{O,d'}})^2}<\infty.
\]
The smallest constant in the actual best-tie inequality is exactly \(\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)\). The least constant that makes every pairwise implication
\(R_d(P)\le R_{d'}(P)\Rightarrow \mathcal V_d^*(P)\le C(P)\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)\)
valid is
\[
 \max_{\substack{d,d'\in\mathcal D:\,R_d(P)\le R_{d'}(P)}}
 \frac{\mathcal V_d^*(P)}{\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)}.
\]
Every displayed constant is finite at a fixed \(P\). Nevertheless, for every prescribed finite \(\chi>1\), the \(\chi\)-indexed binary region satisfies \(\sup_{P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}}\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)=\infty\), even though the uniquely raw-risk-selected design has paired cost \(\chi\) times that of the arm-normalized source-score-optimal design at the center laws. Across all center cost-disadvantage factors, every minimizing sequence satisfies
\[
 \chi_{\mathrm{opt}}(\epsilon)
 \sim\frac{\sqrt[3]{18}}{15}\epsilon^{-2/3},
\]
and the exact least-divergent envelope obeys
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \epsilon^2\inf_{\chi>1}\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 &=\epsilon^2\inf_{\chi>1}\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})\\
 &=\frac1{726}+\frac{5\sqrt[3]{12}}{968}\epsilon^{2/3}
 +o(\epsilon^{2/3}).
\end{aligned}
\]
Thus finiteness of \(\mathcal D\) yields an exact law-specific enumeration, not a primitive uniform raw-risk-to-source-score guarantee; optimizing the witness's cost disadvantage does not remove the leading \(1/(726\epsilon^2)\) divergence.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\).  The family \(\mathcal D\) is finite and nonempty.  Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}nondegenerate\text{-}components}\), positive unit costs, and Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}budget\text{-}profile}\) imply
\[
 0<\mathcal V_d^*(P)
 =\left(\sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}+
 \sqrt{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}}\right)^2<\infty
 \qquad(d\in\mathcal D).                                \tag{38}
\]
