The selector \(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)\) is a nonempty subset of \(\mathcal D\).  Substitution of (38) into its defining best-tie ratio proves the displayed identity.  Since the numerator minimizes over a subset of the denominator's index set, the ratio is at least one; finiteness and strict positivity in (38) make it finite.

Let \(C\) be a constant in the actual best-tie inequality.  That inequality holds exactly when
\[
 \min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)}\mathcal V_d^*(P)
 \le C\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P).
\]
Division by the positive denominator shows that the smallest such \(C\) is exactly \(\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)\).

For the pairwise assertion, put
\[
 C_{\mathrm{pair}}(P)=
 \max_{\substack{d,d'\in\mathcal D:\,
 R_d(P)\le R_{d'}(P)}}
 \frac{\mathcal V_d^*(P)}{\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)}.          \tag{39}
\]
The maximum is over a nonempty finite set, since every diagonal pair is included, and every ratio is finite by (38).  Any constant satisfying every stated implication must dominate each ratio in (39), hence must be at least \(C_{\mathrm{pair}}(P)\).  Conversely, the definition of the maximum makes every implication valid with \(C=C_{\mathrm{pair}}(P)\).  This proves sharpness of the pairwise constant.

Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}predictive\text{-}impossibility}\) and Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) show, for every fixed \(\chi>1\), that each center belongs to its defining ball in \(\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}\), the raw-risk selector uniquely chooses the design whose paired cost is \(\chi\) times that of \(d_{\mathrm e}\), and
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 >\frac{\epsilon^{-2}+14}{726}.
\]
Letting \(\epsilon\downarrow0\) proves the asserted supremum is infinite.  The sharp-elbow optimization also proves that every minimizing factor has the stated cube-root scale and that both the arm-normalized and IKM-normalized optimized envelopes have the displayed second-order expansion.  Therefore finiteness of the catalogue gives exact lawwise enumeration but no uniform primitive guarantee.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The theorem distinguishes exact fixed-law enumeration from a uniform guarantee. Its two-rule companion supplies the treatment-aware and IKM-normalized enumerations. \textit{Gap.} Finiteness of the catalogue guarantees evaluability at a fixed law but no law-uniform approximation factor for either prediction ordering. \textit{Consumer.} It gives downstream readers the exact constants to compute and the condition whose uniform boundedness would be needed on a restricted law class.

\begin{lemma}[lem:treatment-aware-sharp-binary-elbow]\label{lem:treatment-aware-sharp-binary-elbow}
Fix \(\chi>1\) and \(0<\epsilon<10^{-3}\). For a binary center design having early and late proxy signals \(x,y\in(0,1)\), its treatment-aware observational prediction risk is
\[
 \mathcal R_A(x,y)=
 \frac{15(1-x^2)(1-y^2)(16-x^2-y^2-14x^2y^2)}
 {\{16(1+xy)^2-(x+y)^2\}\{16(1-xy)^2-(x-y)^2\}}. \tag{1}
\]
Consequently,
\[
 R_{d_{\mathrm e}}^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=\frac{585}{1024},
 \qquad
 R_{d_{\mathrm p}}^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 \le R_{d_{\mathrm p}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 <\frac{3}{10\chi}<\frac3{10}<\frac{585}{1024}. \tag{2}
\]
Thus
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\{d_{\mathrm p}\},
\]
and, at every center,
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon}),
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{A,\mathrm{IKM}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon}). \tag{3}
\]
Hence the exact ratio, the lower bound \((\epsilon^{-2}+14)/726\), the optimizing scale \(\chi_{\mathrm{opt}}(\epsilon)\sim(\sqrt[3]{18}/15)\epsilon^{-2/3}\), and the sharp second-order expansion in Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) hold unchanged for both treatment-aware ratios.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Fix a binary center design with signals \(x,y\in(0,1)\), and write \(r=W_x\), \(s=W_y\), and \(t=T\).  Under the observational law, direct summation over the Rademacher variable \(U\) gives
\[
 P(T=t,W_x=r,W_y=s\mid G=O)
 =\frac18\left\{1+xyrs+\frac t4(xr+ys)\right\},           \tag{40}
\]
and
\[
 \mathbb E(U\mid T=t,W_x=r,W_y=s,G=O)
 =\frac{t/4+xr+ys+(t/4)xyrs}
 {1+xyrs+(t/4)(xr+ys)}.                                   \tag{41}
\]
Because \(Y=U\), the treatment-aware squared-error risk is the expectation of the conditional variance \(1-\mathbb E(U\mid T,W_x,W_y,G=O)^2\).

To sum the eight cells, set \(q=rs\), \(v=tr\),
\[
 a_q=1+xyq,
 \qquad b_q=x+yq.
\]
For each \((q,v)\in\{-1,1\}^2\), the denominator in (41) is \(a_q+vb_q/4\), while the numerator, up to the irrelevant sign \(r\), is \(b_q+va_q/4\).  The elementary identity
\[
 (a_q+vb_q/4)^2-(b_q+va_q/4)^2
 =\frac{15}{16}(a_q^2-b_q^2)                              \tag{42}
\]
and summation first over \(v\) therefore give
\[
 R_d^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\frac{15}{2}\sum_{q\in\{-1,1\}}
 \frac{a_q(a_q^2-b_q^2)}{16a_q^2-b_q^2}.                 \tag{43}
\]
Now
\[
 a_q^2-b_q^2=(1-x^2)(1-y^2)
\]
for both signs of \(q\).  Placing the two terms in (43) over their common denominator and simplifying yields
\[
 R_d^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\frac{15(1-x^2)(1-y^2)(16-x^2-y^2-14x^2y^2)}
 {\{16(1+xy)^2-(x+y)^2\}\{16(1-xy)^2-(x-y)^2\}},
\]
which is (1).  Every denominator is positive because \(16a_q^2-b_q^2>15a_q^2>0\), where \(|b_q|<a_q\) follows from \(a_q^2-b_q^2=(1-x^2)(1-y^2)>0\).

Substitution of \(x=y=1/2\) gives
\[
 R_{d_{\mathrm e}}^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=\frac{585}{1024}.
\]
For \(d_{\mathrm p}\), conditioning on the additional variable \(A\) cannot increase the optimal \(L_2\) prediction error, so
\[
 R_{d_{\mathrm p}}^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 \le R_{d_{\mathrm p}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon}).
\]
Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) supplies
\[
 R_{d_{\mathrm p}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 <\frac3{10\chi}<\frac3{10}<\frac{585}{1024},
\]
so both unrestricted prediction selectors are the singleton \(\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\).  Since their selected design is identical, their best-tie arm-normalized ratios are identical, and the same is true for their IKM-normalized ratios.  The exact ratio, lower bound, optimizing scale, and second-order expansion now transfer verbatim from the sharp-elbow lemma.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[lem:joint-predictive-binary-stability]\label{lem:joint-predictive-binary-stability}
For each fixed \(\chi>1\) and \(0<\epsilon<10^{-3}\), the radii \(r_\epsilon\) and \(r_\epsilon^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) in Lemmas~\(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}witness\text{-}stability}\) and \(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}ikmw\text{-}normalized\text{-}stability}\) may be jointly decreased, without losing any conclusion of those lemmas, so that every law in the corresponding smaller finite-cell ball also satisfies
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)=\{d_{\mathrm p}\},
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)>\frac12\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P_\epsilon),
\]
and, in the IKM-normalized ball,
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{A,\mathrm{IKM}}(P)
 >\frac12\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{A,\mathrm{IKM}}(P_\epsilon).
\]
Thus the radii used to form \(\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}\) and \(\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) can preserve both strict prediction-risk orderings simultaneously with the arm-normalized and IKM-normalized optimizer orderings.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Fix \((\chi,\epsilon)\).  On the relative interior of \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{bin}}(\chi)\), every observed cell of \((A,X,Z_{M_d},Y)\) has positive probability.  Hence
\[
 R_d^A(P)=
 \mathbb E_P\{\operatorname{Var}_P(Y\mid A,X,Z_{M_d},G=O)\mid G=O\}
\]
is a finite sum of cell probabilities and conditional-moment ratios with positive denominators, and is continuous in the complete latent-cell vector.  Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}treatment\text{-}aware\text{-}sharp\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) gives a strict center gap
\[
 R_{d_{\mathrm p}}^A(P_\epsilon)<R_{d_{\mathrm e}}^A(P_\epsilon).
\]
Thus a relative neighborhood of the center preserves \(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)=\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\).

The arm-normalized and IKM-normalized profiles are continuous and strictly positive on the regular balls already supplied by Lemmas~\(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}witness\text{-}stability}\) and \(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}ikmw\text{-}normalized\text{-}stability}\).  Therefore both treatment-aware best-tie ratios are continuous there.  Since each center ratio is positive, further relative neighborhoods satisfy
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)
 >\frac12\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P_\epsilon),
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{A,\mathrm{IKM}}(P)
 >\frac12\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{A,\mathrm{IKM}}(P_\epsilon),
\]
respectively.  Intersecting these finitely many neighborhoods with the two pre-existing balls, and replacing each radius by the minimum of the corresponding positive radii, loses none of the earlier conclusions and proves all assertions.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[thm:two-rule-predictive-impossibility]\label{thm:two-rule-predictive-impossibility}
Fix \(B>0\), and choose the radii defining \(\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}\) and \(\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) after the joint shrinking in Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}joint\text{-}predictive\text{-}binary\text{-}stability}\). Then every \(P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}\) is regular, CLW-admissible, and common-identifying, and
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)
 =\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)
 =\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)
 =\{d_{\mathrm p}\},
 \qquad D^*(P)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\}.                         \tag{1}
\]
For every fixed \(P\) in this region, \(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)=\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\) for all \(B\ge B_0(P)\), and
\[
 \sup_{P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}}\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)
 =\sup_{P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}}\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)
 =\sup_{P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}}\rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)
 =\infty.                                                   \tag{2}
\]
Every \(P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) additionally satisfies \(D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\}\) and
\[
 \sup_{P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}}
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P;B)
 =\sup_{P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}}
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P)
 =\sup_{P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}}
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{A,\mathrm{IKM}}(P)
 =\infty.                                                   \tag{3}
\]
At every center all six ratios in (2)--(3) agree. For fixed \(\chi>1\), their common value satisfies
\[
 \rho(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\frac{5\chi+1}{363(10\chi-3)}\epsilon^{-2}
 +O_\chi(\epsilon^{-1}),
 \qquad
 \frac{5\chi+1}{363(10\chi-3)}
 =\frac1{726}+\frac5{726(10\chi-3)}>\frac1{726}.          \tag{4}
\]
After optimizing the exact common center ratio over \(\chi>1\), every minimizer satisfies
\[
 \chi\sim\frac{\sqrt[3]{18}}{15}\epsilon^{-2/3},         \tag{5}
\]
and each of the six optimized center envelopes obeys
\[
 \epsilon^2\inf_{\chi>1}\rho(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\frac1{726}+\frac{5\sqrt[3]{12}}{968}\epsilon^{2/3}
 +o(\epsilon^{2/3}).                                       \tag{6}
\]
The named conclusion is only for the CLW population affine-linear rule under its stated transport. The unrestricted-risk conclusions are secondary and no assertion concerns CLW's empirical equation~(3.6), OGA, original RCT theorem, arbitrary predictive procedures, persistent-confounding analysis, or bridge-role choice.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(\chi>1\).  Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}witness\text{-}stability}\) permits the radii in \(\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}\) to preserve regularity, CLW admissibility, common identification, the named CLW selector, raw-risk selector, and arm-normalized optimizer in (1).  Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}joint\text{-}predictive\text{-}binary\text{-}stability}\) decreases the same radii without losing those conclusions and adds the treatment-aware selector identity.  The integer conclusion follows from the threshold established in the binary-stability lemma.

Every center belongs to its defining ball.  Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) proves equality of the named ratios and unadjusted generic ratios, and Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}treatment\text{-}aware\text{-}sharp\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) adds equality of both treatment-aware ratios.  Their common center value exceeds \((\epsilon^{-2}+14)/726\).  Letting \(\epsilon\downarrow0\) proves the three arm-normalized suprema in (2).  On \(\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}\), the IKM and joint stability lemmas preserve the strict IKM optimizer and more than one half of each diverging center ratio, proving (3).

For fixed \(\chi\), the exact common center formula gives (4), and
\[
 \frac{5\chi+1}{363(10\chi-3)}-\frac1{726}
 =\frac5{726(10\chi-3)}>0.                                \tag{44}
\]
The rescaled optimization in the sharp-elbow lemma proves (5)--(6); equality of all six center ratios transfers these conclusions to each envelope.  The construction uses the transported CLW population affine criterion only for the named conclusion.  The two unrestricted risks are distinct secondary objects, and none of the broader excluded procedures enters this proof.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The named affine-linear obstruction and two secondary unrestricted-risk obstructions share one explicit binary mechanism and sharp center envelope. \textit{Gap.} The unrestricted rules are generic secondary comparisons and are not attributed to Carneiro--Lee--Wilhelm or arbitrary predictive procedures. \textit{Consumer.} The result separates failure of the named affine criterion from the broader lesson that outcome fit can miss inverse-bridge stability.

\begin{theorem}[thm:two-rule-finite-catalogue-predictive-guarantee]\label{thm:two-rule-finite-catalogue-predictive-guarantee}
First fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{CLW}}\) and \(B>0\). The named continuous CLW population rule has the exact finite-catalogue ratios
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)
 =\frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)}
 (\sqrt{c_{E,d}V_{E,d}}+\sqrt{c_{O,d}V_{O,d}})^2}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}
 (\sqrt{c_{E,d'}V_{E,d'}}+\sqrt{c_{O,d'}V_{O,d'}})^2}
 \in[1,\infty),                                            \tag{1}
\]
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P;B)
 =\frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)}
 \mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d',\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)}
 \in[1,\infty).                                            \tag{2}
\]
Each ratio is the smallest constant for its actual best-tie selector. For either positive profile \(w_d\), the least constant making every named-rule order implication valid is
\[
 \max_{\substack{d,d'\in\mathcal D:\,
 \Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d}(P;B)
 \le\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d'}(P;B)}}\frac{w_d}{w_{d'}}.       \tag{3}
\]
On the positive-count integer domain, the same formula with \(\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}\) gives the exact integer-selector constant. Computing every criterion, selector, and pairwise maximum takes at most \(O(|\mathcal D|^2)\) arithmetic comparisons after the designwise moments have been supplied.

Separately, for every \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\), the generic unrestricted unadjusted and treatment-aware population squared-risk rules have the exact identities
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)
 =\frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}(P)}\mathcal V_d^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)},
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)
 =\frac{\min_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{pred}}^A(P)}\mathcal V_d^*(P)}
 {\min_{d'\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d'}^*(P)},            \tag{4}
\]
with the analogous two IKM-normalized identities and the corresponding exact pairwise maxima ordered by \(R_d(P)\) or \(R_d^A(P)\). These generic assertions do not require \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{CLW}}\).

The jointly shrunk binary regions have unbounded best-tie ratios for the named CLW rule and, secondarily, for both generic rules under both profiles. At every center all six ratios agree and
\[
 \epsilon^2\inf_{\chi>1}\rho(P_{\chi,\epsilon})
 =\frac1{726}+\frac{5\sqrt[3]{12}}{968}\epsilon^{2/3}
 +o(\epsilon^{2/3}),                                       \tag{5}
\]
where \(\rho\) can be any of the six center ratios. Thus finiteness gives an exact computable lawwise enumeration, not a law-uniform named or generic guarantee.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{CLW}}\) and \(B>0\).  The moment and full-Gram conditions make every \(r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)\), and hence the continuous named criterion, finite.  Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}budget\text{-}profile}\) and Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}nondegenerate\text{-}components}\) imply \(0<\mathcal V_d^*(P)<\infty\).  Source-arm positivity gives \(0<(1-p_{g,1})^2<1\), so the IKM profiles are also finite and positive.  The finite named selector is nonempty.  Substitution of the profile formulas gives (1)--(2).  Each numerator minimizes over a nonempty subset of the denominator's index set, proving that each ratio is finite and at least one.  Division by the positive denominator shows that the smallest constant in each actual best-tie inequality is the displayed ratio.

For either positive profile \(w_d\), set
\[
 C_{\mathrm{ord}}^{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)
 =\max_{\substack{d,d'\in\mathcal D:\,
 \Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d}(P;B)
 \le\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d'}(P;B)}}\frac{w_d}{w_{d'}}.       \tag{45}
\]
Every constant satisfying all named-rule order implications must dominate each indexed ratio; conversely, (45) dominates every such pair.  This proves (3).  On the domain where every compared integer count is positive, the identical finite argument with \(\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}\) proves the exact integer constant.  Computing the criteria and minima takes \(O(|\mathcal D|)\) comparisons, and enumerating every ordered pair takes at most \(O(|\mathcal D|^2)\).

Now fix \(P\in\mathfrak P_{\mathcal D}^{\mathrm{obs}}\).  The unadjusted identity is Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}finite\text{-}catalogue\text{-}predictive\text{-}guarantee}\).  Direct substitution into the definitions proves the treatment-aware identity in (4) and both IKM analogues.  Repeating (45) with the ordering criteria \(R_d(P)\) or \(R_d^A(P)\) proves their exact pairwise maxima without imposing the narrower CLW domain.

Finally, Theorem~\(\mathrm{thm{:}two\text{-}rule\text{-}predictive\text{-}impossibility}\) proves unbounded ratios for the named continuous rule and both generic rules on the jointly shrunk regions.  Lemmas~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) and \(\mathrm{lem{:}treatment\text{-}aware\text{-}sharp\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) prove equality of all six center ratios and the common expansion (5).  Hence the finite enumerations are exact and computable law by law but furnish no law-uniform guarantee.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Finite enumeration gives exact lawwise selector constants for continuous and integer named rules and both secondary risk selectors. \textit{Gap.} Lawwise computability does not imply a law-uniform bridge-value guarantee. \textit{Consumer.} A designer can enumerate every criterion, best-tie ratio, and pairwise order certificate in quadratic catalogue time.

\begin{theorem}[thm:empirical-treatment-aware-predictive-selector]\label{thm:empirical-treatment-aware-predictive-selector}
Fix \(\chi>1\), let \(\epsilon_m\in(0,10^{-3})\) satisfy \(\epsilon_m\downarrow0\), and suppose the independently funded full-catalogue pilot consists of independent and identically distributed draws from \(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m}\), independently of the main study. Apply the split-sample saturated finite-cell rule in Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}treatment\text{-}aware\text{-}pilot\text{-}selector}\). Then
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}
 |\widehat R_{d,m}^A-R_d^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})|=O_P(m^{-1/2}), \tag{1}
\]
\[
 P_{\chi,\epsilon_m}\{\widehat d_m^A=d_{\mathrm p}\}\longrightarrow1. \tag{2}
\]
Moreover, the achieved arm-normalized and IKM-normalized profile ratios diverge in probability:
\[
 \frac{\mathcal V_{\widehat d_m^A}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 {\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_d^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 \longrightarrow_P\infty,
 \qquad
 \frac{\mathcal V_{\widehat d_m^A,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 {\min_{d\in\mathcal D}\mathcal V_{d,\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 \longrightarrow_P\infty. \tag{3}
\]
Thus the treatment-aware population impossibility is attained by a feasible pilot selector using only bounded binary outcomes and saturated finite-cell regression; no inverse-problem learner or growing sieve is used.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let \(m_O\) be the number of observational pilot records.  At every law on the triangular center path, \(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m}(G=O)=1/2\), and hence
\[
 \frac{m_O}{m}=\frac12+O_P(m^{-1/2}),
 \qquad P(m_O<m/4)\longrightarrow0.                       \tag{46}
\]
Conditional on the source labels, the ordered observational records are independent and identically distributed from the observational source law, and the deterministic training and validation halves are independent.  For fixed \(\chi\), all cells of \((A,X,Z_{M_d})\) under both designs have probabilities bounded below by a positive constant uniformly over \(0<\epsilon_m<10^{-3}\).  This follows from the strictly positive treatment kernels, the fixed signal \(y_\chi\in(0,1)\), the two fixed one-half signals, and the fact that the weak signal lies in \((0,10^{-3})\).  The number of designs and cells is fixed, and \(|Y|=1\).

Let
\[
 f_d^A(v)=\mathbb E_{P_{\chi,\epsilon_m}}(Y\mid A,X,Z_{M_d}=v,G=O)
\]
be the oracle finite-cell regression.  Binomial concentration for the training cell counts, followed by Chebyshev's inequality for each bounded cell mean, gives
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}\max_v
 |\widehat f_{d,m}^A(v)-f_d^A(v)|=O_P(m^{-1/2}).            \tag{47}
\]
The zero-denominator convention is harmless because the probability of any empty training cell tends to zero exponentially, uniformly over the finitely many cells.

Conditional on the training half, every validation loss is bounded by \(4\).  The conditional variance bound, (46), and a finite union bound give
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}\left|
 \widehat R_{d,m}^A-
 \mathbb E\{(Y-\widehat f_{d,m}^A(A,X,Z_{M_d}))^2
 \mid\text{training},G=O\}
 \right|=O_P(m^{-1/2}).                                   \tag{48}
\]
The conditional-mean projection identity gives, design by design,
\[
\begin{aligned}
 &\mathbb E\{(Y-\widehat f_{d,m}^A(A,X,Z_{M_d}))^2
 \mid\text{training},G=O\}\\
 &\qquad=R_d^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})
 +\mathbb E\{(\widehat f_{d,m}^A-f_d^A)^2
 \mid\text{training},G=O\}.
\end{aligned}                                               \tag{49}
\]
The last term is \(O_P(m^{-1})\) uniformly in \(d\) by (47).  Equations (48)--(49) prove (1).

The center risk gap is bounded away from zero uniformly in \(m\).  Indeed, Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}treatment\text{-}aware\text{-}sharp\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) gives
\[
 R_{d_{\mathrm e}}^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})
 =\frac{585}{1024},
 \qquad
 R_{d_{\mathrm p}}^A(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})
 <\frac3{10\chi}<\frac3{10}.
\]
Thus the gap exceeds \(585/1024-3/10>0\), and (1) implies
\[
 P_{\chi,\epsilon_m}\{\widehat d_m^A=d_{\mathrm p}\}\longrightarrow1,
\]
regardless of the deterministic rate at which \(\epsilon_m\downarrow0\).

On this selection event, each achieved ratio equals its population center ratio at \(d_{\mathrm p}\).  Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) gives, under both normalizations,
\[
 \frac{\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm p}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 {\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm e}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 =\frac{\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm p},\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 {\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm e},\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 >\frac{\epsilon_m^{-2}+14}{726}\longrightarrow\infty.  \tag{50}
\]
For any finite threshold, (50) eventually exceeds it and the selection event has probability tending to one.  This proves both convergences in (3).  Independence of the pilot and main study is exactly Assumption~\(\mathrm{ass{:}independent\text{-}pilot}\); the construction itself uses no bridge learner or growing sieve.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} A feasible split-sample saturated predictor attains the secondary treatment-aware population choice along the binary sequence and inherits its diverging bridge-value ratio. \textit{Gap.} This remains a secondary saturated finite-cell implementation of generic treatment-aware risk, distinct from the separately proved exact CLW equation~(3.6) affine OLS selector. \textit{Consumer.} It shows that the secondary population obstruction is not an artifact of an infeasible oracle selector.

\begin{lemma}[lem:exact-integer-binary-stability]\label{lem:exact-integer-binary-stability}
Fix \(\chi>1\) and \(0<\epsilon<10^{-3}\).  The radii \(r_\epsilon\) and \(r_\epsilon^{\mathrm{IKM}}\) in Lemmas~\(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}witness\text{-}stability}\) and \(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}ikmw\text{-}normalized\text{-}stability}\) may be jointly decreased, without losing any of their conclusions or those of Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}joint\text{-}predictive\text{-}binary\text{-}stability}\), so that every law \(P\) in the resulting \(r_\epsilon\)-ball satisfies
\[
 2C_{d_{\mathrm p}}r_{d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)
 <C_{d_{\mathrm e}}r_{d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P),   \tag{1}
\]
and hence, for every \(B\ge5\chi=C_{d_{\mathrm p}}\),
\[
 \mathcal D_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)=\{d_{\mathrm p}\},
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)=\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P;B)
 >\frac12\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}(P_\epsilon;B).               \tag{2}
\]
Every law in the resulting \(r_\epsilon^{\mathrm{IKM}}\)-ball also satisfies
\[
 D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\},
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM,int}}(P;B)
 =\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P;B)
 >\frac12\rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}(P_\epsilon;B) \tag{3}
\]
for every \(B\ge5\chi\).  At the center itself, (2)--(3) hold for every \(B\ge5\chi\), and both integer ratios equal their corresponding continuous center ratios.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
At a center, Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) gives
\[
 C_{d_{\mathrm p}}=5\chi,
 \qquad C_{d_{\mathrm e}}=5,
 \qquad
 r_{d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_\epsilon)<\frac3{10\chi},
 \qquad
 r_{d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_\epsilon)=\frac35.
\]
Consequently
\[
 2C_{d_{\mathrm p}}r_{d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_\epsilon)
 <3=C_{d_{\mathrm e}}r_{d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_\epsilon). \tag{51}
\]

Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}witness\text{-}stability}\) supplies a relative finite-cell ball on which regularity, CLW admissibility, common identification, the continuous named ordering, the raw-risk ordering, the arm-normalized optimizer ordering, and their one-half ratio bounds hold.  Its proof expresses
\[
 r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P)=
 \mathbb E_P(Y^2\mid G=O)-g_d(P)^\top\Gamma_d(P)^{-1}g_d(P),
\]
where all moments are continuous functions of the positive cell vector and the full Gram determinants stay nonzero.  Thus both sides of (51) are continuous.  Shrinking the ball preserves the strict margin (51).  Intersecting with the smaller ball allowed by Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}joint\text{-}predictive\text{-}binary\text{-}stability}\) loses none of these properties.

For every \(B\ge C_{d_{\mathrm p}}=5\chi\), set \(z_d=B/C_d\).  Then \(z_d\ge1\), and the elementary floor inequalities \(z/\lfloor z\rfloor<2\) and \(z/\lfloor z\rfloor\ge1\) give
\[
 \frac{B}{\lfloor B/C_{d_{\mathrm p}}\rfloor}<2C_{d_{\mathrm p}},
 \qquad
 \frac{B}{\lfloor B/C_{d_{\mathrm e}}\rfloor}\ge C_{d_{\mathrm e}}. \tag{52}
\]
Multiplying by the two positive residuals and using the preserved margin yields
\[
 B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)
 <B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B).
\]
Because \(\mathcal D=\{d_{\mathrm p},d_{\mathrm e}\}\), the exact integer selector is \(\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\).  The continuous selector is the same singleton throughout the ball, so the two arm-normalized best-tie ratios are identical.  The inherited one-half bound proves (2).

Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}binary\text{-}ikmw\text{-}normalized\text{-}stability}\) supplies a further ball on which \(D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P)=\{d_{\mathrm e}\}\) and the continuous IKM ratio exceeds half its center value.  Intersect it with the preceding ball and the joint-prediction ball.  The integer and continuous named selectors are again both \(\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\), so their IKM ratio numerators coincide and (3) follows.  At the center, the same floor calculation applies directly, proving the last assertion.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} A budget-independent residual margin upgrades eventual floor-count agreement to exact selection for every feasible integer budget on the whole relative finite-cell neighborhood. \textit{Gap.} The earlier stability result supplied only a law-specific eventual threshold and did not define exact-integer bridge-value ratios. \textit{Consumer.} It is the uniform stability certificate used by the exact integer impossibility theorem and the binary-region definitions.

\begin{theorem}[thm:exact-integer-clw-predictive-failure]\label{thm:exact-integer-clw-predictive-failure}
Fix \(\chi>1\).  For every \(0<\epsilon<10^{-3}\) and every \(B\ge5\chi\), the exact integer-budget CLW selector at \(P_{\chi,\epsilon}\) is \(\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\), whereas \(D^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon})=\{d_{\mathrm e}\}\).  The eight center ratios
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}},\quad
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}},\quad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}},\quad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^A,
 \qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM}},\quad
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM,int}},\quad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{IKM}},\quad
 \rho_{\mathrm{pred}}^{A,\mathrm{IKM}}
\]
all equal
\[
 \rho_\chi(\epsilon)=\frac{25}{3872}
 \left\{
 \sqrt{\frac{20\chi(5\chi-1)}{10\chi-3}}
 +\sqrt{\frac{32(5\chi+1)}{75(10\chi-3)}
 (\epsilon^{-2}+14)}
 \right\}^{2}.                                             \tag{1}
\]
This common value exceeds \((\epsilon^{-2}+14)/726\) and, for fixed \(\chi\),
\[
 \rho_\chi(\epsilon)=
 \frac{5\chi+1}{363(10\chi-3)}\epsilon^{-2}
 +O_\chi(\epsilon^{-1}).                                  \tag{2}
\]
For every \(B\ge5\chi\), the suitably shrunk regions satisfy
\[
 \sup_{P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}}
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P;B)=\infty,
 \qquad
 \sup_{P\in\mathcal U_{\mathrm{bin}}^{\mathrm{IKM}}}
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM,int}}(P;B)=\infty.      \tag{3}
\]
The optimized integer envelope is understood with joint budget feasibility:
\[
 \inf_{\substack{\chi>1\\ B\ge5\chi}}
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\inf_{\substack{\chi>1\\ B\ge5\chi}}
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{IKM,int}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\inf_{\chi>1}\rho_\chi(\epsilon),                     \tag{4}
\]
and every minimizing \(\chi\) satisfies
\[
 \chi_{\mathrm{opt}}(\epsilon)
 \sim\frac{\sqrt[3]{18}}{15}\epsilon^{-2/3},
\]
\[
 \epsilon^2\inf_{\chi>1}\rho_\chi(\epsilon)
 =\frac1{726}+\frac{5\sqrt[3]{12}}{968}\epsilon^{2/3}
 +o(\epsilon^{2/3}).                                      \tag{5}
\]
Thus (5) applies along any budget schedule large enough to satisfy \(B_\epsilon\ge5\chi_{\mathrm{opt}}(\epsilon)\); it is not an unrestricted optimization over \(\chi\) at one fixed finite budget.  The named empirical counterpart is treated separately and no result concerns approximate OGA, an arbitrary predictive method, the original randomized-trial theorem, persistent-confounding analysis by Carneiro--Lee--Wilhelm, or bridge-role choice by that paper.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The center residual and cost formulas in Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\), combined with the floor inequalities (52), give
\[
 B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{int}}<3
 \le B\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{int}}
\]
for every \(B\ge5\chi\).  Hence the integer selector is \(\{d_{\mathrm p}\}\).  The same lemma gives \(D^*=D_{\mathrm{IKM}}^*=\{d_{\mathrm e}\}\), equality of the continuous named and unadjusted generic ratios, and formula (1).  Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}treatment\text{-}aware\text{-}sharp\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) adds equality of the treatment-aware ratios.  Because the integer and continuous named selectors are the same singleton, the two integer ratios equal their continuous analogues.  This proves all eight equalities.  Dropping the first nonnegative square-root term in (1) gives the stated lower bound, and expansion at fixed \(\chi\) gives (2).

Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}binary\text{-}region}\) chooses the radii after Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}exact\text{-}integer\text{-}binary\text{-}stability}\).  Hence for each center ball and every \(B\ge5\chi\), the integer ratio equals the continuous named ratio and exceeds half the corresponding center value.  Every center belongs to its ball, while the center lower bound diverges as \(\epsilon\downarrow0\); this proves the first equality in (3).  The IKM region and conclusion (3) of the stability lemma prove the second in the same way.

For every feasible pair \((\chi,B)\), the integer ratio equals \(\rho_\chi(\epsilon)\) and is independent of \(B\).  Conversely, for every \(\chi>1\), the feasible set of budgets is nonempty, for example at \(B=5\chi\).  Therefore taking the infimum over all jointly feasible pairs gives exactly
\[
 \inf_{\substack{\chi>1\\B\ge5\chi}}
 \rho_{\mathrm{CLW}}^{\mathrm{int}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon};B)
 =\inf_{\chi>1}\rho_\chi(\epsilon),
\]
and the identical argument applies to the IKM-normalized ratio.  The rescaled optimization in the sharp-elbow lemma then gives (5) and the cube-root minimizing scale.  The final budget-schedule qualification follows because feasibility of the minimizing center is exactly \(B_\epsilon\ge5\chi_{\mathrm{opt}}(\epsilon)\).
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The all-feasible-budget floor comparison is the exact named population measurement-design headline. \textit{Gap.} Continuous profiling alone does not certify the purchased integer design, and fixed-budget optimization cannot range over infeasible cost factors. \textit{Consumer.} A designer obtains a budget-feasible obstruction and an explicit certificate for when the sharp optimized elbow applies.

\begin{theorem}[thm:empirical-clw-integer-selector]\label{thm:empirical-clw-integer-selector}
Fix \(\chi>1\), let \(\epsilon_m\in(0,10^{-3})\) satisfy \(\epsilon_m\downarrow0\), and suppose the independently funded full-catalogue pilot consists of independent and identically distributed draws from \(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m}\), independently of the main study.  Apply Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}clw\text{-}empirical\text{-}linear\text{-}cost\text{-}selector}\).  Then
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}
 \left|\widehat r_{d,m}^{\mathrm{lin}}
 -r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})\right|
 =O_P(m^{-1/2}).                                           \tag{1}
\]
For every fixed \(B\ge5\chi\),
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}
 \left|\widehat\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d,m}^{\mathrm{int}}(B)
 -\Psi_{\mathrm{CLW},d}^{\mathrm{int}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m};B)\right|
 =O_P(m^{-1/2}),                                           \tag{2}
\]
with the analogous conclusion for the continuous criteria, and
\[
 P_{\chi,\epsilon_m}\!\left\{
 \widehat d_{\mathrm{CLW},m}(B)
 =\widehat d_{\mathrm{CLW},m}^{\mathrm{int}}(B)
 =d_{\mathrm p}\right\}\longrightarrow1.                \tag{3}
\]
Moreover,
\[
 \widehat\rho_{\mathrm{CLW},m}^{\mathrm{int}}
 (P_{\chi,\epsilon_m};B)\longrightarrow_P+\infty,
 \qquad
 \widehat\rho_{\mathrm{CLW},m}^{\mathrm{IKM,int}}
 (P_{\chi,\epsilon_m};B)\longrightarrow_P+\infty.       \tag{4}
\]
No rate relation between \(m\) and \(\epsilon_m\) is required.  The boundary \(B=5\chi\) is included.  No conclusion is asserted for \(B<5\chi\), where the prediction-selected design can have zero exact budget count, or at the singular limit \(\epsilon=0\).  This theorem analyzes the exact finite-catalogue affine least-squares minimizer corresponding to Carneiro--Lee--Wilhelm equation~(3.6), not their approximate OGA procedure.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let \(m_O\) denote the number of observational pilot records.  At every center \(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m}(G=O)=1/2\), so
\[
 \frac{m_O}{m}=\frac12+O_P(m^{-1/2}),
 \qquad P(m_O<m/4)\longrightarrow0.                       \tag{53}
\]
Every coordinate product in \(Y^2\), \(\xi_dY\), or \(\xi_d\xi_d^\top\) is bounded uniformly on the binary family.  Expressing an observational average as the ratio of its \(m^{-1}\)-scaled source-weighted sum to \(m_O/m\), Chebyshev's inequality and (53) give, uniformly over the two designs and finitely many coordinates,
\[
 \max_{d\in\mathcal D}\left(
 |\widehat s_{d,m}-s_d|
 +\|\widehat g_{d,m}-g_d\|
 +\|\widehat\Gamma_{d,m}-\Gamma_d\|_{\mathrm{op}}
 \right)=O_P(m^{-1/2}),                                   \tag{54}
\]
where these empirical moments are proof intermediates obtained by expanding the quadratic in Definition~\(\mathrm{def{:}clw\text{-}empirical\text{-}linear\text{-}cost\text{-}selector}\), and the population moments are evaluated at \(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m}\).

Lemma~\(\mathrm{lem{:}sharp\text{-}uniform\text{-}binary\text{-}elbow}\) gives Gram eigenvalues \(1,1\pm xy\).  For \(d_{\mathrm p}\), \(xy=\epsilon_my_\chi<10^{-3}\), and for \(d_{\mathrm e}\), \(xy=1/4\).  Hence
\[
 \min_{d\in\mathcal D}\lambda_{\min}(\Gamma_d)\ge\frac34. \tag{55}
\]
Equations (54)--(55) imply that, with probability tending to one, every empirical Gram is nonsingular with smallest eigenvalue at least \(3/8\).  On this event,
\[
 \widehat r_{d,m}^{\mathrm{lin}}
 =\widehat s_{d,m}-\widehat g_{d,m}^\top
 \widehat\Gamma_{d,m}^{-1}\widehat g_{d,m},
 \qquad
 r_d^{\mathrm{lin}}=s_d-g_d^\top\Gamma_d^{-1}g_d.          \tag{56}
\]
The inverse identity
\[
 \widehat\Gamma_{d,m}^{-1}-\Gamma_d^{-1}
 =\widehat\Gamma_{d,m}^{-1}
 (\Gamma_d-\widehat\Gamma_{d,m})\Gamma_d^{-1}             \tag{57}
\]
and the uniform inverse bounds from (55) show that the difference in (56) is \(O_P(m^{-1/2})\), uniformly in \(d\).  On the exceptional singular event the minimized residual remains in \([0,1]\), since the zero coefficient is feasible and \(Y^2=1\); the exceptional probability tends to zero, so it does not alter the \(O_P(m^{-1/2})\) conclusion.  This proves (1).

Fix \(B\ge5\chi\).  Both floor counts are positive.  Multiplication of (1) by the fixed constants \(C_d/B\) proves the continuous criterion rate, and division by the fixed positive integers \(\lfloor B/C_d\rfloor\) proves (2).

It remains to show that the population gaps do not collapse as \(\epsilon_m\downarrow0\).  The center formulas give
\[
 C_{d_{\mathrm p}}r_{d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})<\frac32,
 \qquad
 C_{d_{\mathrm e}}r_{d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})=3, \tag{58}
\]
so the continuous population gap is bounded below by \(3/(2B)\).  For the integer criterion, set
\[
 k_{\mathrm p}=\left\lfloor\frac{B}{5\chi}\right\rfloor,
 \qquad
 k_{\mathrm e}=\left\lfloor\frac B5\right\rfloor.
\]
Both integers are positive.  If \(z=B/(5\chi)\), then \(z<2\lfloor z\rfloor=2k_{\mathrm p}\), so
\[
 k_{\mathrm e}\le\chi z<2\chi k_{\mathrm p}.
\]
Consequently the fixed number
\[
 \delta_{\chi,B}
 =\frac{3}{5k_{\mathrm e}}-
 \frac{3}{10\chi k_{\mathrm p}}>0.                        \tag{59}
\]
Since
\[
 r_{d_{\mathrm p}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})
 <\frac3{10\chi},
 \qquad
 r_{d_{\mathrm e}}^{\mathrm{lin}}(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})=\frac35,
\]
the exact integer criterion gap exceeds \(\delta_{\chi,B}\) for every \(m\).  The criterion consistency and the two fixed positive gaps imply
\[
 P_{\chi,\epsilon_m}\!\left\{
 \widehat d_{\mathrm{CLW},m}(B)
 =\widehat d_{\mathrm{CLW},m}^{\mathrm{int}}(B)
 =d_{\mathrm p}\right\}\longrightarrow1.                \tag{60}
\]
At \(B=5\chi\), \(k_{\mathrm p}=1\) and (59) remains strict, so the boundary is included.

On the event in (60), each achieved empirical ratio equals the corresponding population profile ratio at \(d_{\mathrm p}\).  Balanced treatment makes the IKM profile one quarter of the arm-normalized profile design by design, so the ratios agree, and the sharp-elbow lower bound gives
\[
 \frac{\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm p}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 {\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm e}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 =\frac{\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm p},\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 {\mathcal V_{d_{\mathrm e},\mathrm{IKM}}^*(P_{\chi,\epsilon_m})}
 >\frac{\epsilon_m^{-2}+14}{726}\longrightarrow\infty.  \tag{61}
\]
For every finite threshold, (61) eventually exceeds it and the event in (60) has probability tending to one.  This proves (4).  No relation between \(m\) and \(\epsilon_m\) was used.  Each \(\epsilon_m\) is strictly positive, so every law on the path is regular; \(\epsilon=0\) is only a singular limit.  No step uses OGA or a bridge learner.
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} A feasible exact finite-catalogue pilot OLS rule consistently optimizes the affine criterion yet inherits the divergent bridge-value ratio. \textit{Gap.} This analyzes the exact equation-(3.6) minimizer, not approximate OGA or the original randomized-trial theorem. \textit{Consumer.} It shows that population-oracle infeasibility is not the source of the negative result.

\section{Interpretation}

The actionable selection criterion is the pairwise bridge design-value gap \(\Delta_P(d,d')\), computed from the two source-score variances and known costs.  At the binary centers, the late proxy lets the CLW affine regression predict \(Y\) very well, but the weak early proxy makes the observational selection bridge unstable.  The exact integer rule and its empirically consistent OLS implementation therefore optimize the wrong information geometry: estimating the predictive objective accurately does not control bridge-value loss.  Every candidate remains regular and identifies the same effect on the witness region, so the obstruction is efficiency rather than identification.

\section*{Technical internal limitation (diagnostic only)}

The named conclusion is confined to the source-faithful affine-linear CLW specialization, its exact floor-budget population rule, and the exact finite-catalogue OLS minimizer corresponding to equation~(3.6).  It does not analyze approximate OGA, arbitrary predictive algorithms, the original randomized-trial theorem, bridge-role choice, or persistent-confounding analysis by Carneiro--Lee--Wilhelm.  Equality between the affine residual and unrestricted \(R_d(P)\) is proved only at the symmetric centers.  The witness region is relatively open only inside the constrained finite-cell model, and the inverse-bridge envelope is deliberately nonuniform as \(\epsilon\downarrow0\).  At one fixed finite budget the feasible integer centers obey \(\chi\le B/5\); the cube-root \(1/726\) optimization therefore requires joint budget feasibility or a growing budget schedule.  Post-selection inference remains conditional on the declared selected-path learner, multiplier, product-rate, pilot-variance, and main-study variance conditions outside the proved fixed-dimensional subclass.

\section{Honest scope}

For every fixed \(\chi>1\), the paper proves that the named exact integer population selector chooses \(d_{\mathrm p}\) at the centers and throughout suitably shrunk constrained-model neighborhoods for every \(B\ge5\chi\).  Its arm-normalized and IKM-normalized integer-selector ratios equal the corresponding continuous center ratios, have fixed-\(\chi\) leading coefficient \((5\chi+1)/\{363(10\chi-3)\}>1/726\), and remain unbounded on the relatively open regions.  The value \(1/726\) arises after optimization over jointly feasible \((\chi,B)\), with \(\chi_{\mathrm{opt}}(\epsilon)\sim\sqrt[3]{18}\epsilon^{-2/3}/15\) and the stated second-order term; it is not an unrestricted fixed-finite-budget optimization over \(\chi\).  Along the bounded finite-cell path with uniformly nonsingular full Grams, the independently funded pilot OLS criterion is uniformly root-\(m\) consistent and its exact integer selector inherits the divergent ratio without any rate link between \(m\) and \(\epsilon_m\).  Generic unrestricted \(R_d(P)\) and \(R_d^A(P)\) results are secondary and remain distinct from affine linear risk off center.  The proposed positive rule enumerates observable bridge values; Wald inference targets the selected observed bridge contrast on the observable class and the causal long-term effect only on the common-identification subclass.  Absolute IKM efficiency and broader learner attainability retain their separately stated premises.
